# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1986

## Śrīla Prabhupādadeva-māhātmyāṣṭakam

(by Kuśakratha dāsa)

### Text 1

*acchddayantisa-kala-kalesa-*  
*kotyah prakdmarh kali-kala-rdhum*  
*kdlarh kardlam karunena yasya*  
*namami tarn sri-prabhupddadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*acchddayanti—*cover; *isa—*of the Supreme Personality of Godhead; *kald—*of the glories; *kalesa—*of moons; *kotyah—*many millions; *prakdmam—*completely; *kali—*of Kali; *kala—*of the age; *rdhum—*the Rahu planet; *kalam—*dark; *kardlam—*inauspicious; *karunena—*by the mercy; *yasya—*of whom.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Because of his kindness, many millions of moons of the splendid glory of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, are now eclipsing the inauspicious dark Rahu planet of the age of Kali.**

*dalle daridrebhya ihaiva lake*

**Пословный перевод:**

*padmd-isvara—*of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the husband of the goddess of fortune; *sn-pada-padma—*for the lotus feet; *rdga—*of love; *sri-padmaraga—*with rubies; *ddika—*beginning; *sampadam—*the treasure; *yah—*who; *datte—*gives; *daridrebhyah—*to the poor; *iha—*here; *eva—*certainly; *loke—*in this world.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. To the poverty-stricken souls in this world he gave the great treasure of pure love for Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

*kdka-loke kalahamsa-vrndam*  
*namdmi tarn sri-prabhupddadevam*

Note: This poem is written in *upajāti,* the same meter as Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s *Śrī Gurv-aṣṭakam.*

## Meaning of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā by Vīrabāhu dāsa

To understand the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā, first one has to understand the meaning of life. Throughout the history of humankind, different cultures have produced innumerable thinkers with as many "philosophies of life," and, again and again. Time, in time, has devoured them.

Everything in this world seems to have the same fate. The *Bhagavad-gītā,* the formidable Vedic scripture, seemingly confirms this, wherein the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, says, "Time I am, the great destroyer of the worlds. . ."(Bg. 11.32)

In the face of this colossus, Time, does anything stand? Doesn't *everything* partake in the relativities created by Time? What is the use of *my* "philosophy of life" of today, if soon it will be the foodstuff of Time?

Such is life and its meaning for mundane humankind.

Relative.  
Temporary.  
Distressing.  
Perplexing!

It is this kind of perplexity that brought about the great thoughts. And, indeed, this perplexity is wanted. Next to the *Gītā,* if there is a famous and authoritative treatise of thought, it is the *Vedānta-sūtra,* which begins by saying *athāto brahma-jijñāsā,* which in the language of this essay translates as: "Now \[that we can ponder\] is the time to become perplexed." It is this kind of perplexity that brought about the *Bhagavad-gītā.* Representing us, big, mighty Arjuna, the great, invincible warrior, looking at life on his own, became perplexed.

"Now I am confused about my duty and have lost all composure because of miserly weakness . . ." (Bg. 2.7) "I can find no means to drive away this grief which is drying up my senses. I will not be able to dispel it even if I win a prosperous, unrivaled kingdom on earth with sovereignty like the demigods in heaven." (Bg. 2.8)

Taking away the chariot, the bow, and, of course, the arrows, that is a picture of us. *Bewildered* us!

But *Bhagavad-gītā* is not the lament of an impotent mundaner confronting the impossible puzzle of life. Arjuna had the sense we need. It shows in the next two sentences of verse 7 quoted above: "In this condition I am asking You to tell me for certain what is best for me. Now I am Your disciple, and a soul surrendered unto You. Please instruct me." Lucky Arjuna! He was asking Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Time Himself. In reply he was given the *Bhagavad-gītā, Kṛṣṇa's* philosophy of life.

Relative? No. *Absolute.*  
Temporary? No. *Eternal.*  
Distressing? No. *Blissful.*  
Perplexing? No. *Fulfilling* as Kṛṣṇa Himself.

Therefore, the supreme thinker's philosophy did not become a meal of Time. His words partake of another nature, explaining which He explained the ultimate meaning of life.

"Yet there is another, unmanifest nature, which is eternal and is transcendental to this manifested and unmanifested matter. It is supreme and is never annihilated. When all in this world is annihilated, that part remains as it is.

"That which the Vedantists describe as unmanifest and infallible, that which is known as the supreme destination, that place from which, having attained it, one never returns—that is My supreme abode." (Bg. 8.20-21)

"... one must seek that place from which, having gone, one never returns, and there surrender to that Supreme Personality of Godhead from whom everything began and from whom everything has extended since time immemorial.

"That supreme abode of Mine is not illumined by the sun or moon, nor by fire or electricity. Those who reach it never return to this material world." (Bg. 15.4, 6)

Thus, we are not meant to die. Our temporary life is meant to make us eternal—to take us to that place from which "one never returns," and engage us there in our permanent life, filled with varieties of blissful activities, enjoying with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Person, along with our fellow eternals.

To go to Kṛṣṇa: this is the meaning of life. And Kṛṣṇa has given the clue for going to Kṛṣṇa. "Those who are free from false prestige, illusion, and false association, who understand the eternal, who are done with material lust, who are freed from the dualities of happiness and distress, and who, unbewildered, know how to surrender unto the Supreme Person, attain to that eternal kingdom." (Bg. 15.5) Perplexed once again? No need. Kṛṣṇa has given the clue to the clue. "Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized souls can impart knowledge unto you because they have seen the truth." (Bg. 4.34)

That truth was handed by Kṛṣṇa from the beginning of creation, and it has descended through the eras in a chain *of gurus,* or spiritual masters, and their disciples: the disciplic succession. According to *Bhagavad-gītā* and the other authoritative sources of spiritual understanding, like the *Upaniṣads,* for example, the disciplic succession is the simple yet perfect means by which God conveys to the sincere seekers the ultimate purpose of life and the way to achieve it.

"This supreme science was thus received through the chain of disciplic succession" (Bg. 4.2) "In order to learn the transcendental science, one must approach the bona fide spiritual master, in disciplic succession, who is fixed in the Absolute Truth." *(Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* I.2.I2)

Five thousand years ago, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, "the literary incarnation of Kṛṣṇa," compiled all transcendental knowledge and recorded and presented it in the form of books, a task that placed him as the original *guru* for humanity. Thus, a genuine spiritual master has to come in the disciplic succession from Vyāsadeva and is therefore considered a representative of him.

Vyāsa-pūjā (literally, "the worship of Vyāsadeva") is the occasion on which the disciples of a *guru* express their gratitude both to Vyāsadeva, for originally handing down spiritual knowledge, and to their own spiritual master, for embodying and transmitting such knowledge. The disciplic succession is a current flowing in both directions. Just as the transcendental knowledge is being received from Vyāsadeva through the disciplic succession, we can as well relay our thanks to him through the same disciplic succession, manifest in the person of our spiritual master. Therefore, on the appearance day of one's *guru* we celebrate the ceremony of Vyāsa-pūjā, honoring him just as we would have honored Vyāsadeva.

This is neither artificial nor symbolic, most particularly in the case of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the most prominent and powerful representative of Vyāsadeva that our times have seen and who we are honoring in this book. He brought to life the teachings of Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the Western world and attracted thousands of disciples and followers, who have taken those teachings to heart more naturally than their own native cultures.

In the following pages we join once again, as we do every year, to adore and glorify him to the limits of our capacity, which, time and time again, we find totally insufficient and inadequate for the endeavor. Nevertheless, we feel the urge to attempt it, for we want it to be known how great he is, how deeply he transformed us, and how enormous was his contribution to the world. We are still learning about it all, as the glories of the most exalted devotee of the Lord are as unlimited as the Lord's, and in the process of understanding more about him we want to share our ecstasy, so that everyone may have the opportunity to experience it and thus advance in the development of spiritual emotions.

The ever-expanding glories of this outstanding confidential associate of Śrī Kṛṣṇa lead us to foresee that Vyāsa-pūjā will one day have a place in every calendar of the world as the celebration to honor the most merciful and magnanimous soul that walked amongst us. His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. May he bless us all!

Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Introduction by Draviḍa dāsa

As the International Society for Krishna Consciousness spreads more and more with each passing year, as leaders in far-flung corners of the globe build up their preaching projects, capturing new ground in the battle against the forces of Kali, the tendency toward insularity may arise. Foreseeing this with his pellucid transcendental vision, Śrīla Prabhupāda created many institutions to bring us together and break down any barriers that might impede the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Not the least of these institutions is the yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book of homages to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

To read these offerings is to look deep into the heart of ISKCON, for the very life blood of the Society is its leaders' faith in and love for Śrīla Prabhupāda, and this faith and love find eloquent expression on these pages. Outsiders can never understand this collection of encomiums, repeated each year in similar language, often by many of the same devotees, any more than they can understand an ecstatic *kīrtana.* Indeed, the Vyāsa-pūjā book is precisely a worldwide *kīrtana,* a raising of voices in praise of the most exalted saint the world has ever known.

Who but a devotee of Kṛṣṇa can fathom the boundless gratitude a disciple feels toward his spiritual master for the priceless gift of transcendental knowledge? Who but a disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda can appreciate how the soothing balm of shared remembrance of him eases the burning pain of separation? Who but a devotee in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement can begin to understand how the force of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotion for *his* spiritual master, and Prabhupāda's unflinching determination to carry out his order to preach at all costs, is the same force that sustains ISKCON to this very day by empowering his faithful disciples? No, only those who have written, and those who will read, these offerings can enter into the mysteries of these understandings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda told us that *"guru* is one." He often quoted the verse from the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* beginning *tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta,* which explains the qualifications of a bona fide *guru:* "\[He\] has realized the conclusions of the scriptures by deliberation and is able to convince others of these conclusions. Such great personalities, who have taken shelter of the Supreme Godhead, leaving aside all material considerations, should be understood to be bona fide spiritual masters." Since the Lord is one and His message is one, and since the prime qualification *of the guru* is that he has realized that message and is fully absorbed in service to the Lord, all bona fide spiritual masters in disciplic line from Lord Kṛṣṇa are one in this sense: They are all transparent via media for the Lord's message and mercy and can thus deliver their disciples from nescience and reinstate them in their loving relationship of service to the Lord.

The next verse of the *Bhāgavatam* (11.3.22) gives further illumination on the *guru*-disciple relationship and its transcendental fruit: "Accepting the bona fide spiritual master as one's life and soul and worshipable deity, the disciple should learn from him the process of pure devotional service. The Supreme Lord, Hari, the soul of all souls, is inclined to give Himself to His pure devotees. Therefore the disciple should learn from the spiritual master to serve the Lord without duplicity and in such a faithful and favorable way that the Lord, being satisfied, will offer Himself to the faithful disciple."

Here we see a twofold aspect of Śrīla Prabhupāda's glorious position: While he is certainly "one" with all other bona fide *gurus* in that he is bearing the same message and is situated at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa, for his disciples Śrīla Prabhupāda—and Śrīla Prabhupāda alone—is their very "life and soul and worshipable deity." The homages printed here express a blend of those two understandings: glorification of Prabhupāda's universal features as Kṛṣṇa's faithful messenger, along with heartfelt appreciations of his personal love and compassion for all those who came in touch with him, of his intense desire to see each one of us floundering in this ocean of nescience take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness and escape from endless lifetimes of suffering. The genuine spiritual emotions that result from this dual understanding of Prabhupāda—reverence and gratitude, humility and love, awe and solicitude—are churned by expression through these offerings. These emotions are not mere sentimentality, for, as the *Bhāgavatam* states, when they deepen and mature through the disciple's sustained, faithful following of his *guru's* instructions, they eventually attract Kṛṣṇa to give Himself personally to the disciple. Indeed, the sentiments a sincere disciple feels for his *guru* are the very substance of *bhakti.*

For Śrīla Prabhupāda's granddisciples, these pages will also give great inspiration. Prabhupāda once said, "ISKCON is my body." With ISKCON nondifferent from Prabhupāda himself, how can any member of this Society fail to have an intimate relationship with him? The books we read and distribute, the *sādhana* we perform, the fine details of Deity worship—from cooking to *kīrtana—*all are gifts from Śrīla Prabhupāda. Hearing or reading these offerings can only deepen every devotee's faith in that unequaled *jagad-guru,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, and the institution he founded and nurtured with such inconceivable effort. Vyāsa-pūjā is like a great Vedic sacrifice in honor of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and each of the offerings in this transcendental volume is another pouring of oblations into the fire—*Svāhā!—*making the flame of devotion to Prabhupāda burn ever brighter and higher in the hearts of his followers.

In September of 1965, just four days before landing at Boston's Commonwealth Pier, Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote his famous *Prayer to the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa.* Though by Kṛṣṇa's grace the Atlantic crossing had been unusually calm, Śrīla Prabhupāda had suffered two heart attacks early in the journey and, he thought, had come close to passing away. In a mood of great humility, Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote this prayer to the Lord, glorifying his spiritual master, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, begging for the strength to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world, and expressing a fervent hope to once again frolic with Kṛṣṇa in Goloka Vṛndāvana. In that prayer Śrīla Prabhupāda included a verse of Prahlāda Mahārāja's from the *Bhāgavatam,* a verse that perfectly captures the grateful disciple's mood of devotion toward his spiritual master. In these times when the good ship ISKCON is plying through some heavy seas as it crosses the vast ocean of the material world on its way to Vaikuṇṭha, I would like, on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, to paraphrase this verse and appeal to my Godbrothers and Godsisters to enter into its mood with deep conviction: "My dear Lord, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Śrīla Prabhupāda kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve the transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I ever leave his service?" All glories to Jagad-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Draviḍa dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

Kṛṣṇa is one  
You are one

*your servant,*  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Balavanta dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

THERE WAS A TIME when people lived independently, developing their individual lives like gardeners nurturing small trees. But the conditions of Kali spread like storms from an unfriendly climate, and the citizens were forced to combine and organize for protection and nourishment. Thus, institutional nation states, trading companies, and world corporations emerged like gigantic trees, sheltering the masses but shrouding lesser growth. As time progressed, these institutions prospered, spreading their tentacles throughout the world by expanding influence and power. Because an institution does not die with changing seasons or passing lifetimes, they, like ancient and gnarled trees, have endured. Materialistic organizations have thus spawned misery and chaos, conditioning the lives of successive generations and stamping their imprint upon the fabric of human destiny.

Meanwhile, in India, the great bastions of Vedic culture having crumbled in the oblivion of time, the people have passed their days like men cultivating small and short-lived plants. Nonetheless, the creepers of devotional life have somehow been preserved, being passed carefully from generation to generation.

The devotional creeper springs from another world, providing fruit so satisfying that having once relished it the hungry man cannot be placated by any other taste. Indeed, this luscious fruit is intoxicating in its sweetness and bestows unparalleled vigor and bodily strength. It gives a vision of eternity and ultimately elevates its recipients to the highest level of spiritual perfection. Fortunately, while the creeper of devotion ordinarily does not grow in adverse climates, it has been adapted for the present age by the most expert, transcendental gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

With wisdom like time itself, however, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura could see that small trees, while producing delightful fruit, could not easily withstand the rigors of Kali. Thus, as Lord Kṛṣṇa learned to graft trees in the house of Sandipani Muni, the great Ṭhākura attached the creeper of pure devotional service to a gigantic institutional trunk.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Prabhupāda then took the seed from the vision of Bhaktivinoda and planted it firmly in the soil of Indian spirituality. It grew magnificently, and many persons came for shelter and to taste the nectar of its fruits.

Your Divine Grace picked the most nectarean of the ripened fruits of that tree, journeyed with your cargo to our then-barren shores. While eating happily, you planted numerous seeds in the hearts of your followers. Then with great care you took a special seed from the tree, planted it determinedly in the ground of American spiritual aspiration. You watered and carefully tended your ISKCON seedling. The tree grew rapidly. Its roots were strong, but it was young, and the trunk neither stout nor large. You mercifully provided full direction for the nurture and development of this tree in your transcendental books. Your cultivation was perfect, an ideal example. Then, for reasons known only to you, you departed, leaving your precious garden in the hands of fledgling disciples.

As many birds, small animals, and other entities live by the grace of a grand and spreading oak, similarly we, your followers, now owe our livelihood to your devotional tree. As it grows, we will grow; as it prospers, we will prosper; when it is diseased, we will be diseased; and if it dies, we will die. Development of the tree will require removal of weeds and poisons, while supplying the water of service and devotion.

The largest and best of trees develop slowly. A king was once told that a certain tree would take fifty years to fructify. Lesser men were discouraged; the king responded: "Then plant immediately." We may not complete your ISKCON in a day, but we have no greater mission. Indeed, you have created historical times in which to work. While the fruits of ordinary labor rot with the death of the laborer, you have given us a chance to build the future. Your request was "You American boys and girls cooperate together to place this movement in front of all others." My prayer is that we may now begin to do so.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Balavanta dāsa

## Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances. You have often referred to Kṛṣṇa consciousness as a science. If the process is executed correctly, there will be positive and predictable results, and if not, significant complications will arise. These experiences gained during the lifetime of a devotee practically form a *nīti-śāstra,* which guides one in practical devotional life as he endeavors to perfect his service to his spiritual master in this deceiving world of *māyā.* Those practicing devotional service should always remember how the razor's edge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, being held so close to our face, can beautify it or scar it in a moment, depending on our consciousness. As such, the devotees must become most attentive and meticulous in practicing this science, so that one day the world will recognize how you were the topmost servant amongst all servants of God in bringing humanity from the lowest possible experiences to the highest form of liberation. So let us always make our priorities, desires, and understandings identical with yours, so that we can reciprocate with you and one day be qualified to be known as one of your real disciples.

Cooperation and sharing of experience must exist before there can be genuine spiritual competition. Then everyone will bring out the best abilities in each other. Otherwise politics, secrecy, separatism, and loss of relationships will win.

Patiently maintaining our projects before expanding into new ones cultivates the mode of goodness. Otherwise, passion in the name of expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness will cause us to neglect the obvious, and in a frenzy of overendeavor, sufficient funds will not go into the book production, which, like a heart not receiving sufficient blood, will painfully stop our overall preaching effort.

First we should find great pleasure in reading, printing, and distributing your books, along with doing our own writing. In this way we will be inspired by you how to increase set distribution more and more, and everyone in the world will feel a complete relationship with you. Otherwise we will become preoccupied with publishing our own realizations and selfishly deprive the world of your intimate association.

When everyone takes personal responsibility for distributing your books, then Kṛṣṇa will give us the opportunity to enter into the real political arena and influence the statesmen of every country. Otherwise we will become irresponsible and fall into internal politics, seeing each other, instead of the atheists, as the enemy.

Only when there is systematic training for all our *āśramas* will our movement become stable and peaceful, as these things do not come automatically. Otherwise, we will create a masquerade party of *brahmacārīs, gṛhasthas,* and *sannyāsīs.*

When the G.B.C. members themselves are accountable to the G.B.C. body, then an equitable system of Kṛṣṇa conscious management will naturally follow on all other levels. Otherwise, local temple leaders will develop the maveric psychology of "absolute below and relative above," where they consider their authorities to be only opinion givers, while their own opinion becomes unquestionable.

First there should be obedience to the orders of our higher authorities before there can be love and trust. Then we can have genuine and lasting relationships. Otherwise we will become a movement of sentimental cliches.

First we should see to it that our own devotees are placed properly in their *āśrama* and their service. This is essential in order to prepare ISKCON to interface with the outside world. Otherwise we will miss precious preaching opportunities, and although we have qualified devotees, we will fail to penetrate society.

By arranging for the majority of our devotees to preach directly as Vaiṣṇavas, while presenting your books and ISKCON on their own merit, we will create the most sublime public relations. Otherwise the people we are approaching will feel used and will be forced by the modes of nature to be overly dependent on mundane techniques, courts, and "hype" to uplift our public image.

Accountability to higher authority and collective decision-making are more important than absolute individualism, which only turns one into a pretentious pedagogue. In the name of decentralization, one puts himself in the center of everything and causes unilateral chaos.

Fulfillment of the order of the spiritual master must be there before deep internal ecstasy can be spoken of. Otherwise one will become resentful towards the bliss of successful preachers and impersonally think that everyone's realizations and opinions are equal. Then out of immaturity one may fall into *sahajiyā-'ism,* mistaking mundane feelings for spiritual.

If we actually care about ISKCON, and do not want to exploit it, we will want to see a G.B.C. which actually governs and to which all members are accountable. This is far more of an emergency than anything else. First we must be satisfied understanding whose servant of the servant we are; otherwise the heart will develop irresistible independence that will lead to impersonalism, where service to the whole will be neglected out of preoccupation with oneself.

Before considering oneself to be your disciple, everyone must have a servant of your servant whom he is the servant of. In this way, all devotees in ISKCON will, in a higher sense, be Godbrothers. Otherwise we will create an exclusive caste of Prabhupāda disciples who actually want independence, and who, in the name of Your Divine Grace, become lawless.

The magnitude of the special projects such as the Māyāpur City is meant to make us totally dependent on you. In this way ISKCON will always remain a transcendental dynasty, with you as the founding father. If we think our local projects are more important, we may actually go into competition with you and lose the unity of ISKCON, and our spiritual lives as well.

As much as possible, direct and open preaching must become a daily function in the life of a devotee. Then the "taste we have always been hankering for" will be our daily *prasādam.* Otherwise, the process of engaging others in hearing and chanting will become too infrequent, and mundane engagements will appear more practical and satisfying than *saṅkīrtana.*

First we should be convinced that your books can be distributed on their own merit before introducing other paraphernalia. Otherwise, instead of serving book distribution, paraphernalia will usurp the throne of Vyāsa, and books will appear as an impractical burden.

Establishing book quotas instead of *lakṣmī* quotas will make us develop the real paraphernalia—compassion towards the fallen souls. Otherwise, if we hide our identity, people will appear as commodities, and our preachers will become aloof, not really caring whether people read the books or not, or whether they ever see them again.

Training and proper placement of our devotees must come of age before there can be respect amongst Vaiṣṇavas. Otherwise position will become more important than function and practical results, and one will dry up due to a lack of appreciation and reciprocation.

When purity is deeply established on all levels of leadership, then we can think of the big ISKCON and of significantly changing the world. Otherwise we will become worldly instead of becoming the world.

By establishing quality distribution as the basis of *saṅkīrtana* we will certainly become enlightened as to how to keep on increasing the mass distribution of books. Otherwise, out of exhaustion so many distributors will become shooting stars and retired incarnations, and scams will replace real potency, bringing out the tendency to become dishonest.

We must develop meekness and freedom from offense and faultfinding before raising our hand to become *guru.* Otherwise, one will not be protected by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and the acid of others' *karma* will dissolve one's integrity.

After some time, the preachers should be separated from the leechers before attempting to further expand our activities. Then those who are worthy will receive association. Otherwise good energy will feed malignant tumors in the dress of devotees, and one will lose the vital strength to grow.

If we take the time to try to understand each other and the issues before us before we take things to a vote, then our decisions will naturally tend towards consensus and unanimity Otherwise voting will never bring satisfaction, as either a large minority will remain dissatisfied or a small minority will frustrate the majority.

If we first of all correctly understand your point of view on philosophy, management, and preaching before we attempt to be creative, then we will certainly be able to successfully fill in the great skyscraper framework which you left behind. Otherwise we will only display artificial intelligence and become "foolish active," thus risking what you have already given us.

One day, by your blessings, I may understand a fraction of what it takes to serve you properly. Service to you is no doubt the greatest challenge in life, as your satisfaction alone can free us forever from death. Perhaps in some lifetime I may have the good fortune to be with you and serve you once again.

*Your stumbling servant,*  
Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

## Bhaktitīrtha Swami

**Tomorrow I Must Meet Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Today is the last day of my life. Tomorrow I must meet Śrīla Prabhupāda. What will I say to him? What will he say to me?

I will say to Śrīla Prabhupāda, "I am Ghanaśyāma dāsa, the one who distributed your sets of books to the academic communities."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but have you given up sex desire for me?"

I will say, "I have never broken any of the regulative principles, and I have faithfully chanted over sixteen rounds every single day since I joined the movement—never once missing."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but have you given up sex desire?"

I will say, "I have maintained a program of fasting all day on every Ekādaśī normally from liquids and all foods for most of my life as a devotee."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but have you given up sex desire for me?"

I will say, "I have also maintained a program of eating mainly once a day for most of my life as a devotee."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but have you given up sex desire for me?"

I will say, "I have for most of my devotional life maintained sleeping less than six hours each day."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but have you given up sex desire for me?"

I will say, "Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have faithfully read all of your books, and I am trying now to distribute them in many countries where there is neither paper nor printing presses available."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, my son, but have you given up sex desire?"

I will say, "I persevered as your G.B.C. member responsible for the largest number of countries in the world, i.e. fifty, which happen to be the poorest in the world."

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Very nice, but how about the sex desire?"

I will speak about my cultivating a few heads of state and a few diplomats.

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "Are you now free of sex desire?"

I will give Śrīla Prabhupāda a report on the temple construction and farm projects in a few different countries.

Śrīla Prabhupāda will say, "This is very good, but how about the sex desire?"

I will give a report on the number of temples I have opened and the number of devotees and new disciples I have made.

Then I will notice Śrīla Prabhupāda's voice becoming even more penetrating as he chastises me (just as he did the very last time I was with him before he left the planet): "This is all by Kṛṣṇa's mercy, and not ours." It is today that this chastisement has full meaning for me. You were telling me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then and even more so now, that I must give up this sex desire, subtle and gross.

We must first all realize that any achievements we have accomplished internally, externally, individually or collectively, are simply by your mercy. Secondly, we are to understand that as long as we still have such devastating sex desire, all of our offerings to you are tinged, having various degrees of contamination. The disciple can never repay the spiritual master for what he has given. Also, we understand that one must be careful not to think that the *guru* needs him or that he is doing something wonderful. Whatever we offer, it is so incomplete if we have not given up sex desire. You, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, preached to your last breath, completely selfless, for the benefit of your disciples and the world. Now we must also give everything we have to you. As today I die, and tomorrow I have to meet you, it is today—right now—I must give you the greatest offering a disciple can give his *guru,* while I still have a little more time. I must give up all remaining attachment to sex life, subtle and gross. I can understand that I have nothing really to give you, but if I can become totally free of all sex desire, then I can really become your puppet, so you can do with me as you like. Now I am so much of an imperfect instrument.

In these days our Society is having many problems and varieties of understandings. These things are very small in contrast to our real problem, which is sex attachment. This attachment is obfuscating our vision tremendously and making big problems seem small and small problems appear big. While we are debating, the demons are getting more and more positioned to attack our movement and the global community in general. Most of us will not become really serious until major catastropheshit us directly in the face. But how can I talk when I now hear you saying to me with deep compassion but with sadness, "You cannot come with me. Still you are possessing sex desire"? As you walk away, my pain is unbearable. As I reflect on the pain I have and am giving you by my imperfect state, my own pain increases that much more. I scream, crying out in anguish, "Please forgive me, please help me, please don't leave me!" Then I remember I still have a few hours to surrender everything to you, as today I die and tomorrow I must meet you.

I am the insignificant beggar who is quickly running out of time. But by your mercy I have this last chance to make the greatest offering—to offer you myself in an uncontaminated state, free of all sex desire, so as to be a perfect instrument for your mission.

Bhakti Tirtha Swami

## Bhavānanda Goswami

Dazzling, effulgent master,  
To serve you, the purpose of my life.

Without that source present,  
My consciousness torn bitter by strife.

Sweet and gentle master,  
Whose smile encourages and cradles,

I beg you do not kick me back  
Among the world, where all is fatal.

Powerful, unyielding master,  
Destroying all confusion,

Give me shelter in the knowledge  
That this world is all illusion.

Compassionate and kind master,  
Helping all who turn to you,

My final plea, be kind to me;  
From life's miseries, please rescue.

Bhavānanda Goswami

## Girirāja Swami

**Eternal**

When I first met His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, His Divine Grace instructed me to read his books and to write. In one letter he wrote, "Amidst our heavy duties, we must write something to glorify the Supreme Lord."

As we know from the Vedic literatures, to glorify the devotee is as effective as directly glorifying the Lord. Therefore in Bombay Śrīla Prabhupāda commented, "Kṛṣṇa is already glorified, so He is more pleased when His devotee is glorified."

Over the past weeks I have taken the opportunity to write some words to glorify His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. I have written for my own purification, but on this occasion, I would like to share some of this writing with others.

**Where Are You?**

Prabhupāda,  
Where are you?

Are you defeating  
the māyāvādīs,  
or saving  
the hippies?

Are you walking  
in your garden,  
smelling a rose,  
or walking on the beach,  
poking your cane  
in the sandy arguments  
of the material scientists?

Are you taking massage,  
or prasādam?

I wish I was with you.

But I am not  
qualified.

The next item may be a bit fanatical:

The One

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lost were we  
until the day  
You came to us  
to show the way

From māyā’s night  
to Kṛṣṇa's light.

Many may come  
and many may go  
but none to me  
will ever show  
What you have done—  
not even one.

I am not happy  
to state this fact  
but reality exists—  
the truth is exact.

You are the one  
Lord Caitanya sent,  
you are the one  
Bhaktivinoda meant

When he said  
the day will come  
when Bengalis and  
Britons all will be one

In chanting the name  
of Śrī Hari  
and changing the course  
of history.

We surrender ourselves  
to your lotus feet  
with all the souls  
that we may meet

On planet earth  
for ten thousand years:  
all are yours;  
remove their fears.

Thus we declare  
without pride or shame  
Who is that person,  
What is his name:

BHAKTIVEDANTA SWAMI,  
Śrīla A.C.,

Inscriber of the books,  
deliverer of the world,  
a house made for all,  
a banner unfurled:

PRABHUPADA and ISKCON,  
together and one forever.

In the next item, I give my idea of how the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading so wonderfully to every town and country of the world.

**Reciprocation**

All you have to do  
is to do  
something  
for him,  
for Prabhupāda,

and He,  
Kṛṣṇa,  
will do everything  
for you

because he  
has no other desire  
than to glorify  
Him.

Actually, I had the realization that Kṛṣṇa is spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world just to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are doing so little, actually, but by Kṛṣṇa's grace that little effort is producing big results. Why? My realization is that only because Kṛṣṇa wants to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda, His pure devotee, is He making everything so wonderful and so successful. Kṛṣṇa is even making us wonderful, *mlecchas* and *yavanas,* just to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda. And why is Kṛṣṇa desiring only to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda? Because Śrīla Prabhupāda has no desire other than to glorify Kṛṣṇa. So reciprocally, at least in terms of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in this material world (earth), Śrī Kṛṣṇa has no desire other than to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda. And why? Because Śrīla Prabhupāda has no desire other than to glorify Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The qualities of Śrīla Prabhupāda are an ocean. I have tasted only a drop of that ocean, but I hope that others more fortunate may drink from that ocean and share its nectarean contents with others.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Swami

Dear Godbrothers. Godsisters, disciples, and friends,

WE ARE ASSEMBLED HERE to celebrate the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony of the appearance day of our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder*-ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. Vyāsa-pūjā is the day on which we glorify the bona fide spiritual master appearing in disciplic succession from Śrīla Vyāsadeva. The disciplic succession is an unbroken chain through which Vedic knowledge is passed down from the spiritual master to the disciple. Lord Kṛṣṇa first imparted the Vedic knowledge to Brahma within his heart. Brahma then spoke it to Nārada, and Nārada explained this spiritual science to Vyāsadeva. Vyāsadeva is known as the literary incarnation because he compiled the *Vedas* and put them into written language. The word *pūjā* means "worship." So today we are worshiping Śrīla Prabhupāda, who appeared in the unbroken chain of disciplic succession from Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The bona fide spiritual master is well versed in the Vedic conclusion and spiritually situated on the path of unalloyed devotional service. This Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony is not something whimsical or a recent invention. This celebration is an ancient tradition rooted in the Vedic heritage of this country. The disciples, on this occasion, present their offering of love to the spiritual master, expressing their gratitude for his having lifted them out of the material world. On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our beloved spiritual master, is being worshiped in his temples around the world in his *vigraha* form by offerings of flowers held in folded palms—*puṣpāñjali.* Disciples, grand-disciples, and friends will present words of appreciation to give some expression of their love for Śrīla Prabhupāda and what Śrīla Prabhupāda meant to each one of us. Śrīla Prabhupāda's instructions were perfect, and he was a pure example. Just as one benefits by hearing the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, similarly by hearing the nectarean pastimes of exalted pure devotees like Śrīla Prabhupāda, everybody will benefit.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was born during the Janmāṣṭamī celebrations in Calcutta in 1896. Śrīla Prabhupāda was born of pure Vaiṣṇava parents. Śrīla Prabhupāda's father raised his son to be a pure Kṛṣṇa conscious devotee. Śrīla Prabhupāda was also well educated, and therefore in his very first meeting with his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, he was asked to preach Lord Caitanya's message throughout the whole world. Caitanya Mahāprabhu's philosophy is universal and meant for the entire humanity—not just for the people of one particular country. Mahāprabhu appeared to claim all the conditioned souls back to Godhead. He predicted that in every town and village of the world He would be known. This transcendental knowledge had been known in India for thousands of years, but Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī wanted that this knowledge should now be broadcast to the whole world. Śrīla Prabhupāda's life was full of hardships and struggles. A devotee of the Lord takes pleasure in facing hardships and difficulties. Queen Kuntī in her famous prayers asked that her calamities may increase, as these difficulties forced her to depend further on the lotus feet of the Lord. In 1938 Śrīla Prabhupāda started writing Vaiṣṇava literature in English. He was writing and publishing his articles in the Gaudiya Math publication called *The Harmonist* because Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta stressed book printing and book distribution, as these books could be read all over the world. And when Śrīla Prabhupāda was in the householder *āśrama,* he was simultaneously trying to write and publish. In 1939 he first wrote an essay called "Introduction to Gita Panishad," in which he summarized the philosophy of the *Bhagavad-gītā.* Although there were many commentaries in English, in reality very few of them could really convey the mood of the speaker. Lord Kṛṣṇa. *Bhagavad-gītā* is the perfection of all religions, the true jewel of education, and the king of all knowledge. In the early fourties, Śrīla Prabhupāda started a monthly magazine called *Back to Godhead,* which is still being published by our Society. Śrīla Prabhupāda coined the following motto, which was carried on the cover: "Godhead is light. Nescience is darkness. Where there is Godhead there is no nescience." This motto conveyed what Śrīla Prabhupāda was trying to advocate. A society must stay God conscious, and in the absence of God consciousness one is in darkness and illusion. When a human being forgets that he is the son of the Supreme Lord and identifies with the body and senses, then he is considered to be a madman or in ignorance. The defeat of the present-day civilization is that the human society is identifying with the body and the senses. Nobody appears to be worried about the spiritual nature of the soul. Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda has said that just as one cannot separate the body from the soul, similarly a disciple cannot separate his life from the instructions of the spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda's mission in life was to fulfill the request of his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura had requested Śrīla Prabhupāda to take this message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the Western world. Śrīla Prabhupāda was determined to carry out the orders of the spiritual master through the medium of writing, publishing his books, and preaching. Determination is one of the essential ingredients for advancement in spiritual life. With great difficulty Śrīla Prabhupāda had translated the *Bhagavad-gītā.* As Śrīla Prabhupāda at that time did not have any secretaries or disciples, he personally handled twelve hundred pages of translations and purports on the *Bhagavad-gītā.* He was struggling to find somebody who could finance the publication of this great book. Prabhupāda approached various people for financial assistance, and in the end he found somebody who was ready to help publish the book. But to his surprise the manuscript which he had translated and personally handwritten with great care was stolen. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda did not become discouraged, and he started translating the whole *Gītā* all over again. Śrīla Prabhupāda was given the transcendental power of attorney by his spiritual master to explain the philosophy of Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda had to struggle to raise finances to carry out his publishing work. He would look for every opportunity to carry out the instructions of his spiritual master. He was regularly corresponding with leaders of society, encouraging them to discharge their spiritual responsibilities towards their citizens. Prabhupāda did not waste even a minute. Even while traveling late in the night, if the compartment was not crowded he would turn on a light while others slept, and work on his books. Śrīla Prabhupāda established a League of Devotees in Jhansi in 1952. Śrīla Prabhupāda was always preaching. He was convinced that Caitanya Mahāprabhu's philosophy would be accepted by society. He was *dṛḍha-vrata—*staunchly determined. Without income or institutional shelter, Śrīla Prabhupāda began staying in various homes in Delhi, living from one week to another in different places. Śrīla Prabhupāda was going through very difficult times. Śrīla Prabhupāda was homeless. He was moving from one location to another, seeking donors and help in order to realize the instructions of his spiritual master. To devotees, struggling for Kṛṣṇa is a great burden of love, just like the burden a mother feels in bringing up a child. Śrīla Prabhupāda had firm faith in Kṛṣṇa. He always had full faith that if he served Kṛṣṇa, then all necessities would be provided. Śrīla Prabhupāda exhibited complete humility. Once, while approaching a well-to-do house, the owner came out and told Śrīla Prabhupāda to go away. Śrīla Prabhupāda was publishing his spiritual paper in tabloid form. He would have bold, thought-provoking headlines on the topics of the day. Some of the headlines which he published were "The Lower Self to Mankind," "Philosophical Problems Within Social Awareness," "Sufferings in Humanity," and "The Pure Consciousness of Nationalism." Śrīla Prabhupāda played the role of the writer, publisher, fund-raiser, and salesman. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda was not an ordinary newspaper salesman, but as Kṛṣṇa's representative he was sending the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. As Śrīla Prabhupāda did not have any disciples, he was personally distributing the *Back to Godhead* magazine, which he was publishing with great difficulty. One day when he was distributing the *Back to Godhead* magazine in 112-degree heat in Delhi, he suffered a severe heatstroke and became unconscious. But as soon as he was brought back to consciousness, he resumed his transcendental distribution.

With great difficulty Śrīla Prabhupāda managed to obtain a free passage on a regular cargo carrier of the Scindia Steam Navigation Company and voyaged to New York in September of 1965. He experienced sea-sickness, dizziness, and vomiting, but that did not discourage him. On the way to America Prabhupāda had a dream—Lord Kṛṣṇa in His many forms was rowing a boat, and He told Prabhupāda that he should not fear but should go along. Śrīla Prabhupāda suffered two heart attacks on his journey to America. However, he tolerated the difficulties, meditating on the purpose of his mission. On the way, on the second night, Prabhupāda had a dream in which Lord Kṛṣṇa assured him that his mission would be successful, and he should not fear. Prabhupāda journeyed to America to teach the people that happiness is found by following the laws of God. As in Delhi, he had no permanent shelter. He moved from one location to another, till eventually he attracted some followers and started the first Kṛṣṇa conscious temple in New York. Even before Śrīla Prabhupāda had opened the first temple, he would talk to his friends, saying that there are temples and books which are all existing, but there is only time that is separating us from them. Śrīla Prabhupāda would say that he would soon have temples filled with devotees. Śrīla Prabhupāda was confident that his mission would be successful. He did not compromise himself. He stuck to the principles of purity. On one occasion he was requested by the commercially minded *swamis* of New York to compromise and not insist on the ancient principles of purity that he was advocating. Śrīla Prabhupāda, however, refused to comply with the wishes of these bogus *swamis.* The first temple in New York was a storefront. Gradually Prabhupāda opened the second temple, and then the third, and so on. Śrīla Prabhupāda introduced the ancient Jagannātha car festival to the West. Śrīla Prabhupāda came to the West to fulfill the desires and the vision of his spiritual master and Śrīla Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda. Śrīla Prabhupāda also introduced the ancient Vedic system of Deity worship. Deity worship enables one to become purified. The first Rathayātrā festival outside India was held in San Francisco. Later on, this festival was carried to major cities of the world, and today there is an annual Rathayātrā festival in almost every major city where ISKCON has a temple. Śrīla Prabhupāda pushed on in spite of experiencing poor health. Śrīla Prabhupāda explained that the purpose of Kṛṣṇa consciousness was to become free of material anxiety, which everybody is filled with. Śrīla Prabhupāda had many ideas of festivals. Becoming free from all anxiety and returning to the spiritual world was the purpose of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda was humble and tolerant, and he was enthusiastic and patient to push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He wanted his disciples to also develop these qualities. In a brief span of fifteen years, Śrīla Prabhupāda opened hundreds of temples and converted thousands of people to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda was able to inspire the greatest of materialists to give up their sinful activities in favor of pure spiritual life. He was able to inspire thousands of impersonalists to become personalists. It was Śrīla Prabhupāda who created the desire to serve Kṛṣṇa in the heart of his devotees. Just as the monsoon revives the land by making it fresh and watered, similarly chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* takes away the dirty habits and revives one's original beauty. Śrīla Prabhupāda was able to attract the youth, whom he described as the flowers of the countries. Śrīla Prabhupāda made plans to fulfill Caitanya Mahāprabhu's prediction that Kṛṣṇa consciousness would spread in every town and village of the world, and he instructed his followers to go and open centers in every town and village of the world. While Prabhupāda was on this planet, hundreds of Kṛṣṇa conscious centers were opened, and from these permanent centers hundreds of devotees were traveling in mobile vans to different cities, preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In addition to opening centers, traveling around to each center to preach, Śrīla Prabhupāda traveled around the world nearly fourteen times. Prabhupāda wrote almost eighty books, big and small. His books were received very well by scholars all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda convinced the whole world that Caitanya Mahāprabhu's teachings were the highest truth. Prabhupāda wanted his students to explain the philosophy of the Absolute Truth, which would enable visitors to comprehend the actual spiritual truth beyond sectarian religious feelings. Śrīla Prabhupāda demonstrated that the world could be spiritualized by linking material things with the Supreme Personality of Godhead through the medium of *bhakti-yoga.* From 1965 to 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda concentrated on establishing Kṛṣṇa conscious centers in America. His feeling was that if America, the most powerful nation of the world, became Kṛṣṇa conscious, then the rest of the world would follow.

In 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda returned to India. He struggled for years to purchase Hare Kṛṣṇa Land in Bombay. He personally developed and directed the construction of ISKCON's major projects in Vṛndāvana, Māyāpur, and Bombay. In addition to writing and publishing his books, Śrīla Prabhupāda inspired his disciples to also distribute these books. As Śrīla Prabhupāda said in his introduction to the *Bhāgavatam,* in thousands of years literally there have been thousands of books written, but these books are failing to bring about peace on earth. Peace on earth can only be realized when the spiritual vacuum is filled. This vacuum can be filled by these books. There would have been no value to Śrīla Prabhupāda's writing these books if these books were not distributed. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda inspired his disciples to think of ways by which the conditioned souls, who are used to crowlike literature, may be attracted to this swanlike literature. Book distribution is devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, and it produces an ecstasy that is spiritual and completely distinct from any form of material happiness. These books are so powerful that if anybody reads them, he can be saved from hellish life. This vision of Śrīla Prabhupāda's books gave him great ecstasy. Prabhupāda's vision was to replace mundane literature with transcendental. He desired that at least in every home there should be one piece of Kṛṣṇa conscious literature. Prabhupāda once said that if a person read even one page of any of his books, his life could become perfect. To make the world Kṛṣṇa conscious, Prabhupāda wanted his books to be distributed. Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted his books to be distributed widely. These books and magazines which Prabhupāda published were to destroy the illusion of society The whole world is engaged on a suicidal path. We have to save it. Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote over eighty books. He completed an entire *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* manuscript, which was later on published in seventeen volumes, during a brief span of eighteen months in 1973-74. This was no small feat. Indeed, it was a remarkable feat, because in addition to writing these books, he was also engaged in overseeing the affairs of the Society and constantly traveling. Śrīla Prabhupāda's spiritual master had instructed him, "If you have some money, print books." Therefore, Prabhupāda was always pushing this point. Śrīla Prabhupāda inspired his disciples to preach. He was always eager to hear about successful preaching. When Śrīla Prabhupāda was physically present, whatever successful breakthroughs in preaching were achieved in one place were at once communicated to him, and he would then broadcast this transcendental news to others. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us the following motto, which summarizes his mood: "Preaching is the essence, purity is the force, books are the basis, and utility is the principle." Śrīla Prabhupāda's instructions can unite the present Vaiṣṇavas and the rest of the society. Loving Prabhupāda means to manifest our love for living by his instructions.

In Vedic literatures we read about demons being killed by incarnations of the Lord or by devotees. But Śrīla Prabhupāda actually liberated thousands of people who had demoniac mentalities. Friends, our spiritual master was not an ordinary person. He was the absolute *ācārya* for the whole age of Kali, and for thousands and thousands of years he will remain an example to be strictly followed by anyone in the role of a bona fide spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda traveled to Ravana's kingdom in the Western world at the age of seventy and began a massive assault on impersonalism and godlessness. Just as Lord Siva, the greatest of all Vaiṣṇavas, shrunk the ocean of poison to an amount which could fit in the palm of his hand and then drank the deadly mixture, Śrīla Prabhupāda mercifully shrunk the poisonous ocean of Kali and held back the devastating tidal waves of passion and ignorance, which would otherwise have destroyed all the good qualities of mankind. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us one religion, Kṛṣṇa consciousness; one scripture, *Bhagavad-gītā;* and one *mantra.* Hare Kṛṣṇa. Just as the opulence of Lord Kṛṣṇa cannot be understood by the conditioned souls, so also Śrīla Prabhupāda's greatness cannot be easily appreciated. Śrīla Prabhupāda stood out from the ordinary group of *swamis* and *sādhus* as a full moon stands out amongst the stars. Śrīla Prabhupāda was the perfect *ācārya* and spiritual master of the whole world. Śrīla Prabhupāda was unsurpassed in his service to Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrīla Prabhupāda was the greatest Vaiṣṇava preacher and a staunch soldier in the *saṅkīrtana* army of Lord Caitanya. Śrīla Prabhupāda's determination to preach and transcend all material impediments can be compared to the mood of Grandfather Bhīṣmadeva, who executed perfect meditation on Lord Kṛṣṇa even while pierced with so many arrows. Similarly, even in an advanced age and failing health, Śrīla Prabhupāda went on preaching until his final breath. Even when doctors would come and tell Śrīla Prabhupāda to take rest, he would not stop but went on preaching.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you often said that our love for you will be shown by how we cooperate together. You said that ISKCON is your body and book distribution is your heart. Therefore, our real worship of you is not just offering of flowers and ringing the bell, but to cooperate together and expand book distribution. No one could have possibly accomplished what Śrīla Prabhupāda did in such a brief period, and no one will be able to accomplish such a feat in the future. From the moment that Śrīla Prabhupāda appeared on this planet up until his physical departure in 1977, he demonstrated nectarean pastimes, which will be relished by the Vaiṣṇavas eternally. And Śrīla Prabhupāda's transcendental pastimes have not stopped; rather, they are increasing daily through the expansion of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. As the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement expands, this Vyāsa-pūjā day of Śrīla Prabhupāda's is going to become one of the most important celebrations in the world's calendar. A day will come when Śrīla Prabhupāda's appearance-day celebration will eclipse the birthday celebrations of personalities such as Christ. Therefore on this most auspicious day of all days, I bow down at the lotus feet of my eternal spiritual master and pray that he will always allow me to surrender fully at his divine lotus feet, so that I may always be engaged in pure devotional service to the Personality of Godhead.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I was blinded by the darkness of ignorance, but my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him."**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet."**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"My respectful obeisances are unto you, O servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

JUST as worship of the Ganges cannot be properly performed without first obtaining *gaṅgā-jala,* so glorification of the spiritual master cannot be successful without first obtaining his mercy. That is why, falling down at the lotus feet of my eternal lord and master, Om Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I humbly offer him my most respectful obeisances and pray that he will kindly fulfill my desire to glorify him.

Such homage as is uttered all over the world on this Vyāsa-pūjā day is no inconsidered hyperbole. It is the heartfelt statement of those who could not fully express their indebtedness to Śrīla Prabhupāda even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahma, for Śrīla Prabhupāda appeared to deliver us from the fearsome claws of material existence.

Forgetting our constitutional position as eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa, we have languished in one material body after another. Sometimes we are raised to the higher planetary systems or to material prosperity and foolishly consider ourselves well-situated. At other times we are plunged into hellish situations and consider ourselves unfortunate. Our state is exactly like that of a criminal who is punished by being repeatedly submerged in the water and raised from the water. In either case, we are miserable, waging our futile struggle against material nature. Insane with the desire to enjoy separately from Kṛṣṇa, we are bound to *māyā’s* dunking stool by her three *guṇas,* or ropes. Believing ourselves to be independent enjoyers, we are imprisoned in perpetuity in the bodily conception of life.

*sādhu-śāstra-kṛpāya yadi kṛṣṇonmukha haya*  
*sei jīva nistare, māyā tāhāre chāḍaya*

**"If, however, the conditioned soul becomes Kṛṣṇa conscious by the mercy of saintly persons who voluntarily preach scriptural injunctions and help him to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, the conditioned soul is liberated from the clutches of** ***māyā,*** **who gives him up."**

Eternal master, you are that peerless saintly person whose mercy is bestowed upon us in the form of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness (ISKCON). ISKCON means:

—*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* unpolluted by personal ambition and mental concoction, the essence of all Vedic knowledge, the direct words of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead;

—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the mature fruit of the desire tree of the Vedic literatures further sweetened by your Bhaktivedanta purports, which distinguish reality from illusion, are sufficient in themselves for God realization, and are destined to provoke a revolution in the impious lives of a misguided civilization;

—*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* the divine pastimes of Lord Caitanya, the golden *avatāra,* and His eternal associates, who continuously plunder the transcendental storehouse of love of God and profusely distribute its contents without discrimination;

—the *bṛhat mṛdaṅga,* transcendental book publication and distribution, which is the top priority and supreme welfare work for all mankind;

—the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* the holy names of the Lord, transcendentally blissful, the best of all benedictions, the form of all transcendental *rasas,* untouched by the material energy, no less powerful than the Lord Himself, and the fundamental religious system in this age of Kali;

—connection through your lotus feet to a bona fide disciplic succession originating with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and passing from Brahmāji, Nārada Muni, and Vedavyāsa to Lord Caitanya Himself, Nityānanda Prabhu, the six Gosvāmīs, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja;

—saintly association of great souls with whom to serve Kṛṣṇa, chant and dance in *kīrtana,* discuss *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* honor *prasādam,* preach cooperatively, and push on your *saṅkīrtana* movement;

—admittance to the holy *dhāmas,* especially Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana-dhāma, where the divine pastimes of the Supreme Lord and His associates are revealed on this planet;

—the worship of the *arcā-vigraha* in beautiful temples, so that all impersonal and voidist conceptions of the Supreme shall forever be banished from the minds of men;

—ecstatic festivals and holy days like the yearly Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana festival, Rathayātrā, Janmāṣṭamī, and Ekādaśī, to mention only a few;

—*gurukulas* and *varṇāśrama* colleges, which will educate humanity how to finally become human;

—farm communities where simple living and high thinking are the basis of self-sufficiency, and where millions of conditioned souls will receive engagement, nourishment, and guidance from devotees;

—*varṇāśrama-dharma* and the ways and means to introduce it in society;

—the Māyāpur project to tightly bind your far-flung *yātrās* and zones in a common monumental undertaking for the glorification of Lord Caitanya;

—wonderful *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* which immunizes us from the onslaught of *māyā* (just as an antiseptic vaccine protects a person from the attack of an epidemic) and nourishes our Kṛṣṇa consciousness;

—countless lectures in which you patiently explain the fundamental axiom that we are not this body but spirit souls, eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa, the supreme enjoyer, proprietor, and friend;

—swift chastisement for all our concoctions which obstruct pure devotional service;

—intimate loving moments which can never be forgotten or taken away;

—yourself, the perfect example of an empowered preacher, pure devotee, and ever well-wisher who never for a second forgot Kṛṣṇa in life, as in death.

These and countless other matchless gifts are embodied in the wonderful ISKCON you bequeathed to us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Attachment to your ISKCON is not measured in mere formal membership or religious sentiment but in the purity with which we dedicate ourselves to your devotional service. Indeed, this purity is the very emblem of all those who would profess membership in and sentiment for the ISKCON you struggled so hard to create and which we must faithfully serve and protect until our last breath.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you emphatically declared that ISKCON is your body. Just as a pure devotee who engages in the service of the Supreme Lord and who constantly thinks of Him should never be considered to have a material body, so we should never think of your ISKCON as something material. It is a grave offense to think of the spiritual master as an ordinary human being with a body consisting of material ingredients *(guruṣu nara-matiḥ. . . nārakī saḥ).* Let us comprehend once and for all that the ISKCON you left us is your selfsame body, the very one we always longed to touch and serve. According to ordinary vision, imperfections in the body of the pure devotee may seem prominent, but despite such seeming defects, the body of a pure devotee cannot be polluted. "\[Even though\] dirty foam or garbage \[may sometimes be\] seen floating on the water of the Ganges, . . . one who goes to the Ganges does not mind the foam and dirty things floating in the water. With his hand, he pushes away such nasty things, bathes in the Ganges and gains the beneficial results." Therefore, let us tenderly care for your ISKCON body by pushing away all nasty, impure things and by exhibiting the purified behavior worthy of Vaiṣṇava sons and daughters of Your Divine Grace.

Dear lord and master, please bless us with the indomitable conviction that only the kind of purity embodied by you, and not some material formula, will give us the force to "do as \[you were\] doing" even in your physical absence. Only you are able to lead ISKCON now, as then, and always.

"O my Lord, as the Ganges forever flows to the sea without hindrances, let \[our\] attraction be constantly drawn unto you without being diverted to anyone else." May this attraction be forever felt by *all* members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. May it be felt by untold generations of Vaiṣṇavas to come. If we truly love you, let us die to live, and living ensure that with the passage of time your ISKCON never becomes a moribund religious institution but that it remains a radiant and transcendental beacon for all those who have lost their way back home, back to Godhead in the darkness of Kali-yuga.

As the Founder*-Ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, you have forever distinguished yourself as the most munificent among all Vaiṣṇava personalities, for it is you alone who have built ISKCON, the only house in which we can all live together with you.

*Thus prays the lowly sweeper in your house named*  
Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Harikeśa Swami

**My Testimonial**

It is very hard to live without the association of Śrīla Prabhupāda. He made life easy. He answered our questions, solved our problems, directed our works, and encouraged us according to our capacity. He kept peace and inspired cooperation. He organized preaching, book distribution, book production. Deity worship, school preaching, as well as instructed us in general management principles. He stimulated the opening of new temples and the creation of farm projects. He engaged us in the construction of magnificent temples in India, while expanding our knowledge of the holy *dhāmas.* He kept us always ready and eager to enthusiastically endeavor in acts of purification. It is not possible to completely list all his glorious achievements.

There is no end to the wonderful legacy left by Śrīla Prabhupāda. Historical personalities will come and go, but the gifts of Śrīla Prabhupāda will long remain. Even today we cannot properly calculate all that he has done for us. Prabhupāda wrote that the historians will look back on this era as the time when the entire course of Kali-yuga was turned. Later, when this actually begins to manifest, we will look back on those words and wonder, "What a vision! How clear and dynamic! Again Prabhupāda was seeing far ahead of all others."

Who else ever even dared to bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western world? Who else ventured forth to cross the ocean and actually establish a new spiritual order? Who else defied the bounds of old age and real and potential disease to inject the spark of Kṛṣṇa consciousness deep within the hearts of such lowborn and foolish persons as we? And above all this, who else actually succeeded in accomplishing this powerful spiritual rescue mission?

**My Presentation to You, my Eternal Master**

Can I always remember what you have given me? Certainly, but the exact details of how these principles were given are getting weaker and weaker day by day. I know that the essence of what you wanted is there in your books, lectures, morning walks, and letters, but I also clearly remember that which you considered most important, that which you drilled into my mind with great determination throughout the many years of personal instruction. Thus I feel safe that even my dulled memory cannot cheat me; there are too many cross-checks to keep everything in line.

No one person can ever equal you. Certainly no one alive today can even approach you in greatness. It is not difficult to get carried away with praising your singular position. Even Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura predicted your activities when he proclaimed that a powerful preacher will appear in the future to spread the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu in every town and village. The feeling that I carefully maintain in my heart is that you are the greatest representative of the Lord. I am simply proud to have such a master as you.

Even were a group of extremely great personalities to come together, it would be their perfection to reflect even a portion of your greatness. This realization is not a cause of cynicism, since all your dedicated servants take pleasure in standing in the shadow of your overpowering presence. It is a delight to think that I am helpless without you, for this thought demands that I surrender to you and your mission utterly and totally, without reservation.

My mind is useless. It helps me in only one way: It makes my life so miserable that there is nothing left for me to do but surrender unto your lotus feet. This might be a benediction in disguise. I cannot accept anything within this world as being a proper substitute for pure loving devotional service to your lotus feet.

Help me to serve you. I will supply all the energy I can, whatever intelligence is possible, and as much desire as I have. Please give me the shelter of your lotus feet so I may always be sufficiently protected to accomplish those things you wish me to do. I am ever remaining as a soul surrendered unto you, eagerly awaiting whatever instruction you wish to give me. I have no other desire than that.

I have nothing more to offer than my life and all that goes with it; please accept this.

*Your humble servant, eternally prostrated at your lotus feet,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

On this most auspicious day of Śrīla Prabhupāda's divine appearance in this world, those who remain faithfully engaged in his service may truly aspire to understand his glory. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sings, "How can my earthly words describe / that feeling soft and broad?" As we reminisce on moments spent with Śrīla Prabhupāda or hear bona fide stories of his activities, we relish the spiritual continuity of our lives, through him, and measure our place on the path back home, back to Godhead, by contemplating, honestly, how much we love him, how much we've done exactly what he asked of us.

The future is the road to Prabhupāda, who stands at Kṛṣṇa's side. Thus, we gather within our heart all that he told us to do and, firmly bound and lead by those very orders, push on our mission, push on our journey. How fortunate are those who, firm on the path, are not seduced by mundane careers, *"varṇāśrama"* sex, or a shameless chopping envy of Prabhupāda's own ISKCON body. Now, on this most blessed day, newly inspired, let us pour abundantly, and all around, the cleansing waters of singular devotion to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let the crystal streams of obedient love dash away the dust and dirt of discord in the holy community of his unflinching servants. Scorning the putrid couch of sense gratification, let us joyfully dance in the flowery meadows of *saṅkīrtana,* and gathering strength in the enchantment of that dance, let us pin the earth, as a well-earned medal, on the divine chest of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*The servant of his servant,*  
Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

PLEASE accept my *dandavats* at your lotus feet. All glories to your wonderful service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your compassion, in the form of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, has made the whole universe auspicious.

You know everything about me, yet somehow you have tried to take my help in spreading your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. But what can I do, helplessly flapping in the winds of the modes of nature? If you want me to do anything, please ask Lord Kṛṣṇa to make me dance as you like.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jaya Patāka Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my respectful obeisances! All glories to your lotus feet! I humbly beg to write you this letter on the occasion of the ninety-first Vyāsa-pūjā anniversary of Your Divine Grace's transcendental appearance as a humble offering and to submit to Your Divine Grace my overdue report on recent developments in the holy *dhāma.*

You have blessed this universe with your divine association from 1896 until 1986 in your *vapuḥ* and *vāṇī* forms. May we, your loyal followers, as unworthy as we may be, be empowered to expand the transcendental influence of your spiritual presence for the next ten thousand years and more—in order to deliver the fallen souls of this world by your mercy.

The Fifth Centennial celebration of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu's appearance held in India was a great success simply due to Your Divine Grace's causeless mercy. The prime minister and president of India both presided over functions commemorating Lord Caitanya's Quincentennial, and in respect to Your Divine Grace, ISKCON representatives were chief guests at the functions. Nationwide television broadcasts portrayed Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's life in the most positive way, and public interest has soared to new heights. The six-thousand-kilometer Śrī Caitanya Pādayātrā brought Your Divine Grace into Śrī Māyāpur for a heart-rending finale to this great *yajña* started in Dvārakā on Rādhāṣṭamī, eighteen months before. The most significant achievement of all is the way your personal plan to popularize Śrī Māyāpur has been realized—Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma is in the limelight all over the subcontinent of South Asia. Everyone is conscious of Śrī Māyāpur, due to your transcendental mercy!

Devotees from all over the world attended the Śrī Māyāpur festival this year, fulfilling your instruction to attend the annual Śrī Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana pilgrimage festival. The more than six million pilgrims who visited the Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir were deeply inspired by seeing the United Nations of the spiritual world which Your Divine Grace has created! A beautiful permanent gallery of Lord Caitanya's pastimes has been established in Śrī Māyāpur, in order to further your instruction to make "holy places of the Lord's transcendental pastimes." A permanent exposition grounds to present the activities and philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has also been added to your Śrī Māyāpur project's medicine bag for curing the disease of *māyā’s* illusion for those who visit the "kingdom of God" at Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma.

These developments in the holy abode are all by Your Divine Grace's causeless mercy—this we can clearly understand. Had we only been quicker to take up Your Divine Grace's instructions, today our level of progress would be unlimitedly more. Your Divine Grace instructed us to build another "long guesthouse" when the first one was finished. Now a new guesthouse is almost completed, and we can now realize that the demand for additional guest facilities is much greater than we had ever realized. In fact, to accommodate the visiting devotees this year, we had to build bunk beds in the rooms to avoid overcrowding. If only we had built the next building immediately, when you advised us to do so!
