Your Divine Grace informed us that in the Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya main temple—the Temple of Vedic Planetarium—lifesize Pañca-tattva, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and the eight Gopīs, and the Guru-paramparā would be the principal worshipable deities. Already the lifesize Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and eight Gopīs have appeared of their own will, pushing us to provide the largest temple in the world for Their Lordships and the Pañca-tattva! I can hear Your Divine Grace telling us to proceed faster and faster in completing your transcendental plans, and instructing devotees individually how much you are depending on them to fulfill *your dream!* By Your Divine Grace's insurmountable determination and inconceivable enthusiasm to preach Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *vāṇī* all over the world, you are inspiring the devotees with transcendental energy to carry out your plan! Seeing your transcendental hand at work, my eagerness grows to see your master plan manifest itself and flood the world in ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa!

Seeing how Your Divine Grace is doing everything out of your intense desire to carry out the instructions of the previous *ācāryas,* I can better see how insignificant I am. I am able to do practically nothing, yet by your mercy everything is manifesting, one thing after another. I pray that I may be fortunate enough to continue serving Your Divine Grace even life after life (if that is your desire) in the association of your pure devotees, who are free from all desire for material gain and liberation. I feel ashamed that I have not been a productive servant, as I would like to be. I have fulfilled but a fraction of the instructions Your Divine Grace has given to me. I only venture to keep my life in the hope that in the future I may succeed in carrying out all of your instructions to me. I am simply crying at your lotus feet for your causeless mercy. I have no other hope or shelter in this world.

**The Nectar of Śrīla Prabhupāda's Instructions**

PART ONE: Obstacles Śrīla Prabhupāda Warned About in *Upadeśāmṛta*  
Beg today, spend by tomorrow,  
Eat according to your service.  
Atyāhāra (overeating and hoarding) causes us sorrow.  
Remain on guard against excess.  
Do as I do! Follow me home—  
To Godhead, the spiritual sky!  
Prayāsa work is ākāśa-kusuma (mundane overendeavoring)—  
Valueless "flowers in the sky."  
Glorify Kṛṣṇa—praise His name!  
Vibrate the transcendental sound.  
Prajalpa (idle talk, rumors), the witch, steals our fame  
When we bark around like a hound.

Know the śāstra (Vedic scriptures), follow the rules.  
Whimsical acts kill devotion.  
Preach to make pure, rules are just tools—  
Aids to achieve pure devotion.  
Friendship, society, and love,  
Barren and dry without Kṛṣṇa!  
"Māyā friends" (association with the materially-minded) deflect our pure love,  
Covering our real love to Kṛṣṇa!  
Desire to gain—profit is good  
If in Kṛṣṇa's service it's spent.  
Other profit is putrid food.  
A feast for crows, not for gents.

Your insignificant servant,  
Jaya Patāka Swami

## Mukunda Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances.

From the perspective of a peon trying to serve your instructions day after day, the range and depth of your divine person seems to expand without limit. Like the spiritual world with its endless array of variegated splendors and rasas, you are an infinite reservoir of great and transcendentally mysterious qualities.

This perception of you confirms the maxim that the spiritual master is great and the disciple small; that God is omnipotent and living beings insignificant. Your overpowering humility compels us to harken to Caitanya Mahāprabhu's clarion call—to always carry oneself as the servant of all, master of none; to become like the lowly straw in the street and the ever-tolerant tree; and to offer respect to all, desiring none for ourselves.

You have done the impossible—started a worldwide movement that is transforming beastly humans into godly saints, infused it with a spiritual potency that propels it forward even in your physical absence, and established principles in your books and in your personal instructions that continue to guide your worldwide mission all over the world.

Although you have given us many facilities, positions, and responsibilities to carry out your orders, we are never more than your instruments, existing only for the purpose of implementing your grand design to respiritualize the world.

You are a paragon for a human race. You are the goal for which we can tirelessly toil to the end of life. You sustain us through all adversity. You reconcile the irreconcilable. You make the impossible possible. You make the beautiful hideous and the hideous beautiful. You make the difficult easy. And you transform curses into blessings.

Miraculously, mercifully, you have extended your personal self directly into our hearts and given us the opportunity to personally serve you, and thus our hearts become filled with transcendental love and happiness in your association. Indeed, you have given us the greatest gift imaginable—yourself.

May we continue in your service life after life in our determined march toward spiritual perfection.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Mukunda Goswami

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are the shelter of innumerable universes.

You descended into this world from the eternal abode of Kṛṣṇa and came to enrich us with the Lord's own devotional service, which cannot be had for any amount of gold but is only given by the intimate associates of Lord Caitanya, such as yourself, who come to this planet to redeem the conditioned souls. You were born of Gour Mohan De, a most venerable Vaiṣṇava who always prayed that you would become a servant of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. And your mother, Rajani, protected you much as Mother Yaśodā worried about the well-being of her son, Kṛṣṇa. You could hear the bells ringing softly as your father performed *pūjā,* daily worshiping the Deities of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. And as Mahārāja Parīkṣit from his most early childhood became attracted to the worship of the Deities, so you also were always meditating on the Lord's form. Accompanied by your childhood friend, Siddhesvar, your mother would take you to the Rādhā-Govinda temple. You would cross Harrison Road and enter into the temple, where you would gaze at the effulgent Deities in a mood of intense attraction. Who can understand your pure heart, O Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, who has always known Kṛṣṇa, and who has descended to glorify Him and to spread His glories unlimitedly to others?

From your eternal father, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you received the manifest blessings of all the *ācāryas,* and he, the son of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and himself a "ray of Viṣṇu," bestowed his blessings upon you and prepared you to become the natural heir to his visionary preaching mission, which is nondifferent from the mercy of Lord Gaurāṅga. And you, as the most loyal and pure son of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, have brought the mercy of the Gauḍīya movement to every town and hamlet throughout the world, having literally canvassed personally from door to door, so that every man, woman, and child might become a devotee of Kṛṣṇa. You are so magnanimous, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so effulgent with love of Godhead, so merciful, that no living entity, neither moving nor unmoving, is beyond your vision, and all become enriched with love of Godhead due to your all-encompassing wisdom.

We continue carrying out the work that you have shown us how to perform, and mostly we cannot perceive your absence from this world, in that you have taught us everything we need to continue completely absorbed in devotional service, and your mercy is always upon us. At times such as this, although simultaneously I can feel your separation, still I can intensely feel your presence, that you are near me, and that the entire planet will never be bereft of your loving association, your unending compassion, and the manifestation of your deep love in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, celebrated everywhere throughout the three worlds as the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, ISK.CON, which is the embodiment of the disciplic succession and the compassion of the *ācāryas,* those who are the direct descendants of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

As fish are caught by a fisherman in a net, as *māyā* binds the conditioned soul, so I have become caught by you, along with so many others who are your eternal servitors. I have become a recipient of your mercy, and in this way my life has become meaningful and effulgent. As a child, I used to marvel that I was alive, that I was notably distinct from the lifeless material objects around me. How unthinkable it is that life could have developed from matter! And even more so, now I am astonished that not only am I alive but that I have known you, that I have received your mercy, that I know that I am an eternal servitor of the Lord, that Lord Caitanya has become my only refuge, that I am connected with Kṛṣṇa once again by vibrating His holy names.

Were things to be measured by years, by days, hours, and minutes, then my association with you would have seemed too brief, too fragmentary, a short sojourn in eternal time. But your association is eternal, and now we are becoming stronger, more determined to serve Kṛṣṇa, and that is because you are always with us. And your soft features, the beautiful form of Your Lordship that I knew, is very near, just beyond the clouds of my shrouded consciousness. But you are always present, and I am even now breathing and serving because of your blessings.

My divine and eternal master, I write this insignificant offering to you, praying that you may be unlimitedly glorified on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day. I would pray that we, your disciples, and those future generations who follow in your footsteps may continue your movement as you wanted us to, and that still others may be blessed by your compassion through our endeavor, which is nothing more than your blessings.

My eternal father, as I strive to please you and to serve you, I pray to become pure, to be worthy of your trust in me. I pray to represent you, as we all represent you, by acting with the purity which you have always displayed in all your actions. If I can develop this constant determination, then I will know that I am able to please you.

I have no other shelter than you.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Pañcadraviḍa Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me the strength  
To remain Kṛṣṇa conscious and go to any length  
To distribute this knowledge in the form of your books  
To every town, every country, every village, every nook  
Where conditioned souls hide, far away from the light  
Of Lord Kṛṣṇa's mercy, in māyā’s dark night.  
As we travel, we see many cultures and types  
Of people who claim they know what is right.  
There are Muslims and Christians, Hindus and Buddhists,  
Conservatives and Socialists, Communists and Capitalists.  
You've taught us that they're just attached to their sects,  
They're entangled like fish caught up in a net  
Of bodily consciousness, so they can't see  
How to follow Kṛṣṇa's words, "Just surrender to Me."

So you've shown us how to transcend this confusion  
And cross over the ocean of māyā’s illusion,  
How to feed the bird, not just polish the cage  
By chanting harināma, the medicine for this age.  
If we follow this process, we can always reside  
In the spiritual world by Lord Kṛṣṇa's side.

It's not enough to simply chant; we must go out and fight  
Against the wrongs of Kali and establish Kṛṣṇa's right.  
We must help the fallen souls to get out of the ocean  
Of material attachment, go to Godhead, back to home.

So you've started an army—ISKCON is its name.  
It's known over all the world, and to you belongs the fame.  
You 've enlisted us as soldiers in the war against māyā.  
Some of us may fall away, and some may rise still higher  
To lead your forces onward, overrun the enemy Kali  
And establish you as emperor for all to plainly see.  
So, Prabhupāda, please help me, I'm just a lowly soul.  
I've got no qualification to be your man at all.  
It's only by your mercy that I could get the chance  
To join the ISKCON movement and dance the saṅkīrtana dance.  
So save me from disaster and kindly let me stay  
Underneath your lotus feet forever in this way.

*Your servant,*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

YOU HAVE WRITTEN in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:* "O Lord of the universe, soul of the universe, O personality of the form of the universe, please, therefore, sever my tie of affection for my kinsmen, the Pāṇḍavas and the Vṛṣṇis." In the purport you have written, "A pure devotee cuts off the limited ties of affection for his family and widens his activities of devotional service for all forgotten souls. To cut off all family affection means to broaden the field of activities. Without doing this, no one can be qualified as a *brāhmaṇa,* a king, a public leader, or a devotee of the Lord. Such personalities as a *brāhmaṇa,* a devotee, a king, or a public leader must be very broadminded in discharging their respective duties. Śrīmatī Kuntīdevī was conscious of this fact, and being weak she prayed to be free from such bondage of family affection. The Lord is addressed as the Lord of the universe, or the Lord of the universal mind, indicating His all-powerful ability to cut the hard knot of family affection. Therefore, it is sometimes experienced that the Lord, out of His special affinity towards a weak devotee, breaks the family affection by force of circumstances arranged by His all-powerful energy. By doing so He causes the devotee to become completely dependent on Him and thus clears the path for his going back to Godhead."

To be completely dependent on the Lord, one must become completely dependent on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and live one's life *only* for executing your mission within this world. In my present condition I am trying to correct my many faults and bad qualities. In your physical absence, I am trying to remember your instructions which emphasized your mission to write, publish, and distribute transcendental literatures throughout the whole world, and to utilize whatever money we may have for this work. I am trying to take shelter of your G.B.C. Body, as you ordered all your followers to do in your last will. Wherever I am placed, and whatever I am assigned, I know it will be meant to be utilized only to expand your BBT. I pray that I may offer you a total lifetime of complete service to your books, and in that way, when I finally quit this body, I may be left with the epitaph: "He lived and died for the BBT." I pray I may offer you my whole life in this way.

*Your unworthy and disappointing servant,*  
Rāmeśvara Swami

## Rūpānuga dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances. With each appearance day you appear fresher and fresher in my heart. When you left your physical presence *(vapuḥ),* in time I realized with a new certainty how you remain just as fully present in the form of your teachings, or *vāṇī.* In one sense, your *vapuḥ* is still present in the form of your deities and pictures in your temples and books. Even before your disappearance, you trained us how to worship your deity form in Vṛndāvana so we could understand how you wanted us to worship you in the future.

You gave us every instruction, every teaching we would ever need to get back home, back to Godhead, back to our original Kṛṣṇa consciousness, our personal association with Lord Kṛṣṇa. Now I, your eternal disciple, can be safe and successful in this material world under the care of those instructions. Whatever I know now or may ever know of the Lord is by your personal introduction. Kindly go on introducing me to our Supreme Lord, so that I may also introduce others.

I was like a boy playing in his father's yard who hears his father's call. There was a choice to go and loiter in some corner, pretending not to hear, or to obediently answer. Due to the seriousness of your tone of voice, I chose to answer. I had answered once before, when you wanted help to spread your teachings. But this time I approached with some uncertainty, like the son who is told by the father, "Now it's time to have a heart-to-heart talk."

Certainly my record indicates I'm not one of your better sons or students. I've gotten some A's, an F or two, with at best a B average. With some audacity I used to hope for promotions or higher grades, until I realized my fallen condition. But you want me for a monitor in the class, so there must be some necessity. The monitor teaches the subjects authorized by the fully qualified professor and also by example.

In your *Easy Journey to Other Planets* you described twenty progressive points by which one can prepare himself for an easy journey back to Godhead. The thirteenth step is explained as follows: "He must not take on unlimited disciples. This means that a candidate who has successfully followed the first twelve items can also become a spiritual master himself, just as a student becomes a monitor in class with a limited number of disciples." You took a risk on me, so I'll accept the razor's edge very carefully.

For days on end I listened to your old storefront tapes, trying to put my finger on what it was, what you did that won over our hearts. You were reaching out to us with great kindness and enthusiasm, befriending us, trying to teach us something very basic. It was something in your voice, in your patient explanations. It was *love.* You loved us so much we could not resist. We were so fallen, lost souls at the end of life's line, even though we were still young. Your love saved us, gave us life, new life. And still we live by your love.

In the hot summer of 1966, you lectured: "But, one thing, when we stand on our consciousness platform, you must know this consciousness platform and the dovetailing of consciousness \[with the Supreme\]—the whole thing—is based on love . . . love . . . *pure* love." You gave the example of how a father is pleased when his small son offers him a little piece of cookie in such a loving way. The son's wanting the father to enjoy the cookie is on the consideration of love, the exchange of love. And it is for our sake, our benefit, that we should offer like that. This is dovetailing. "Oh, God wants something to eat from me. Why should I not give Him something to eat?"

In a purport in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* you say that the disciple learns love from the spiritual master. So, first I'll teach them love for you, then love for one another in our spiritual society, because they have the same spiritual founding father. With our blessings they'll learn love for Lord Kṛṣṇa. And then they can love everyone—every man, bird, beast, bug, every living entity—and thus spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others.

To fulfill your desire, I need the blessings of my Godbrothers and Godsisters, your beloved disciples. Directly and indirectly, knowingly and unknowingly, I have sometimes been a party to and a cause of anxiety for them, which may have hampered their service to you. I have no excuse. I can only beg their forgiveness and hope for their blessings.

Now I pray to reject desires arising from false ego and drive out any enjoying spirit remaining in my heart. I want to be like the Kṛṣṇa conscious man you describe in the *Bhagavad-gītā:* "A Kṛṣṇa conscious man is not in need of anything, because the Lord fulfills all his material necessities. Therefore he is like the ocean—always full in himself. Desires may come to him like the waters of the rivers that flow into the ocean, but he is steady in his activities, and he is not even slightly disturbed by desires for sense gratification. That is the proof of a Kṛṣṇa conscious man—one who has lost all inclinations for material sense gratification, although the desires are present." I pray to keep a healthy fear of *māyā,* never foolishly assuming freedom from her urges, but increasing my tolerance.

These are my prayers for *guru-kṛpā,* your mercy, so that I may fulfill your instructions to me: "At the present moment there is nothing but great calamity, but by injecting Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the masses, we can change the situation greatly." Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, *we* can change the situation, working together. And all the credit goes to you. "All credit to the master, not the servant." All the glory belongs to you.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

please accept my humble obeisances. I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā homage while sitting on top of "Govardhana Hill" at Gītā-nāgarī. It is springtime, and the cows are grazing on fresh grasses and mooing with peaceful satisfaction. There have been improvements here since your visit in 1976—some new buildings, more devotees, more oxen working in the fields—but in important, basic ways the farm remains as it was when you blessed it with your presence and your approval. I think that this balance of expanding upon your orders while at the same time keeping everything exactly as you wanted is a key to our pleasing you—and it is not always easily achieved.

If we never added anything at all to your projects, you might be displeased that we were lazy and timid and did not use our brains and efforts to expand the preaching. Yet, if you came back and found too many basic changes, you might also be disturbed. For example, you stated once that your greatest anxiety was that we might make changes in the Deity worship after you had set it up just as you wanted it.

I don't think we should be overconfident that we have achieved this balance, but we should be introspective, always thinking whether we are pleasing you, and we should also consult with your living representatives, our Godbrothers. It is only by the mercy *of guru* and the Vaiṣṇavas that we can please Lord Kṛṣṇa.

In the material world all things must pass, but your word will never pass away, for you are the eternal spiritual master. Even your specific orders you intended to last for the duration of the golden era of Lord Caitanya, which continues for ten thousand years within Kali-yuga. You said, for example, that your books would last as the law books for mankind. Therefore, while we may write additional books in *paramparā* (which you also requested), we are not at liberty to put your books aside or deemphasize them. If we want to please you, we must go on reading and distributing your books and bequeathing this as a priority to the coming generations of devotees. If we stay faithful to your books, then we know that you are always with us.

Similarly, we have your instructions and example that we should live in the association of devotees in ISKCON. You worked like a superhuman even up until your last days, giving great emphasis to the establishment of ISKCON communities. We are meant to live and prosper spiritually in these ISKCON centers. They are not a makeshift arrangement for the 1970s or 1980s, but as long as we live we will please you by keeping the ISKCON centers, be they big or small.

And to govern ISKCON we have the system of shared leadership for which you carefully trained us, the G.B.C., and the temple presidents. We cannot do away with such basic institutions and claim it as your desire.

In a similar way, although we have expanded into a movement with many spiritual masters, as you desired, yet you alone are the movement's founder*-ācārya,* and no one can replace you in that unique, worshipable seat.

You have also given the basic foundation for *gurukula,* and we would be foolish to overlook it in favor of something else. And you have taught us how to worship the Deity, how to hold *kīrtana,* how to emphasize Vṛndāvana and Māyāpur, how to give up all sense gratification and dedicate our lives to the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Both in broad outline as well as in many personal details, you have shown us your way.

On your appearance day I make my homage and pray to my responsible Godbrothers to help me stay strictly on the Bhaktivedanta Swami Marg. I know I have tendencies to deviate, and therefore I need to be reminded that I am only a small servant and my duty is to serve within your ISKCON. Neither do I want to simply sit on the Prabhupāda path like a dead stone or bray like a bothersome donkey. I aspire to keep up with the pace and to be a pleasing addition. I will please you by helping to expand the numbers of your loyal followers and by bringing to you good news of increases, expansions, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness revolutions in the impious civilization of Kali-yuga. I want to help, within ISKCON, to change the world's population to your way of thinking and doing. I beg you to allow me to work like that and to allow me to help my Godbrothers to also work like that—combining the virtues of progressive change with unchanging obedience to the way of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

**Oh, My Master's Lotus Feet Are Bittersweet**

God's greatest gift, service to my master,  
Exorcist of the ghost of past desire,  
Sweet purgative, bittersweet oil of castor.

I've found my master's service does inspire  
Desirelessness of all but pleasing him;  
Without remorse, I watch life's joys expire.

Let lust retire, that most acidic sin,  
By guru's grace, its misery alkalize  
My soured life, rescued by his discipline.  
I hide my sin in shame lest he recognize  
My faults and condemn what I enjoyed;  
Better than praise: chastisement satisfies.  
My futile lies, God's servant to avoid,  
Fearing his virtues would smash my vain conceit,  
Forced by his love into service unalloyed.

How can I avoid the shelter of his feet,  
When the feelings of my heart are overjoyed?  
Oh, my master's lotus feet are bittersweet.

Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto you, the ocean of mercy and all-auspiciousness, particularly for us, your disciples and followers,

With straw between my teeth I submit this "tale of incompetency."

As every year brings your appearance day, I am finding it increasingly difficult to present some adequate glorification. Although my sense of gratitude toward you is growing with the seconds by leaps and bounds, my understanding of you seems to be decreasing more and more. Your stature is augmenting before my eyes in unlimited proportions, and when I approach you through my thoughts, I feel utterly insignificant. Just as when you were personally present, before you I become dumbfounded. But in the past your soothing presence, the atmosphere it created, would relieve me from any kind of anxiety. Now it is a horrible feeling. I feel so dry, empty. I am searching within for some proper words of praise, but my inspiration is meager. It is as if I were on the shore of the ocean, trying to understand how to jump over it. After so many years, this is my advancement: I can't even praise the supreme object of my worship!

Kṛṣṇa consciousness means fearlessness. "Abhaya Caraṇāravinda": Kṛṣṇa inspired both your father and your spiritual master with this most befitting name for you, describing the perfect stage of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the only truly fearless position. And I am just fearful. Literally! Full of fear. My only hope is to gain a place at your lotus feet. But how? Service. I was always afraid to ask: "How can I serve you?" Why? Because you might have told me. I had a better idea. Still, by your infinite compassion and kind understanding you blessed my idea. Now I am just ashamed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the giver; I am just a beggar. I only have one thing: my connection with you. Of course, it is the most valuable thing. Kṛṣṇa is in your possession, and you can give Him to whomever you choose. May I only want Kṛṣṇa from you. May I never become a *guru-druha* (an ungrateful enjoyer of your favors). Please give me the blessing that I may be able to please you. Please bless me that one day I may not come empty-handed before you. Please always dwell in my heart, so I may be able to give you to others. There is no greater wealth in the three worlds than to have you.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Accra

**Ten Years in Separation**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Time is nigh, I breath with a sigh, that day will come no doubt  
When the bewildered masses, involved in so many classes, take pains to find a way out.  
The Christians were saying for so many years, and now the last days  
They proclaim are definitely here. A new world, a new day, a new dawn—I ponder on.

Will the world be clammering at our door for shelter? Will all their sophistication fall down in prostration?  
Is the time for change in the environment nearing that will facilitate our putting Kṛṣṇa consciousness on a firm bearing?  
The books have gone out, and still more go every day, but when the desire to know God in truth will come, no one can say. Ours is not to say why or when; it is guru-ājñā till I put down my pen.  
Preach the word and hope for the best, satisfy your guru, let Kṛṣṇa's mercy do the rest.

I am inclined, no doubt, to speculate, but I feel that before the end of this century we shall know our fate.  
Civilization will be in grave doubt as to its own perpetuation, as to its plans / or sense gratification, and what to speak of  
economic proliferation.

Destiny is sure to slap their face for having the truth so near and still run the rat race.  
Durgā is hard, but what's to come up ahead will shock them all but dead.  
When life is not a surety, and the future an uncertainty, perhaps only then will they shout, "He Govinda! Hare Kṛṣṇa!"

Time will tell. It will ring a death knell, scare them all to hell.  
But that will be our hour of glory, as long as the devotees still proclaim your glory.  
Who to turn to will be foremost in their minds, and I as your servant will shine.

Go to the lotus feet of the supreme servant of God. He has the answer to save you from death.  
Surrender to Prabhupāda, and be blessed.  
O Prabhupāda, your mercy is all I need;  
Save me, a fallen, my heart doth bleed.  
Only you can relieve me from this toil.  
I surrender to you, heart and soul.

Your servants of ISKCON Accra, Ghana

## Ann Arbor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on the most auspicious day of your divine Vyāsa-pūjā.

Of the many auspicious days throughout the year, this day of the celebration of your appearance is the most dear and cherished by your followers. Without your coming and teaching us, what meaning would there be to the other various Vaiṣṇava festival days? They would pass us by, unobserved, as they did for countless years throughout many births.

Without your mercifully appearing to save us, we shudder to think of what our lives might be like. We can only be certain that we would be forced to visit many of the hellish planets described in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* translations.

"As fire is kindled by wood from another fire, the divine consciousness of man can similarly be kindled by another divine grace. His Divine Grace the spiritual master can kindle the spiritual fire from the woodlike living entity by imparting proper spiritual messages injected through the receptive ear. Therefore, one is required to approach the proper spiritual master with receptive ears only, and thus divine existence is gradually realized." *(Bhāg.* 1.2.32purport)

And so, although we are dull and woodlike, by constantly hearing from you we are gradually reviving our divine consciousness. You are administering the medicine whereby we can become free from the anguish resulting from misidentifying the body with the self. Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa urges us to become His surrendered devotees, promising to protect us from all sinful reactions, you continue to persuade us to cut the hard knots of material attachment deep-rooted within our hearts. You reassure us that there is no happiness to be found in this temporary, miserable material world. We believe you. We have faith that you are genuinely our well-wisher. And although you patiently encourage us, we regret that we have to apologize for being so slow and negligent in paying attention to your every instruction. It is not deliberate rebellion that causes us to fall short, but a long-standing material conditioning. Satsvarūpa Mahārāja writes in his *Reading Reform:* "I don't take the hellish planets or Yamarāja as mythological, but I question why I am not moved to read of it. Why not more frightened of hell or more compassionate toward those on the path to the hellish planets? And when there are descriptions of the beauty of Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual world, certainly I accept them as true, but why am I not more moved, attracted, ecstatic? So perhaps it is not so much intellectual doubt I am mentioning here but a dullness, a wretched dullness toward the living, transcendental reality of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* verses and purports. When will the day come that I will appreciate it?"

In the same way, we are anxious for the day when we will be able to fully appreciate the magnitude of what you have given us. In the meantime, knowing ourselves incompetent of managing any "big" projects, as many of your responsible servants do, we humbly offer you our little service. You have asked us to at least maintain what you have given us, if we cannot increase. And so, by your mercy, we are primarily engaged in trying to expose college students to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We know it is your desire that we make others fortunate by engaging in the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and so we feel safe, hoping that you may bless our meager attempts. In a letter to a disciple of December 22, 1972, you wrote: "I am especially pleased that you are infiltrating to schools and colleges with improved programs to encourage the student class to participate in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I completely approve of this program. I am also glad to hear from you how the distribution of literatures is also improving. That is the real meaning of improvement."

After years of laboriously studying dry speculative knowledge, the students are given a chance to finally experience the nectar of reading absolute knowledge from your books.

By our teaching vegetarian cooking classes and *distributing prasādam* at different campuses, the students are coming to relish Kṛṣṇa *prasādam.* You said that one who is habituated to taking *prasādam* will not have to come back as a ghost, but will obtain the opportunity to take a human birth.

Just as you inaugurated street chanting, we always try to remember the importance of engaging in *hari-nāma.* You said that if one hears the holy name, he must become a devotee.

On this most sacred of sacred days, we pray that we may persevere with faith and enthusiasm in the simple and sublime process of *bhakti-yoga,* which you have personally demonstrated for us. We want to properly worship you every day of the year.

"O King, a saintly person is just like water because he is free from all contamination, gentle by nature, and by speaking creates a beautiful vibration like that of flowing water. Just by seeing, touching, or hearing such a saintly person, the living entity is purified, just as one is cleansed by contact with pure water. Thus a saintly person, just like a holy place, purifies all those who contact him because he always chants the glories of the Lord." *(Bhāg.* 11.7.44)

You are the best of all saintly persons, and we thank you a million times for kindly and selflessly saving the world during its darkest hour.

*Your insignificant eternal servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Apūrva dāsa / Kamalinī-devī dāsī

**Granddisciples**

Śivānanda Sena dāsa / Pañca-tattva

## Athens, Greece

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day.

Please let us not forget our fortune in coming across Your Divine Grace, and above all let us not forget your instructions, by which this fortune can fully manifest itself within our hearts.

You told us how some of your Godbrothers forgot the instructions of your beloved Gurudeva, and soon the missionary spirit of preaching gave way to politics—one party undoing the work of another until the printing press that Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta was ready to sell the marble of the temple in order to buy had to be sold to pay for the legal battles that ensued. Let not history repeat itself.

Actually, you reassured us that nothing can stop this movement, but you also warned us not to misdirect our energy to conflict amongst ourselves, as this above all else was what would hamper the progress of the movement.

You told us that our love for you will be tested after your departure by how we can cooperate together. This ISKCON, being your life's offering to your spiritual master, is a manifestation of your body. Let us not be so blinded by passion and become guilty of the great offense of mishandling it.

You have said that as Westerners we have a strong propensity to quarrel and fight. Passion is strong and if not transcended will blur our proper vision, and we will become so bewildered as to think that there are enemies within our own Society. We will be only too aware of our differences, and conflict will become inevitable. Offenses to those who have dedicated their lives to propagating the glories of the holy names will destroy our devotional creeper and the love and trust on which this Society is founded. Lord Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that it's lust which arises from this passion that is the all-devouring sinful enemy of the world. So it's the prime duty of all in this Society to strictly follow the four regulative principles and chant sixteen rounds a day, avoiding offenses, as you have taught, in order to transcend the mode of passion. Otherwise it will surely undo us.

And then what would be the difference between our Society and the society of nondevotees, which is characterized by unresolvable conflicts between individuals, committees, nations, and idealogies, etc? What is our contribution to the world going to be if we do not strictly follow your instructions? More than anything, the world needs to be shown the perfect example to inspire all to follow. Now that you are not physically present, we have to become that perfect example more than at any other time. Actually, your instructions are very simple, and we should avoid finding solutions outside of them. Only those who are not so simple may find them complicated and difficult or impractical to follow.

Let us not forget to remain humble, more humble than a straw in the street, so as to develop our taste for chanting. And let us never lose our taste and desire for preaching, because as soon as we do, our temples will close or become lifeless monuments of some past glory.

So being bereft of sufficient intelligence, lowly as we are, we humbly beg that you give us that intelligence to see the way you want us to do things and the strength needed to carry them out. Please help us give up any contamination or motivation which may blind our proper vision of the glorious but sometimes difficult path back to Godhead.

*Your servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Gokulānanda dāsa

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda and present ISKCON** ***ācāryas***

Nandagopa dāsa

## Athens, Ohio

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances. As the representative of Lord Caitanya, you are teaching us to be humble, to give all respects to others while wanting none for ourselves. And yet, you are sitting in the most exalted position, and we, your disciples and granddisciples, are glorifying you, even more so on the most auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā. A conditioned soul, afflicted with dual vision, might balk at the seeming paradox. But from the spiritual viewpoint we can see how acceptance of such glorification is actually the essence of Vaiṣṇava humility, because you are accepting it on behalf of Kṛṣṇa and your own Guru Mahārāja. Only the humblest, most selfless Vaiṣṇava could accept such a position, brushing away his own personal desires for anonymity and accepting so much attention for the sake of glorifying Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, and the entire *paramparā,* without becoming at all affected.

You are great, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you show what is real renunciation. Unlike the bogus *yogīs* and impersonalists, who show "pain" if in contact with money (while delighting at the public attention given to their "humility"), you express transcendentally gleeful eagerness to use everything on the Lord's behalf. Out of compassion and true understanding, you gave up your peaceful existence in Vṛndāvana to come and sit on the *vyāsāsana,* which appears like a throne and so draws all kinds of criticism. But you can sit there, so firm and erect, because you know it's not your seat. You are not uncertain and bewildered by any lack of self-confidence; no, you are simply representing your *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, and thus there can be no mistake.

You are great because you are the perfect, transparent medium, and thus we can see Kṛṣṇa, who is the all-great, through you. And you are great because you did not interrupt or end the *paramparā,* or turn attention away from the eternal greatness of Kṛṣṇa towards any independent position of your own. Rather, you are a great link in the chain of disciplic succession that so many other chains can branch out from, in exact accordance with the desire and intention of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are great, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are awakening others to their inherent spiritual perfection and duty to preach on Lord Caitanya's order, and because you leave a truly transcendental legacy for whoever seeks it, unaffected by temporary appearances and disappearances in the material world.

You are great because you are truly *guru,* able to make others *guru* also. Even in the material world, a man wants a son so that his family line may continue. He is not satisfied if his son can produce no seed of his own. He is looking towards his future, in the form of his son's offspring, and he is not satisfied if the son simply turns around and glorifies his past.

You are great because you are the perfect servant of Śrī Guru, in the form of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda. The perfect servant of Śrī Guru is also *guru;* but he who wants to be *guru,* who wants power, authority, prestige, even recognition of his "humility," cannot actually be *guru.* Only he who serves, selflessly, as you do.

You have explained everything perfectly, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Kindly give me your blessings so that I can properly understand and manifest your teachings, and I will then be able to actually glorify you.

*From your servants in Athens, Ohio,*

Narasiṁha Guru dāsa / Ananta dāsa / Kiṅkinī-devī-dāsī / Śruti-devī-dāsī / Sarvamayī-devī-dāsī / Jalebi dāsī

## Atlanta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet

On this auspicious day so many exalted Vaiṣṇavas throughout the world are attempting to offer some glorification of you and the wonderful activities that you exhibited while walking here among us. Just as Ananta Śeṣanāga, with all of His thousands of mouths, cannot sufficiently glorify the Supreme Lord, similarly we, your disciples and granddisciples, cannot with our combined efforts adequately glorify Your Divine Grace. Yet still we are trying in some humble way to inform the world of your transcendental glories.

In your physical absence we are beginning to feel the weight of the great responsibility that you have left for us to bear, namely the spreading of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. You showed us by your own example the importance of this movement, and you spent countless hours training some of your senior disciples so that they would be prepared to carry on this work after your departure from our vision. And although there have been some problems within your movement because of our inabilities, still we, your humble followers, are struggling to carry out your order.

So many of your disciples have left the close association of their Godbrothers and Godsisters, so we pray to you on this day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please enlighten them within their hearts and bring them back to help in pushing on this great movement. If we can work cooperatively for your pleasure, then I am sure that we can have a major impact on the present society and can help bring to the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so many wayward souls. You once said that your greatest suffering was that the mass of people were misdirected. So how can we, your humble followers, not feel some of this pain? If we are to be noted in Vaiṣṇava history as your true followers, then we also have to accept this great anxiety.

So we are praying to you on this most blessed day to please forgive us for any offenses that we might have committed, either consciously or unconsciously, in regards to your wonderful movement, ISKCON. And we are begging for your mercy so that we can always remain your humble servants.

(Written by Balabhadra Bhaṭṭācārya dāsa)

*Your disciples and granddisciples at New Śrī Panihati Dhāma,*

## Auckland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

When a devotee leaves the shelter of your lotus feet, it is sometimes asked, "Was he sincere?" The answer is yes; but somehow or other he did not maintain his realizations. Sincerity is based on our spiritual realizations. In the material world one may declare his sincerity in so many ways, based on his temporary concepts of what is real and meaningful. Unfortunately, however, these realizations are both temporary and illusory, and what appears to be sincerity ends in disappointment. However, on the spiritual platform everything is based on truth, and our sincerity manifests according to our realizations of that truth. The most essential aspect of our devotional service, therefore, is to maintain and deepen our realizations.

In the beginning of my spiritual life my realization was that I was suffering at the hands of the illusory energy, forced to obey the dictates of my senses based on the bodily concept of life. Then by the mercy of Your Divine Grace I came in contact with your devotees, who gave me their association and your instructions and elevated me to the platform of devotional service. How wonderful it was to come to the Sunday feast and enter into the temple's transcendental atmosphere! The wonderful *kīrtana* performed in front of the Deities was incomparable to anything else in this world. Relishing the delicious taste of *prasādam*, smelling the aroma of the incense offered to the Lord, chanting and dancing in ecstasy—to give me the realization that this was real life and that my past life was simply hallucination was your gift. Only by your grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have I been brought into the family of devotees. Only by your grace have I been created, and by your grace I am maintained, and if I forget this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then surely I shall be destroyed.

Please help me maintain this realization.

Your unlimited mercy is always available in your books. There are no hidden secrets. You have given us everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I would therefore like to rededicate myself to your divine lotus feet, in full realization that you are my only shelter in this dark world. If I can pray for one thing, let it be your mercy to allow me never to forget our eternal relationship and thus again become wretched and untouchable. From the bottom of my heart I would like to thank you once again for everything you have given to me. I will dedicate my life and possibly many future lives to attempt to pay back my eternal debt to you, my spiritual master.

(Written by Jayaśīla dāsa)

Your insignificant disciples,

## Baltimore

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. My only prayer is that I may serve you eternally, birth after birth.

As Lord Caitanya's most confidential associate, you have fulfilled the prophecy of Lord Nityānanda to establish Lord Kṛṣṇa's glories in every town and village. Especially in Kali-yuga, without your mercy, there is only cruelty; without your guidance, there is only confusion; and without your embrace, there is only loneliness and heartbreak.

You haven't left us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but are with us as we take shelter of your books, *Līlāmṛta, mūrti,* and the society of devotees that you so kindly created. As our vision becomes purified in your loving service, we experience your presence ever more. In these ways you continue to save us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, from a state of hellish existence, and simultaneously you are gradually turning this planet of death into Śrī Vaikuṇṭha Dhāma.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, taking shelter of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara, we, the residents of New Kulīna-grāma, rededicate ourselves to your mission. We only desire that all the residents of Bhārata-bhūmi receive the full mercy of Lord Caitanya and return home, back to Godhead.

Please inspire and benedict us so we can humbly carry on your mission. Unless you are pleased with us, we are totally incapable of accomplishing anything auspicious, either for ourselves or for others.

(Written by Akhiladhāra dāsa)

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Berkeley

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your two lotus feet. All glories to the Śrī Kṛṣṇa sankir-tana movement, which you brought to the West to save all of the fallen conditioned souls.

It is said by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura that the spiritual master is always chanting and dancing and engaging his disciples in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Actually you are engaging not only us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but you want to engage all of the souls in the universe in loving devotional service to the Lord.

Let us help you fulfill that desire of yours. Let us make our center a place where people can come and take shelter of your lotus feet, and through you obtain the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Please allow us to act as your pure representatives, without distortion or personal ambition, and let us inject your unchanged message into the hearts of all whom we chance to meet. Let us personally advance in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness which you have given us, so that we may gradually (or as quickly as possible) obtain the platform of pure unalloyed Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which we understand you desire us to do. And if you so desire, let us provide a shelter for all of your disciples who may, for one reason or another, feel distressed or in need of transcendental association in a place where Kṛṣṇa and you are eternally at the center of everything.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you, who are the topmost servant of the Lord. Kṛṣṇa is yours; you can give Him to us. Please cast your merciful glance in our direction!

(Written by Vipramukhya Swami)

Trivikrama Swami / Bhaktisāraṅga Swami / Hanumat-preṣaka Swami

and all of your servants at ISKCON Berkeley.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

ON THIS MOST auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

What were we before we met you? Overnight—new hope, new life, new person. No longer a plaything for *māyā,* tossed around in a cold, fearsome world, but now a soldier in Lord Caitanya's army, fighting the forces of ignorance.

So much energy and dedication at your service! Teams of eager preachers sacrificing to save the conditioned souls. You witnessed your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement grow from the humblest of beginnings to a mighty worldwide organization carrying forward the mission of Lord Caitanya.

You were constantly attentive to your fledgling society. Whenever there was indecision or dispute, you revealed the conclusion. Whenever we were tired or afflicted, you nursed us to strength. Whenever selfish desires overwhelmed us, you dispelled our illusions. Whenever we needed encouragement, your smile inspired us again. Traveling, preaching, you reminded us of our close personal connection.

However, it is nearly nine years since we witnessed your pastimes. With heavy hearts we must report that many of those bright, enthusiastic faces, eyes shining with tears of ecstasy, which beheld you during your many visits to England, have somehow faded in these few but long years since your disappearance.

You told us often that our Kṛṣṇa consciousness was not dependent upon your physical presence, but upon our being determinedly fixed on following your instructions. You expected us to stay, always remembering your words and each day becoming more intensely absorbed in your mission. And there is no uncertainty about the nature of your mission. Over and over you petitioned us: "So kindly help me. That is my request. Print as many books as possible, in as many languages as possible, and distribute throughout the whole world. Then this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will automatically increase."

We must admit that because of circumstances, we have not been able to take up that task to the extent we would like. However, now we can assure you that the desire to develop book distribution is overflooding our devotee community like a tidal wave. This year we can promise Your Divine Grace an offering of much-increased *saṅkīrtana* results that we hope will bring you great pleasure. You told us, "The sales figures—this is my only solace in my life. When I hear that my books are selling nicely, I become like a young man."

If we neglect the order of the spiritual master, how can we achieve your mercy? How can we feel your presence? How will we be protected from *māyā’s* influence? But if we take up your order with full faith and conviction, then where is there room for doubts, fear, or difficulty?

You live forever in your books, and any of your disciples who make book distribution their life and soul will never be alone. You will take them by the hand and guide them back to Godhead.

Just as in your presence there was always clear direction, so when we are dedicated to book distribution, there will never be any misunderstanding of your desire.

Just as in your presence we were confident of your fatherly protection, so when we distribute your books, we know you are right by our side.

Just as in your presence any impure thoughts seemed conspicuously out of place in our minds, so when we are meditating on book distribution, material desires will gain no foothold.

Just as in your presence your smile would light our hearts, so when we engage in book distribution, we can sense your pleasure by the joy of *saṅkīrtana.*

Just as in your presence we felt you would never leave us, so by distributing your books, we realize our connection is eternal and complete.

This world is too dangerous and *māyā’s* influence so insidious and overwhelming that both as individuals and as a society we need to be fully absorbed in printing and distributing your transcendental literatures. Otherwise, there is every possibility we will lose sight of you, and then, from lack of direction, protection, purity, higher taste, and realization of you, we may fade away and miss our chance to return back home, back to Godhead.

Hoping to please you by book distribution, we beg your mercy.

*Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Bhaktivedanta Village

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Sanātana Gosvāmī praises the exalted transcendental character of Haridāsa Ṭhākura. He says, "Some behave properly but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach, but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore, you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world." On reading these scriptural instructions, we cannot help thinking of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You, like Haridāsa Ṭhākura, perfectly fit this description of the *jagad-guru,* the spiritual master of the entire world. The two essential qualifications mentioned here are *ācāra,* or proper behavior, and *pracāra,* or the ability to preach the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We see by your entire life's work that you displayed the highest transcendental Vaiṣṇava qualities and simultaneously preached with unrivaled determination and success.

Your perfect Vaiṣṇava behavior was probably best demonstrated by your quality of attracting everyone to you, and thus to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. From Bowery bums and Haight-Ashbury hippies to illustrious leaders and scholars, no one could help but be captivated by your charm, wit, intelligence, and your manner, which was warm and gracious and at the same time dignified, strong, and confident. I remember feeling that being in your association was like being in the presence of a great, noble king or demigod, but still one felt comfortable, safe, and protected, as by a loving father. You always seemed to act perfectly in every situation. Every word, every movement, every feeling seemed perfect for the moment and pleased and satisfied everyone around you, both disciples and nondevotees alike.

But the behavior, qualities, and personality that you exhibited were never on the material platform. Your character was far beyond any mundane charisma, managerial expertise, or public relations skill that other great personalities of history might have possessed. Your external features were manifestations of your inner purity, your absolute, unalloyed, pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. It was the pure, unadulterated quality of the soul in perfect accord with God that shone through, more brilliantly than thousands of suns. Because the pure devotee is acting in this world only on behalf of the Lord, he naturally possesses the divine qualities of Kṛṣṇa Himself, and these qualities are all-attractive to the living entities. Thus, everyone became attracted to you, because you are a transparent medium to Kṛṣṇa, and through you we received a glimpse of the spiritual world, of divine consciousness, and of freedom from material miseries.

As a perfect Vaiṣṇava, your spiritual qualities were not ethereal or illusive but demonstrated by your practical action and behavior. The spiritual master is *ācārya,* he who teaches by example, and you always set the perfect and pure example for us, a goal that we should strive towards, however imperfect our attempt. There was never a shadow of a doubt of your purity and unflinching desire to always serve Kṛṣṇa, even at great personal sacrifice, and therefore you were invulnerable to any attack or criticism from the nondevotees. Your absolute purity was what held our movement together and gave us individual strength and conviction. We know that we would never be cheated by you, that you instructed us only for our ultimate good. You demonstrated renunciation of all material sense gratification, preferring a simple room in Vṛndāvana to the opulent facilities of the modern Western cities. You worked tirelessly, without cessation, writing, preaching, and teaching your disciples.

Cynical, disillusioned, morose as we were, nurtured on Western materialism and agnosticism, how could anyone less great than you have created faith in our hardened hearts? But by your spotless purity, exemplary character, total mercy and compassion, and by your undaunted conviction in your *guru* and the *śāstra,* a glimmer of hope and faith awakened in our hearts. Then, by continual association and by following your instructions, we were able to give up so many bad habits and gain some taste and appreciation for the process of hearing and chanting the glories of Kṛṣṇa.

And just as we can never adequately glorify your personal qualities, equally wonderful is your preaching work within this dark Kali-yuga world. If your example is what brought us to the platform of faith, it was your words and instructions that sustained us in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Jīva Gosvāmī describes that the process by which the spiritual master gives liberation to the disciple is through the imparting of transcendental knowledge. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, alone among all *swamis,* philosophers, and spiritualists, gave us the perfect knowledge of the *Vedas,* intact, unchanged, but in a manner that we could understand and practically apply. Armed with the transcendental knowledge of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and with your purports and instructions, we were able to bring this philosophy into our lives, profoundly change all of our sinful activities and useless pursuits, and take up a life of devotional service. This giving to us of pure spiritual knowledge is the greatest gift we could ever receive, and while we might try for lifetimes, we will never be able to repay this debt to you.

We can only hope and pray that we will use this knowledge properly, to advance in real Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If we can become simple and humble disciples, unconcerned for power, position, and wealth, but only concerned with following your instructions purely, by taking full shelter in the instructions of your books, then perhaps we can please you to some small degree. We pray not to change this philosophy or to deviate from what you have given us, but to glorify you by trying to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and continuing the preaching movement that you have begun for the benefit of all living entities in the world.

Praying for your causeless mercy,

(Written by Tamohara dāsa)

*Your most insignificant servants of Bhaktivedanta Village,*

## Bologna

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I BOW DOWN at the dust of your lotus feet again and again. The thought of remembering you is very sweet. On this occasion my consciousness is enlivened by this effort of glorifying your glorious deeds and appearance. The more I endeavor to serve your divine lotus feet, the more my consciousness becomes purified. You can only be appreciated by the pure mind and senses, and my hope is that by rendering service unto you, one day I will be able to appreciate you more and serve you properly. You are an ocean of compassion, always tolerant and ready to forgive my offenses. While I write this offering I feel so incompetent due to my sinful deeds, and remembering your merciful personality, I feel encouraged to become more serious in serving you properly. Since I have joined your wonderful movement, you have picked me up an unlimited number of times, like a beloved father. Due to my stubborn and obstinate pride, I always try to revert to my gross nature, but you understand very much this defect and patiently engage my mind in your service and in the service of Kṛṣṇa. There is no comparison to your compassion. You saved me from the most dangerous type of material existence. I am eternally indebted to you, life after life. It is not possible for me to repay the debt I have with you. I can pray or try to pray sincerely, in order that one day I will develop a sincere attitude to serve you more sincerely. I'm weak, obstinate. In this condition, what is the hope? You are my hope, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Because of your divine presence I feel very inspired, and this inspiration comes from Kṛṣṇa. You are the true representative of God, and He inspires the desire to serve your lotus feet, even though there are no good qualifications in me. Service to you makes Kṛṣṇa very pleased and purifies my heart more and more. There is no limit to the amount that the heart has to be purified, and there is no limit to your purity No dirty heart can stand in the presence of your shining purity and pure presence. By your causeless mercy only can I think of words for appreciating your divine personality. Now in this miraculous 1986, the year of the five-hundredth appearance of Lord Caitanya, I pray that I will more and more surrender to your divine lotus feet, to your sacred mission, to enthuse others for appreciating your true glory. You have sacrificed everything for Lord Caitanya! *Vairāgya-vidyā-nija-bhakti-yoga.* How great you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You have created ISKCON, the spiritual world on this material planet. Unlimited mercy is pouring out from every center, millions upon millions of people are being reached by your wonderful books, and they are reading them with great delight. Kṛṣṇa has one desire: He wants to be known in every town and village. But your disciples want also to distribute your glory to each and every individual person. Your glory is even greater, because you always put Kṛṣṇa in the center of everything. My only desire is to help your disciples be part of this sacred mission. Telling the people of your qualities, your wonderful personality—this will please Kṛṣṇa even more. He is happy when pure devotees like yourself are glorified. How glorious you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

By your example you are showing us how to fight against *māyā,* how to throw the bombs of your courageous statements, which will defeat atheism. Nothing can stop you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! This world can only be changed by distribution of your powerful books, your message against the evil effects of Kali-yuga. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to develop a honest desire to spontaneously distribute your books, learning that this service will please you very much. Teach me how to become an instrument to act as you wish, without other motivation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so great. The more I write this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, the more thankful I must feel that another year you have helped me. Even though I do not deserve to do service to Kṛṣṇa, you are kindly inspiring me. This is the proof of your all-pervasive compassion.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I hope to remain—begging to remain—your eternal servant. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

(Written by Durgama dāsa)

*Your servants in Bologna, Italy,*

## Bombay

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet! All glories to you, the dearmost servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī!

Whenever I think about writing something about you, I feel completely incapable. I can only pray, like the child Dhruva, for your mercy that I may be able to glorify you appropriately.

The year 1972 was one of expansion for ISKCON India. Many groups of your disciples came from the West on your special order to take up service here. I also came in the fall. You had acquired a four-acre plot of land at Juhu, Bombay, and called it Hare Krishna Land. We were still a fledgling society of devotees, and this was a major transaction. Great excitement rent the air as you declared, "A magnificent temple will very soon come up." As if to demonstrate how He always fulfills the desires of His pure devotees, Kṛṣṇa immediately sent a Dutch architect, who had been wandering about India, to your lotus feet for shelter. Even Their Divine Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsavihārī made Their appearance at Hare Krishna Land in 1972. Under your enthusiastic order plans were drawn up, dignitaries were invited, and a foundation-laying ceremony was held.

But 1973 unfolded an entirely different, unexpected *līlā—*a transformation from sweet to salt, from victory to impasse, from confident exaltation to doubt. The owner of the land tried to cheat us by not giving the conveyance deed. The police were bribed and tried to throw us off the land, and a long court battle seemed inevitable. An inexperienced disciple canceled the sales agreement, and our hopes in court were thus defeated. The devotees became bewildered, exhausted, and overcome by diseases.

In the midst of all despair, you rose up, like a mighty warrior resplendent in shining armor, to face the challenge of *māyā’s* agents. Courageous, undaunted, and tireless, you secured ultimate victory for your master. Like Sañjaya of Kurukṣetra, we were also awestruck by the mystic potency of Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee working together to overcome impossible odds. Of course now it is just history for some, another chapter in *Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* but I would trade all my years in this life and whatever balance may be there to relive those three years with you.

You didn't stay with us long enough to see your temple completed. It is a superexcellent temple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Up to ten thousand people visit every day, and perhaps more than twenty-five thousand on Sundays. Industrialists, government ministers, scholars, and saints all find shelter in your temple. Your vision is immaculate. You have given us a blueprint with facility to save ourselves, Bombay, India, and the whole world from gliding down to hell.

We can never repay you, but I know what would please you. We, the residents of Hare Krishna Land, Bombay, free from envy and duplicity, must work together to maintain this wonderful preaching facility that you have given us. It will expand automatically. We cannot impede the progress, but we can only benefit ourselves by serving your order. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, when I am sure you are observing us, I beg for your mercy. Please give us a taste for the pure holy name and a little service to Rādhā-Rāsavihārī.

*Your servants,*

## Boston

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day. In the *Śrīmad- Bhāgavatam* (7.9.29) we find the following prayer offered to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva by Prahlāda Mahārāja: "My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?"

Within his eloquent statement, the exalted *mahājana* Prahlāda humbly exhibits the proper mentality of a devotee who has been saved from material life by the mercy of a bona fide spiritual master. Although the details of his life are extraordinary (being conceived by the greatest demon, Hiraṇyakaśipu; receiving transcendental instruction from Nārada while within the womb; and being saved by the Supreme Lord, who appeared personally as Nṛsiṁha-deva to protect him), we can nevertheless follow in his footsteps by appreciating the mercy that has been bestowed upon us by Your Divine Grace, our eternal master and friend.

Who is there in this age of Kali who is not overwhelmed by multitudes of material desires? Who is not engaged in all varieties of sinful life? Who is not implicated in karmic reactions that will surely result in animal birth? Who knows the actual purpose of human life? Who really understands anything about the spiritual nature of ourselves and God? Without being saved by Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, who amongst us would not still be blindly following the general populace on the path to hell?

You and no one else opened our eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of transcendental knowledge. You rescued us from the raging waves of the ocean of birth and death by throwing us the lifeline of the holy name, which is the only means of liberation in this age. You built a house in which we can all live happily in this world and return back to Godhead at the end of life. You have given us all that we could have ever hoped for and much more—more than we could have ever dreamt of—for you have given us Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Therefore our first and only duty is to serve Your Divine Grace by following your instructions and carrying out your orders. And what have you asked us? Simply to be happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to cooperatively work together in spreading Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement. This can easily be done by living within ISKCON as you have personally taught us—by chanting sixteen rounds, following the regulative principles, worshiping the Deity, associating with devotees, and reading and distributing your books. Thus by pleasing Your Divine Grace we will surely be pleasing Kṛṣṇa—*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ.* How can we even consider leaving your service or doing anything else?

Prahlāda Mahārāja was seeing Lord Nṛsiṁha face to face and was being offered benedictions by the Lord. But Prahlāda Mahārāja, remembering how he had achieved the mercy of the Lord by the grace of his spiritual master, only desired to remain in the service of Nārada Muni. This attitude is exemplary for all devotees. How can we ever forget that Your Divine Grace is saving us at every moment? Without the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, which contains everything that you have given, how can we make any advancement in spiritual life? Indeed, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have stated that you are ISKCON; therefore how can we serve you outside of your movement?

Our prayer, then, is to be allowed to continue rendering service to Your Divine Grace by assisting you in the propagation of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

(Written by Keśihantā dāsa)
