## Boulder

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I have lamented for many years that although I am your initiated disciple, because of my sinful nature Kṛṣṇa allowed me to have your *darśana* for only the very last years of your earthly *līlā.* While I was still firmly in *māyā’s* grip, more fortunate souls were personally serving you, hearing from you, glorifying you, and worshiping you in your very presence and for many years. I do not know what sins or offenses I must have committed to be denied that greatest of mercy.

Since you left, when my Godbrothers or Godsisters talk of their intimate exchanges or special remembrances of you, I can only smile, try to appreciate their good fortune, and (I admit) hide my envy. Sometimes I wonder: Would Prabhupāda have been especially sweet with me, as he was with that *prabhu*? Or would he have been seemingly harsh, as he was with that devotee? Would he have chastised me, or maybe smiled at me? Maybe he would have discussed at great length with me, or maybe ignored me (mercifully reminding me of my insignificance). And then again I wonder: Why wasn't I allowed to associate with Prabhupāda like that? (It hurts if I think about it too much.)

Then one day Kṛṣṇa helped me to understand something, something which I'd heard many times before but never really understood—something about your mission.

Like a great statesman traveling to foreign lands to execute the special purposes of the king, you came here for a short while, Śrīla Prabhupāda, established your mission, and then went away (on to other places to do your work there). You came not to tour or enjoy this world, but to establish you mission, which is to deliver all conditioned souls unto the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa—to preach, to give us all your instructions (especially in the form of your books), and to establish the ISKCON branch of Lord Caitanya's movement, which will continue your work for the next ten thousand years. If we don't remember your mission, then we don't remember you.

Many years ago in India, when your printer requested you to stop publishing your books because it was too much of a strain for you, you replied, "I must, because it is my mission." In the earliest days in America, when you became sick and almost left your body, you instructed your disciples to pray to Kṛṣṇa for more time because you had not yet "completed your mission." And near the end you stayed with us for so long, despite disease and your being unable to eat, just to strengthen (a bit more) "your mission."

You were physically here for a few years, but your mission will last for thousands. You were physically here to open one hundred temples, but your mission will open millions. You personally were here to initiate ten thousand disciples, but your mission will deliver billions. And although your visit with us was brief and the opportunity for service to your *vapuḥ* was limited, your mission is enduring, and opportunity for service to your *vāṇī* is unlimited.

I was foolishly lamenting for so long that I didn't get to serve you closely or intimately, yet I had failed to understand what it was to be close, and what it meant to be intimate. For as we must daily remind ourselves, the essential purpose of your coming was to "preach the message of Lord Caitanya and deliver these Western countries \[and others\] from voidism and impersonalism." To understand your preaching mission is to understand you. To fail to understand your mission is to fail to understand you. To serve your mission is to serve you, and to fail to carry on your mission is to fail you. To intimately know your mission is the real meaning of intimately knowing and serving you.

If I love you at all, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then I must serve your mission. I must always work to help you deliver the conditioned souls and to keep your ISKCON society strong and intact. If I love you at all, I must always love to distribute your books, distribute *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and distribute the holy name. If I love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then I must sacrifice my personal desires and become a humble part of that preaching mission and the ISKCON movement you established.

Just as a pure devotee accepts everything favorable and rejects everything unfavorable for devotional service, I must accept everything favorable and reject everything unfavorable for serving your mission. This must become the standard for my life.

To serve you by becoming a dedicated preacher and servant of your mission is actually the most intimate service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And it is available to all your disciples, granddisciples, and all future generations. It is available to me, and it will always be available to me. This is how I will realize your smile, your chastisements, your gentleness, guidance, and love. This is how we all can come to know you and feel you with us most intimately at every moment. To forget preaching, to forget service to your mission despite so many remembrances of you, is to forget you.

(Written by Anuttama dāsa)

*Your servants of Boulder Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Anuttama dāsa / Rukmiṇī-devī dāsī / Raṅgavatī-devī dāsī

## Brisbane

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the light of our lives,  
You left us books to keep us alive.  
Just as a tree without water soon dies,  
So too, without you, we are nothing.

This whole world is a cosmic joke—  
Souls straining hard under māyā’s yoke.  
We were beaten and suffering until we awoke  
To the fact that, without you, we are nothing.

In this world we had nothing to say  
As we made silly plans to go our own way.  
We had nothing to do but be led astray.  
Until we met you, we were nothing.

Toy soldiers we were, with no general in sight,  
Just broken men, too weary to fight.  
We had no idea what was wrong or right.  
Again, without you, we were nothing.

The goals we cherished increased our delusion.  
All were based on bodily illusion.  
With just a few words you ended our confusion.  
So we say without you, we are nothing.

Suddenly we were in your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement,  
Surrounded by books of the highest transcendental knowledge,  
Prasādam, kīrtana, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam classes,  
Greeting the Deities, dancing and chanting,  
Kicking the demons and saving the innocent—  
All kinds of transcendental fun—  
All coming from you and leading us back to you and  
Kṛṣṇa—  
Now that's really something!

You gave us a cause, the noblest of all,  
To the saṅkīrtana movement we answered your call.  
O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please never let us fall,  
Because without you, we are nothing.

Brisbane Sankirtana Center, Australia

## Cairns

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

ALL GLORIES TO YOUR LOTUS FEET. All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* movement. All glories to the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. All glories to all the devotees of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

My dear Gurudeva, please accept my full prostrated obeisances, offered again and again, and wholeheartedly offered whenever, by great good fortune, I remember your lotus feet and your great kindness.

All over the world devotees are taking advantage of this auspicious day marking your appearance in this material world to remember you, glorify you, and rededicate themselves to your service and your mission. We pledge ourselves to you.

We have grown a lot since your departure, and we have gone through many sobering changes. There have been many casualties in our war against *maya,* and more than ever we must not dwell in the past but look ever forward to the future, to your service, and to the execution of your mission. There is so much to do, so much to accomplish, that it is almost a crime to divert our attention, as we have done in the recent past.

I remember you always emphasized the positive, the forward march: "Expand the preaching; increase your service." And thus you expertly treated our material conditioning with the only cure—service to Viṣṇu and Vaiṣṇavas.

Janmāṣṭamī 1976 in Hyderabad, after the opening of the Rādhā-Madana-mohana Mandira, you asked me if I was chanting my rounds. I guess you could read the anxiety on my face. We had been working day and night to complete the construction and festival arrangements. I had hardly looked at my *japa* beads. I was feeling miserable about the rounds and the weakness it indicated. With the big push over, things were back to normal. I explained how the rounds weren't too regular with all the work. You asked: "And now?"

I replied, "Yes, I am chanting now," and you replied, "Then that is all right."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, that "forward march" order you gave me then is, I feel, the same instruction you would give us now—to be fixed up and march forward.

There are those who say that the vine is withering. Let those fools go on croaking in the dark; little do they know their noise is simply inviting the snake of *māyā* to devour them.

The great ISKCON tree that you nurtured and which brought forth such wonderful fruits as your devotees, your temples, and your books is still with great vitality producing those fruits today. Those who have lost their service attitude have simply become blind.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength we need. You are our only shelter. We do not want to become lords—just humble servants of Viṣṇu and the Vaiṣṇavas.

All glories to your lotus feet.

(Written by Vaṁśīvādana Suvāsa dāsa)

## Chandigarh

We offer our most humble obeisances to our beloved spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is the eternally present founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and who is the savior of the whole world.

Jagad-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya*!

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

WE ARE such insignificant servants of Your Divine Grace that it is difficult for us to even imagine personally writing to you, what to speak of offering some pleasing glorification of such a great transcendental personality as you. At any rate, you have been unlimitedly kind to us by accepting us as your disciples and granddisciples and giving us shelter at your lotus feet. We feel very unqualified to manufacture any new ideas about the importance of your appearance, you being the greatest representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva to ever bless this earth, even though for such a few short years of pure, unmotivated, and uninterrupted preaching. It is our sincere feeling that there will never, in the next ten thousand years, be anyone as significant as you in our disciplic succession, and it is for this reason that we would simply like to offer a reminder to ourselves from your own eternal words about your special position and our duty to you as the spiritual master, friend, and ever well-wisher of all fallen souls who are suffering in this material world.

You have very nicely stated in *The Path of Perfection:* "In the *bhakti-yoga* process, the role of the spiritual master is most important and essential. Although the spiritual master will always come back until his devotees have achieved God realization, one should not try to take advantage of this. We should not trouble our spiritual master but should complete the *bhakti-yoga* process in this life. The disciple should be serious in his service to his spiritual master, and if the devotee is intelligent he should think, 'Why should I act in such a way that my spiritual master has to take the trouble to reclaim me again? Let me realize Kṛṣṇa in this life.' That is the proper way of thinking. We should not think, 'Oh, I am sure that my spiritual master will come and save me. Therefore I will do as I please.' If we have any affection for our spiritual master, we should complete the process in this life, so that he does not have to return to reclaim us.

"Although the spiritual master assumes responsibility for his disciple, we should not take advantage of this. Rather, we should try to please the spiritual master. We should not put our spiritual master in such a position that he has to reclaim us from a house of prostitution. But even if he has to do so, he will do it, because he assumes this responsibility when he accepts his disciple.

"The *bhakti-yoga* process should be completed in this life, because in this life we have all the instruments necessary to become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious. We have *mṛdaṅgas* and cymbals and tongues with which to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Even if we don't have *mṛdaṅgas* and cymbals, we have a tongue. No one has to purchase a tongue. We also have ears with which to hear the sound that the tongue vibrates. Therefore we have all the instruments we need with us—a tongue and ears. We only have to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and use our ears to hear this vibration, and all perfection will be there. We don't have to become highly educated scientists or philosophers. We have only to hear and chant."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind to us and allow us to continue to serve you in some small way. We pray that we will always be a credit to you and not give you any unnecessary trouble by not being serious to perform all of the basic devotional practices such as attending *maṅgala-ārati* and chanting sixteen rounds minimum.

Please bless us so that we will have strength to carry on this divine mission of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which you have so kindly delivered into the hands of the most fallen souls on this planet. Without this opportunity to serve the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, there would be absolutely no chance for souls as fallen as us to ever rectify our miserable material conditioning and follow you back home, back to Godhead. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, more than we could ever express in words.

*Your most insignificant servants at ISKCON Chandigarh*

## Chicago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

MAY I NEVER FORGET that it was by your mercy that I was first introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as it was you who underwent the austerities of humbly following your Gurudeva by coming west to preach about Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

May I never forget that you have given the science of self-realization, which enabled the devotees to maintain their Kṛṣṇa consciousness and undergo the austerities involved in serving and glorifying Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

May I never forget that it was you who tolerated our inability to fully surrender and give up our attachments, and who was always encouraging us to go on trying our best, despite so many shortcomings.

May I never forget how you always knew exactly how to chastise and correct us in our attempts to please you and serve Kṛṣṇa.

May I never forget your oceanic smile of pleasure when you heard how the devotees were working hard to serve and glorify Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

May I never forget that you came to *us,* to give Kṛṣṇa, undeserving as we were.

May I never forget that your instructions are all I need to go back home, back to Godhead.

May I never forget that it is for you that I maintain this body, using it as an instrument to try and push on your mission of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Your humble servants at Chicago ISKCON.*

## Chittagong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances. While you were physically among us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was so much easier to know what to do to serve and please you. We could understand immediately when and how you were satisfied with our services and when we were displeasing you. We were told to just do it like this, or do it like that, and we could be confident that we were on the correct track.

Now that you have been absent from our material vision for the past nine years, which seem like nine thousand, I feel lost and helpless. Although you have so mercifully remained with us in your books, instructions, and many sincere disciples, I feel so inadequate at not being able to take full advantage of your mercy. I am truly your most foolish disciple. I am constantly fearful that I may not be pleasing you, and I am without sufficient realization and sincerity to know what to do about it.

I simply pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please not forget me, even though I may all too often fail to remember you. Please forgive me as I stumble, please be kind to me, although I am so deficient in sincerity. Please chastise, correct, and guide me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though I am so worthless.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Niṣṭula dāsa

## Christchurch

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

How fortunate we are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to be your followers. To meet such an exalted personality as Your Divine Grace is extremely rare in this world. On your appearance day we offer you our prostrated obeisances many hundreds of times.

In the course of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you taught us, we realize what a wonderful gift you have so mercifully bestowed on human society.

You came and tirelessly worked to save this planet during its darkest hour. By reading about your heroic pastimes we are reminded of the great devotees mentioned throughout the pages of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. You are undoubtedly one of them, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In all our endeavors may we try to please you. By so doing we feel always close to you and under your protection. It is due to your intense desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that more and more people are now waking up to their real life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for appearing here in your beautiful *mūrti* form and for giving us Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra, the life and soul of your Christchurch temple.

May we never be bereft of service to Your Divine Grace and your society of devotees, ISKCON. Jagat-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

## Cincinnati

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances at your glorious lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master extinguishes the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

In Vṛndāvana, during the month of Dāmodara (October 1972), there was an initiation ceremony held on the full-moon night of the *rāsa-līlā,* at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple. The Krishna Balarāma Mandir had barely been started at that time. After the fire *yajña,* the second initiates went into Śrīla Prabhupāda's little room one by one to receive their Gāyatrī *mantra.* As I entered, I saw Śrīla Prabhupāda sitting at his low desk with his two beautiful lotus feet stretched out in front of him. Offering my obeisances, I gently took his lotus feet and placed them firmly on my head. Prabhupāda smiled and motioned for me to come sit beside him. He draped the sacred thread over my shoulder, showed me the way to chant on my fingers, and then chanted the *mantra* as I repeated each word after him. He gave me no specific instructions, but simply said that I should ask the older *brāhmaṇas* what to do. I wanted to say something to express some devotion, some appreciation, some prayer; but no words came. My voice faltered; I was almost crying. "O Śrīla Prabhupāda! I am so fallen. I am such a rascal. Please forgive me!" He smiled. "That's all right," he said, with a wave of his hand.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are truly an ocean of mercy to give such a rascal as me chance after chance to engage in your devotional service: "That's all right."

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-*  
*vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*romāñca -kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"Chanting the holy name, dancing in ecstasy, singing and playing musical instruments, the spiritual master is always gladdened by the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because he is relishing the mellows of pure devotion within his mind, sometimes his hair stands on end, he feels quivering in his body, and tears flow from his eyes like waves. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

The *kīrtana* was uproarious as Prabhupāda entered the airport with the devotees. He was leaving New York after their first, wonderful Ratha-yātrā. The *kīrtana* grew more intense as he went through the security check. Hari Śauri, Guru dāsa, and I followed behind. In the waiting lounge, Śrīla Prabhupāda took a seat and kindly motioned for Guru dāsa to sit next to him. A well-dressed man seated on Prabhupāda's other side, noticing that there were others with Śrīla Prabhupāda, very politely moved to another seat. Prabhupāda again very kindly offered us a seat, this time motioning toward me and Hari Śauri. I gently nudged Hari Śauri toward the seat. He graciously offered it back to me. He was Prabhupāda's personal servant; I nudged him again. He again graciously offered it back to me. I couldn't resist. I sat down next to Śrīla Prabhupāda. He looked very grave, almost sad. Perhaps he knew that this had been his last trip to America. His eyes were overflowing with tears of ecstatic love.

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāṁś ca niyuñjato ’pi*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the Deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other similar worship of the Lord. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

Śrīla Prabhupāda had come to Hyderabad for the first time (November 1972). We had arranged a ten-day *paṇḍāl* festival, to be held at the fairgrounds. There was a large, covered outdoor stage, where we put the altar for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava (Prabhupāda had had Them sent from Māyāpur) and Lord Caitanya. In front of the stage, on a lower level, a wooden platform had been built for Śrīla Prabhupāda's *vyāsāsana* and all the devotees. There was a severe drought at the time. It had not rained in almost two years, so they didn't cover the platform.

On the morning of the fourth day it began to rain. *(Saṅkīrtana yajña ki jaya!)* The devotees moved Prabhupāda's *vyāsāsana* to a covered area so that he would be able to give morning class. I had just finished dressing the Deities when Śrīla Prabhupāda arrived. Prabhupāda had been greeting the Deities from the wooden stage, so since it was raining, I assumed that he would just go straight back to where they had moved his *vyāsāsana.* But, determined to see the Deities, Prabhupāda walked along the narrow parapet of the stage (as I looked on nervously) and offered his *daṇḍavats.* Then the devotees led Śrīla Prabhupāda to his *vyāsāsana* as they had an ecstatic *kīrtana.* Of course, I wanted to go hear Prabhupāda give class, but since I was the *pūjārī* I knew that it was my duty to stay with the Deities. Not a moment later, a devotee came running down to the altar. "Prabhupāda wanted to make sure that someone was with the Deities!" "It's okay," I said, "I'm here." I was totally amazed! Everyone was chanting *"Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!" He was sitting on the *vyāsāsana,* being offered all worship and praise, yet he was thinking, "Is anyone with the Deities?" To Śrīla Prabhupāda They weren't just brass statues. They were Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa Themselves.

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is always offering to Kṛṣṇa four kinds of delicious food (analyzed as that which is licked, chewed, drunk, and sucked). When the spiritual master sees that the devotees are satisfied by eating** ***bhagavat-prasādam,*** **he is satisfied. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

After the Hyderabad festival, Śrīla Prabhupāda went to Bombay for a few days and then onto Ahmedabad for another *paṇḍāl* program. Often, Prabhupāda would be invited to someone's home for lunch. When there wasn't some service that needed to be done, I would try to go along. One day, a small group of devotees accompanied Śrīla Prabhupāda for lunch, and I was able to join them. I decided that whatever Prabhupāda would eat, I would eat also. Whatever he asked for more of, I would too. Prabhupāda was eating nicely, especially the potato *subji.* I was actually full just trying to keep up with him. When we were finished, I still had a *puri* on my plate. Śrīla Prabhupāda looked over at me and said, "Oh? You cannot finish?"

"Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda!" I quickly stuffed the *puri* into my mouth. Then, as he got up and began to leave, we dove at his plate, which was full of *mahā-mahā-prasādam*!

*śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-*  
*mādhurya-līlā guṇa-rūpa-nāmnām*  
*prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is always eager to hear and chant about the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Rādhikā and Mādhava and Their qualities, names, and forms. The spiritual master aspires to relish these at every moment. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

One night, at the Ahmedabad *paṇḍāl* program, Śrīla Prabhupāda asked one of the *mātājīs* to sing *Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava.* We were all very surprised, since there were many *brahmacārīs* present, and there were hundreds of Indians in the audience (and in my opinion she didn't even have a good singing voice). She led the chanting, as we, including Śrīla Prabhupāda, followed. When Śrīla Prabhupāda chanted *Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava,* he would sing with such devotion that simply watching him was a deep Kṛṣṇa conscious experience.- But we could see that whoever was chanting, Prabhupāda was relishing the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Rādhikā and Mādhava.

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is very dear because he is expert in assisting the** ***gopīs,*** **who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa's conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

Śrutakīrti and I were sitting there, taking *prasādam* with Śrīla Prabhupāda, reciting this verse and trying to get some understanding, some appreciation for his inconceivably exalted position. I am still trying. But I am very dull, very slow—*mūḍha.* Please excuse me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for my inability to serve you and glorify you properly.

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\]."**

At the airport in Ahmedabad, we had hung a huge "JAYA SRILA PRABHUPADA!" banner from the top of the main terminal building. As Śrīla Prabhupāda walked from the plane to the waiting car, I pointed out the banner. Prabhupāda smiled. At the life member's house where Śrīla Prabhupāda was to stay, the devotees began to offer *ārati* to Prabhupāda. I was surprised. Of course, when we offered anything to Kṛṣṇa, we first offered to Prabhupāda. But since *guru-pūjā* had not been introduced yet, I had never seen Śrīla Prabhupāda worshiped on his own, as if he were God. As I remembered this verse, I enthusiastically joined the *kīrtana.*

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise my spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master."**

"Please accept my blessings. I am in due receipt of your weekly *saṅkīrtana* newsletters. I am always happy to hear of increasing book sales. These reports are very encouraging to me. Our real business is to print and distribute books. By doing this business, you are all becoming recognized by Kṛṣṇa. Please try to continue working so nicely for Kṛṣṇa, following the rules and regulations, chanting sixteen rounds, and studying the books, and you will finish your business in this material world in this life and enter the spiritual world to live with Kṛṣṇa eternally. Don't become discouraged by any so-called problems. Make your program simple, that is to say, always make sure that you are somehow or other engaged in Kṛṣṇa's service, and the best service is preaching or engaging others in Kṛṣṇa's service. So continue in this way and be happy. Your ever well-wisher, A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami."

Your words, your smiles, everything you did was always so encouraging, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and still is. "Don't become discouraged by any so-called problems. Make your program simple ... So continue in this way and be happy." You are the kindest father, the wisest guide, the ever well-wisher, the most dear friend. May we always remember you. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! JAYA PRABHUPADA!! JAYA PRABHUPADA!! JAYA PRABHUPADA!!!!!

(Written by Śrutadeva dāsa)

*Begging to remain your eternal servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Śrutadeva dāsa

**Granddisciples**

Bala Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Kevalabhakti dāsa / Nara-Nārāyaṇa dāsa / Bhaktin Barbara / Bhaktin Diana / Bhaktin Marlene

## Columbus

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your ISK.CON movement. All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

As the years go by, we have to appreciate more and more how valuable is your association. By your divine instructions, our lives have been transformed into something more than simply a struggle for existence.

We can never repay the gift of pure devotional service that you are giving, but our duty is to try. This effort will be the reciprocation of your mercy.

At the blessed time of your Vyāsa-pūjā, it is also our duty to offer sincere words in glorification of your divine personality and mission. Unfortunate as we are, our words fall far short of properly glorifying a rare, gemlike devotee such as you. Hopefully, you will accept whatever little *bhakti* there may be in between the lines of our feeble attempt.

Perhaps the best words are those of reaffirmation of our allegiance to your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. These words, however, must be supported by practical cooperation amongst ourselves and rigid adherence to the rules and regulations laid down in the *śāstra*. There is no place for sentimental words, devoid of scriptural authority and laxness of *sādhana*.

We must not fall victim to insincerity and complacency. By your mercy alone we have been engaged in Kṛṣṇa's service, and by your mercy alone we shall remain engaged in Kṛṣṇa's service. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

(Written by Karūṣa dāsa)

*Begging to remain in your service, your servants at Columbus Yātrā,*

Karūṣa dāsa / Janaka dāsa / Ikṣvāku dāsa / Dhīmān dāsa / Maheśvara dāsa / Tapanācārya dāsa / Pracetās dāsa / Prabuddha dāsa / Ṛṣabhadeva dāsa / Bhakta Guy / Kusumāpīḍā-devī dāsī / Śaraṇā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Linda

## Costa Rica

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humblest obeisances. We are the fallen candidates for engagement in your service.

It is really impossible for us to write you an offering. Only through your wonderful mercy are we attempting to be understood. The glories of a pure devotee can only be realized by other pure devotees, so we are begging you to forgive our sparse expressions.

It is only by your mercy that we can presently attain the perfection of life through this movement. It is only by your kindness that we can be successful in chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. It is only by your wisdom that the Vedic knowledge can now be understood.

You've given us the easiest method of self-realization. You've saved us from all impersonalism and voidism now existing. You've given us protection by leaving us in the hands of your beloved disciples, your sons, and through them the pain of these suffering souls is being relieved.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have no qualities to address you. Please forgive us.

*Your servants from Costa Rica Mandira.*

## Dallas

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

For the next ten thousand years, you will continue to enthuse us to open temples, cultural centers, and other centers of learning, so that the sweet sound of Kṛṣṇa's holy name, accompanied by *karatālas* and *mṛdaṅgas,* will be continually sung and heard in every corner of every town and village.

For the next ten thousand years, you will inspire us to organize big feasts, open restaurants and other *prasādam* distribution centers, to enable massive distribution of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to all living beings on this planet, until everyone becomes addicted to *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and all slaughterhouses, breweries, tobacco factories, and other sinful places will be forced to close their doors for lack of customers.

For the next ten thousand years, you will order us to print and distribute your books, so that in every home and institution the nectarean messages of *Śrīmad-Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* will be heard and relished every day

For the next ten thousand years, you will guide us to educate and train our children in such a way that they will grow up to be saintly personalities, equipped with all the necessary qualities to be model citizens of this world.

For the next ten thousand years, you will remind us to establish and maintain rural *varṇāśrama* communities, so that all the frustrated people of this world can live according to the directions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In this way, simple living and high thinking will become a reality in every person's life.

For the next ten thousand years, you will empower us to destroy the atheistic propaganda of the so-called scientists and establish the true principles of scientific knowledge, as taught by the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His representatives.

For the next ten thousand years, you will invite us to sing, dance, perform dramas, and organize wonderful festivals, until there is only one culture on this planet—the culture of glorifying the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

For the next ten thousand years, you will continue to guide us, teach us, encourage us, and accept our meager offerings and worship. In these and many more ways you will respiritualize our very existence, so that we may become lovers of God again, with pure and clean hearts, fit to go back home, back to Godhead.

For the next ten thousand years, you will become recognized and glorified as the most dear and empowered servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, and you will be loved by all as the most empowered *śikṣā-guru* for the men, women, and children of all nations.

For the next ten thousand years and eternally, we beg to remain engaged in your loving devotional service.

*Your servants at Rādhā-Kālachandjī Dhāma,*

## Detroit

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

WE THE DEVOTEES of Devasadana Mandira would like to offer our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, begging that whatever tests might befall us, we may not fall under the influence of Kṛṣṇa's illusory potency. As one advances in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, *māyā* appears to be more subtle, and we see profit, adoration, and distinction rear their ugly heads. The self-motivated fall away, not knowing Kṛṣṇa's plan. It is a fact that no one can escape Kṛṣṇa's plan—for although we may have our idea, the Lord in His infinite wisdom has His, and we either accept it willingly or are dragged, kicking and screaming, to follow. You epitomize submission to Kṛṣṇa's will and are therefore qualified to lead us. In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* the sages at Naimiṣāraṇya state:

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**"We think that we have met Your Grace by the will of Providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being."** ***(Bhāg.*** **1.1.22)**

This obviously applies to all of us seated here today, for no one can claim to have crossed the dark ocean of Kali. Yet we can take solace from your humble prayer: "Personally, I have no hope for any direct service for the coming crores of births of the sojourn of my life, but I am confident that someday or other I shall be delivered from this mire of delusion in which I am presently so deeply sunk. Therefore let me with all my earnestness pray at the lotus feet of my divine master to allow me to suffer the lot for which I am destined due to my past misdoings, but to let me have this power of recollection: that I am nothing but a tiny servant of the Absolute Godhead, realized through the unflinching mercy of my divine master. Let me therefore bow down at his lotus feet with all the humility at my command." (Lecture given February 1936, appearance day of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glories are unlimited, so how can we hope to praise you? Yet that is our heartfelt desire, so on this glorious day we beg to remain at your lotus feet.

(Written by Indrānuja dāsa)

*The devotees of Devasadana Mandira,*

## Durban

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances in the divine dust of your lotus feet. On the auspicious occasion of the ninety-first anniversary of your blessed appearance day, we would like to be able to please you in some way. Although our efforts are clumsy and half-hearted, still you transform our gropings into pure devotional service and offer them to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Thus we are always dependent on your causeless mercy to become accepted as devotees. How else could we be accepted?

In 1975 you blessed the soil of South Africa with the touch of your lotus feet and planted a transcendental seed, which is now fully manifested in the blossoming flower of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha Temple of Understanding. Visitors are flocking from all over the world to marvel at this wonderful work of art, which is simply a partial manifestation of your pure devotion, simply a spark of your splendor.

On the glorious opening day you were the first to enter the new temple in your deity form, followed by your disciples and top-ranking officials from every section of society. Seated high on your palanquin as you took first *darsana* of Their beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha, surrounded by press men and photographers, you appeared jubilant, like a great general who has just made a huge conquest over the enemy ranks.

More than one hundred thousand people attended the occasion, the highway being blocked with traffic for three miles in both directions, and many people having to return home disappointed, unable to get in. On this day we could perceive the beginning of the unfolding of Lord Caitanya's plan for South Africa, with black, brown, and white men and women all chanting and dancing together in the ecstasy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Under the able guidance of your dear disciple Bhagavān Goswami, we are attempting to use this great facility in the way that you would want it. Now we are concentrating more on distributing your books, recruiting new devotees, developing *nāma-haṭṭa* groups, distributing *prasādam,* and preaching in the African homelands. Your temple is the focal point from which all these activities are gaining momentum.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are ISKCON, you are your books, you are your temples. If we can only preach, distribute books, and build temples, we will expand your influence everywhere, and more and more people will be delivered by coming into contact with your divine personality, which is as fathomless as the ocean.

Kindly give us a glimpse into that merciful mood of our *vaiṣṇava-sampradāya,* which is perfectly manifest in Your Divine Grace.

Being empowered by you to reflect a fraction of your transcendental qualities, we will be able to bring countless souls to the shelter of your lotus feet.

*Begging your mercy, we remain your servants of Durban temple,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Flushing

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your ISKCON movement, which you started on this planet. We know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you have entered into Kṛṣṇa's *nitya-līlā* in Goloka-dhāma. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are always interested in the ISKCON movement you started in New York for the purpose of lifting up us fallen conditioned souls who were rotting in the cycle of birth and death. We all know what a tremendous struggle you went through in the early days of the ISKCON movement. You have given your disciples all the knowledge through your books. You gave clear instructions that to become self-realized they need not read any other book or go to any other personality for higher understanding of spiritual truth. To be a disciple means to be disciplined—we have to follow, as it is, whatever you have said, without applying our material brain to adjust things according to our own whims. When Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī asked Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu why He was dancing and chanting like a madman, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied that His spiritual master had ordered Him to just chant, because He was fool number one. Similarly, we should also think ourselves fool number one before you, and we should abide by the orders that you have given us.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the courage to preach your glories through this continent. Your glories are unfathomable. Indeed, you are a living *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* or the living essence of all Vedic literatures. By reading your biography, any stone-hearted person will melt. You had so much faith in Lord Kṛṣṇa that you risked your life for our sakes, and you delivered this message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to spiritually bankrupt people like us. Many people do not believe in God, but when they read your biography they will realize that if there was no God, then why would you have taken so much risk on His behalf? And you were successful beyond imagination. You have shown to people in general that God is not dead, and if they surrender unto Him they can also see the miracle, as you have seen in creating this worldwide ISKCON movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we just have to surrender to your desires and execute our duties in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Having you and Lord Kṛṣṇa in our minds, in this way our success will be guaranteed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, some people say that anyone could have done what you did—simply follow in disciplic succession. But this was no simple task. You made so many personal sacrifices for us fallen souls. Who on this planet, at that time in 1965, could have come out of India with no money, no friends, and could have planted the seed of devotion in the hearts of meat-eaters, and women-hunters? No one but you could have done this, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This was your miracle.

*We wish to remain the unworthy servants of your servant,*

Raghunātha dāsa, Bhavatāriṇī-devī dāsī / Rati Lal Patel & family

## Fullerton

*My dear and most beloved eternal guide and friend, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I AM WRITING TO YOU the letter I failed to send during your most merciful presence here with us in this dark corner of Lord Kṛṣṇa's eternal kingdom. I often thought of writing to you during those years before your departure, but somehow I never did. And now many years have passed, and still I have not written you that letter until this very moment. But I don't mind not having written, because I understand why I was unable to before, because I hardly knew you at all. I didn't really know what to say other than to thank you for coming into my life to save me. And it is not that now I think I should write because I have come to know you so well. But rather, now I am writing because I want to know you more than anything else in the world.

I have some small understanding beginning to solidify into something maybe resembling a realization that the only purpose of my existence is to know and love you. Today I have experienced the most vivid glimpse of what you have in store for me if I can only give you my heart. I want to give it to you, but I am rather ashamed to, because how can I think of offering to you such a filthy thing? It pains me to think of placing it at your divine lotus feet, but it also pains me not to. Actually, there is no choice.

I must be brave enough to place my heart before you, even if painful, for you to cast your merciful glance upon. I have, to some extent, I feel, given it to you before throughout these past twelve years, but it was only partially given. Or if more or less fully given, I quickly took it back and shared it with others, elsewhere. But today I have been encouraged by your most merciful self to consider giving it fully, never to take it back.

I feel that today I have taken a giant step forward (at least, giant for a midget like me) towards actually surrendering myself to you as your instrument. I always wanted to, but maybe I didn't know exactly how or wasn't yet ready or just wasn't allowed to, for some unknown reason. But I'm writing to you now to ask you to so kindly help me do so. I think I'm ready.

I want to give my entire being to you for rectification and training. I want you to do with me as you see fit. What do I know about what is good for me anyway? Father knows best. I'm such a foolish son, so arrogant. So I'm asking you to please accept me in my present condition. I don't expect you to say no. You are the mercy incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa, so how can you say no? Because you are eternally free from any desire for selfish enjoyment you can easily be merciful, even to an ignorant fool like me.

In giving my heart to you, I know there will be some pain in the treatment you will have to use in order to clean it so you can offer it to your Lord Kṛṣṇa. When you said that our hearts were just like pieces of coal, which can be repeatedly washed but remain forever black, you must have been, at that very moment, glancing at mine. But I have hope. I have some growing faith—faith in you as my eternal well-wishing friend and guide. I trust that you will not stop helping me wash my heart until finally, one day, one year, one millennium, sometime or other, the pitch-black crust will be worn away, uncovering my true self, my eternal self, my spiritual self.

Although only a tiny, insignificant spark of spirit, my true self has meaning and purpose because it is part of Kṛṣṇa. If it were somehow not part of Kṛṣṇa (although that is not possible), its existence would indeed be meaningless. But since Kṛṣṇa is all that be in all directions, at all times, I know I have meaning and purpose as a fragment of Him, the almighty.

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, with all these probably foolish words, what I really wanted to say I have just suddenly realized is extremely simple. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to love you, and by your mercy I feel I am starting to be able to know how. Please keep teaching me, please keep helping me, please keep allowing me. As Lord Kṛṣṇa's purest devotee I fully trust that you will. I need you to love. I want you to love. Somehow, as the most fortunate of all the myriads of conditioned souls, somehow I have found you to love.

(Written by Haripāda dāsa)

*Your greatly fallen and desperate but hopeful disciples,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Haripāda dāsa / Phalinī-devī dāsī

## Gītā-nāgarī

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this glorious occasion of your appearance day celebration, we, the residents of the Gītā-nagari farm community, would like to offer our heartfelt gratitude to you for the many merciful gifts that you are constantly bestowing upon us. As your disciples, granddisciples, and aspiring *bhaktas,* we are praying to you for the conviction and determination to fulfill your plans and desires for this Gītā-nāgarī village.

You wanted us to live like Kṛṣṇa lived—simple village life: taking care of the cows and producing our own food grains, vegetables, and fruits. You said that a simple village life, devoid of the modern *ugra-karma* civilization, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness go well together. You told us, "Live village life, simple life, and be satisfied with the bare necessities. There is no need of luxury. And save time and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. This is ideal life." To this end we are aiming our activities at Gītā-nāgarī.

Whether one is gazing upon Gītā-nāgarī for the first time or has seen it many times or is constantly taking in it's beauty and charm, this picturesque Gītā-nāgarī is formed of your mercy. It is by your mercy that the cows are joyfully awaiting the luscious green grasses of the pasturing grounds—so much so that they are kicking their heels and raising dust on the way in anticipation. It is through your mercy that the ox teams eagerly hitch up to the plow to groom the earth with their labor. Your mercy stimulates the fruit trees to blossom forth with their array of flower clusters, which fill the air with scent and attract the honeybees by the hundreds to fill their combs with nectar. And that same mercy will set the fruit of those trees to hang in abundance, waiting to fill our storehouse. By your mercy the seas of wheat and oats will turn to their golden hue and ripple under midsummer breezes, beckoning us to harvest their stock of wealth. One will find your mercy in the abundant variety of vegetables and flowers that grace this *dhāma* with their attractive colors, forms, and aromas. All of these are constantly reminding us of the beauty of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your mercy flows in that liquid religiosity that fattens your prize-winning and world-famous cows' milk bags. That special mercy is eagerly taken by the devotees in the form of milk, butter, yogurt, cheese, burfi, etc.—the different forms being too numerous to count. That mercy, as taught by you, is also being distributed outside Gītā-nāgarī village for the benefit of others.

Your mercy has rained upon us the nourishing association of our Godbrothers and Godsisters. It is that association that helps create the favorable atmosphere necessary for rendering pure devotional service as you have taught throughout your instructions. It is your benevolence that has engaged us in these occupational duties of tending Kṛṣṇa's cows and Kṛṣṇa's land. The value of this simple village life can't be measured by material standards, which are governed by greed and lust. But, by the higher standard of fulfilling the purpose of this human form of life, you have guided us toward the perfectional platform of love of God. By these services to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara we are daily feeling the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this simple village setting.

This village is your village. You are the founder-*ācārya*. You are the constant director and guide. You are the ever well-wisher. It is your pleasure that brings us pleasure. Therefore, we pray that our continued efforts to fulfill your desires will be fully satisfying to you.

(Written by Prabhānu dāsa)

*Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Gothenburg

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our eternal father and grandfather,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Obedience is the first law of discipline. "Disciple" means that one accepts the discipline of the teacher, and that implies obedience to his instructions. You perfectly obeyed your Guru Mahārāja's order. Thus on his order you saved us fallen souls. Your example is in itself a lesson for us all. Thus you taught by your example and by your words, and even more wonderful is how you still teach us today. Therefore we are all eternally indebted to you, and on this auspicious day we all pay our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the savior of the whole world, and we are eternally your disciples and granddisciples. Therefore on this sacred day we pray that we may, in the same way that you obeyed your Guru Mahārāja's order, accept the discipline of being your followers and obey your transcendental instructions, thus continuously helping to save the fallen souls.

We beg to remain your eternal servants. We are made of your mercy.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

**Granddisciples**

Svarūpa dāsa / Bhakta Olle / Bhakta Magnus / Mahālakṣmī-devī-dāsī / Aprameyā-devī-dāsī / Bhaktin Christina

## Guadalajara

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

All glories to Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *saṅkīrtana* movement, of which you are the general.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.17.32) it is stated:

*agnau gurāv ātmani ca*  
*sarva-bhūteṣu māṁ param*  
*apṛthag-dhīr upasīta*  
*brahma-varcasvy akalmaṣaḥ*

**"Thus enlightened in Vedic knowledge by service to the spiritual master, freed from all sins and duality, one should worship Me as the Supersoul, as I appear within fire, the spiritual master, one's own self, and all living entities."**

The service to the spiritual master must be absolutely pure and bereft of all material desire. It is like the service unto the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The basic principle is surrender to the bona fide spiritual master, and by this method we will bring a stop to our sinful lives. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the bearer of the light of Vedic knowledge, giving us the transcendental teachings of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and in this way you awake in us real human intelligence.

Your perfect behavior was always guided by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself. Now you have left us your G.B.C. to continue your grandiose work, and also you have left *ācāryas* for us to follow. Such authorized *ācāryas* are exemplary persons and perform your order with love and devotion, bereft of dualities and free of sinful activities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us continue with full faith, with strong determination, your great *saṅkīrtana* movement, and in this way aspire to get your holy association.

*Your faithful servants of New Nīlācala Mandira,*

**Granddisciples**

## Gujarat

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our millions of prostrations at your lotus feet on your ninety-first appearance day. Who would have thought on that day that it was the most significant event in the history of mankind? Simply by the transcendental *yogamāyā* of Kṛṣṇa you appeared, unnoticed, in such a humble way. And it was also by the same arrangement of *yogamāyā* that after completing the most impossible task of transforming thousands of *mlecchas* and *yavanas*, after transplanting the almost lost Vedic culture into the heart of the modern Western world, after firmly establishing the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you passed from this material world in the same humble way, practically unnoticed by most of the world. Of course, the intelligent man can now see that the indications were there from your very birth. You appeared on the next day after Janmāṣṭamī, your mission to broadcast the glories of Kṛṣṇa's divine appearance and activities.

As a matter of fact, there are so many indications throughout your life that you were a unique person. Indeed, an astrologer predicted such an unusual, extraordinary thing. "He will build a house in which the whole world can live." Another astrologer said, *"R*ājā *baneg*ā. \[He will become a king\]." You also said, "Somehow or other, in my circle of friends, everyone accepted me as a leader. If I certified something as good, they would accept it. So in the same way my friends wanted to check if this saintly person Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was approved by me or not. In this way, I first met my Guru Mahārāja with a friend, and immediately I knew he was a genuine saint, and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission was safe in his hands." Well, your spiritual master also recognized you immediately. He knew you were the future torch-bearer. He even told his *brahmacārīs,* "Don't disturb Abhay Babu. When he joins, he will do wonderful things."

History will record how you have done wonderful things, even though today foolish Kali-yuga masses are ignorant of your actual strength and contribution. Like Lord Varāha, who appeared from the nostrils of Brahmā and soon became as big as a mountain, your name and your mission have appeared slowly, in a small way. But soon they will cover the entire earth. You are a pure devotee, and you are the greatest dreamer. You have impossible dreams, and yet the Lord is pleased with you; He grants you inconceivable potency to fulfill them.

This auspicious day, our lord, brings back the sweet memory of those few years that we spent in your company. It puts us in ecstasy to remember how you charmed our snakelike hearts, how you made each one of us feel that you had been his friend for millions of lifetimes. How amazing, because you are a *paramahaṁsa* who has renounced everything, even your own family and children! You are completely detached from everything except the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kṛṣṇa, and yet you accepted us as your children. And it is equally amazing how even though we were born as the lowest, sinful demons, at odds even with our own parents, we became just like a tamed dog, a pet animal, in front of you.

We can never forget those wonderful days, those cherished moments in which you became our master and accepted us as your servants. It is so difficult to lead this life without you. It was so simple to be a servant of a perfect master. There was no envy, no confusion—simply causeless humble service. Even wearing striped shirts, yellow dhotis, and tennis shoes, looking as though we came from outer space, we were ecstatic to do street *saṅkīrtana,* because we were constantly hearing, "Śrīla Prabhupāda said this," "Śrīla Prabhupāda said that," "Prabhupāda is there," "He is coming!!" Indeed, you were our life and soul, and just hearing of you kept us in the spiritual world.

But now we have to assume different roles. Now, in your absence, there is a lot of confusion. We hoped we would learn how to be lower than the straw in the street. We would learn to chant the holy name without offenses. But we are still struggling to be disciples. We are still lost somewhere on the shore of the *vaidhī bhakti* ocean. We have been rowing and rowing for so long, but it seems in vain, because we are still anchored to hundreds of *anarthas.* You are ever available in your *vāṇī* instruction form, but our prejudices and preferences sometimes outweigh your divine instructions.

We remember your own prophetic words from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.28.48): "The *ācārya,* the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, establishes these principles, but when he disappears, things once again become disordered. The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master. At the present moment, practically the entire world is afraid of rogues and nondevotees; therefore this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is started to save the world from irreligious principles. Everyone should cooperate with this movement in order to bring about actual peace and happiness in the world." Again, in the next verse you say, "It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly Otherwise the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations."

Our dearest lord, you are a "Vaikuṇṭha man"—an ambassador of Rādhārāṇī. Only you can purify us. You have given us the best of everything—Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, the great *ācāryas,* the best scriptures, the best *mahā-mantra,* and the best spiritual master:

Your Divine Grace. And yet *māyā* is constantly attacking and taking so many casualties. This turbulent ocean of the material world is so hard to overcome. It constantly overpowers us with the waves of pride, envy, hatred, fault-finding, and sense gratification. You are the most expert captain, who designed a wonderful ship in the form of ISK.CON to pull the whole world through this ocean, and you trained so many captains to take charge of this ship. But obviously, we are not such good captains as we thought, and this only ship in the insurmountable ocean is shaking very heavily, sometimes giving an impression it might sink. So on this auspicious day we pray very hard at your lotus feet for you to intervene and save your ship. Please give us strength and purity, so that we may sacrifice our personal ambitions on the altar of your desires. The biggest contribution we can make in this crisis is that we become pure, unalloyed devotees and stick to your mission. Please drive out all the weeds of the enjoying spirit, gross and subtle, from our hearts. Please keep us as dust particles at your lotus feet, life after life.

*Your humble servants in ISK.CON Gujarat,*

## Guyana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Although we are ardently desiring to submit, for your pleasure, an homage consisting of the most chosen words, composed in the best poetry, befitting your exalted position, we find it difficult to do so, mainly because we are not fully surrendered to your lotus feet. Our steel-framed hearts are full of selfish desires for fame, profit, and adoration, and we are always chasing after the illusory pleasures that this temporary material body can give. Nevertheless, we are constantly praying to your lotus feet that we can at least be allowed to remain under the shelter of your ISKCON movement and be fully engaged in assisting those pure-hearted and loyal disciples of yours who sincerely want to continue preaching about your glories and expanding your mission to every corner of this country.

Indeed, you have arranged everything so perfectly that there is no lack of anything within our movement to keep us fully satisfied and happy, and it is only due to our lack of surrender and dedication that there appear to be difficulties. It is not within our ability to appreciate fully the books. Before your message reached this part of the world, many of us had heard of Lord Kṛṣṇa, coming as we did from Hindu backgrounds, but we never had even the least idea of what pure devotional service was or how easily Lord Caitanya had made love of Godhead available through the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. By your divine grace we became enlightened, and we can only show our gratitude to you by steadfastly holding on to your divine instructions and assisting in spreading Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's mission to every town and village.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your dearmost desire is that we work cooperatively among ourselves, giving up our tendencies for faultfinding. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we are fervently praying for your blessings to remain always engaged in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and constantly endeavoring to preach your glories.

(Written by Bhūtādi dāsa)

*Your eternal servants from Guyana,*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest personality of this day and age. You are the true friend of everyone because you give everyone Kṛṣṇa. You are the shelter of all the devotees. ## Hawaii

**The Significance of Vyāsa Pūjā**

TO UNDERSTAND the significance of Vyāsa-pūjā is to understand life. Not this euphemistic day-to-day drudgery, this constant battle against the indefatigable illusory energy, which, expert trickster, always promises but never delivers, leading everyone on, dangling the carrot just out of reach. No, this is not the life we mean. There is more to it than the eating, sleeping, mating, and the myriad activities humanity pointlessly pursues in the vain bid to assuage its original sin: that of identifying the self with the body. Real life begins with understanding the reality of our situation:

*rsabha uvaca*  
*nāyaṁ dehoh deha-bhājāṁ nṛloke*  
*kāṣṭān kāmān arhate vid-bhujāṁ ye*  
*tapo divyaṁ putrakā yena sattvaṁ*  
*śuddhyed yasmād brahma-saukhyaṁ tv anantam*

**"Lord Ṛṣabhadeva told His sons: 'My dear boys, of all the living entities who have accepted material bodies in this world, one who has been awarded the human form should not work hard day and night for sense gratification, which is available even for dogs and hogs that eat stool. One should engage in penance and austerity to attain the divine position of devotional service. By such activity one's heart is purified, and when one attains this position, he attains eternal, blissful life, which is transcendental to material happiness and which continues forever.' "** ***(Bhāg.*** **5.5.1)**

In this verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Lord Ṛṣabhadeva instructs His sons on the importance of human life. A living entity who has been awarded a human form should act differently from the animals. After undergoing severe hardships all day, human beings try to enjoy themselves at night through eating, drinking, having sex, and sleeping. At the same time they try to properly defend themselves. However, this is not human civilization. At best it is polished animal civilization. Human life is meant for the voluntary practice of austerity for spiritual advancement. By *tapasya* one can get out of the material clutches. When one is situated in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, devotional service, his happiness is guaranteed eternally. After all, every living entity is trying to enjoy happiness, but as long as one is encaged in the material body, one has to undergo different kinds of misery. A higher sense is present in order to attain divine life and eternal happiness by going back home where one belongs—back to Godhead.

Knowledge of spiritual matters, which are eternal and beyond this temporary material manifestation, is called *acintya,* that which is beyond our power of conception. It is useless to argue or speculate on what is inconceivable. If it is truly inconceivable, it is not subject to speculation or experimentation. Our energy is limited and our sense perception is limited; therefore we must rely on Vedic conclusions regarding that subject matter which is inconceivable, just as one must rely .on the mother for information on the identity of the father; there is no other way. Consequently the *Vedas* are known as *śruti,* mother, and because He is the originator of the *Vedas,* Kṛṣṇa is the father. *Vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham:* "By all the *Vedas* I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of *Vedānta,* and I am the knower of the *Vedas."* By the phrase *vedānta-kṛt,* spoken in *Bhagavad-gītā,* Śrī Kṛṣṇa claims to have compiled the *Vedānta;* but everyone knows that the actual author is Śrīla Vyāsadeva. Kṛṣṇa makes this claim because Vyāsadeva is His empowered incarnation who has come to this material world to present the Vedic knowledge in a written form. Strictly speaking, Vedavyāsa is Kṛṣṇa's literary incarnation.

We are greatly indebted to Vyāsadeva because by his mercy we are able to obtain absolute understanding of spiritual life. Indeed, without him we could not begin to conceive of our actual position and would be doomed to wander in this material world, gyrating helplessly from body to body, sometimes stepping high in the heavenly planets as demigods, then paying the awesome price of falling to the lowest life forms and being pitilessly, cruelly forced to inch our way up the eight-million-bodies-long evolutionary scale, fighting for our very life, in constant fear, hunger, and anxiety, to again arrive at the human form. How many lives? How many years? Can we stop dreaming this dream of uniform illusion and face the facts? Can we not see the ignominy, the abysmal absurdity, the abject terror of a myriad births, a myriad deaths? Can we estimate the value of a human form with its unique facility for eternal life?

Yes, we can; but only by the mercy of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. He has given us the only effective weapon against *māyā,* the illusory energy—the weapon of *yoga.* By *yoga* we can bring the powerful mind and senses under control. By becoming free from the dictation of the mind we can come to the platform of reality. Just like when we are driving in a motorcar: We may be traveling at high speed on the motorway, and as long as the car is under control there are no problems, we have full faculties to think, converse, listen to the radio, etc. But suppose there is some mechanical failure, perhaps a tire blows out, the car spins out of control. Immediately there is panic! No longer can we think rationally. In anxiety and fear we can only await the inevitable crash. We have no power to act independently of the unfolding sequence of traumatic events. Similarly, we are situated inside our body just like a passenger in a car. The body is made up of the mind and senses, and if the mind and senses are out of control, we become helpless spectators in this unfolding drama called conditioned life. We have no power to stop and think of our real identity and situation. Only by bringing the mind and senses under control through the practice of *yoga do* we have the opportunity to think rationally and factually, just as it is possible to act and think rationally and factually in a car only when it is under control.

*Vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu śivadam:* "The highest truth is reality distinguished from illusion for the welfare of all." *(Bhāg.* 1.1.2) Physician heal thyself; there is no possibility of welfare of all if we cannot even understand what is the welfare of the individual. Understanding who we really are is the first step: *ahaṁ brahmāsmi,* I am spirit, and I have spiritual activities which link me to the Supreme Spirit, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The linking process is called *yoga,* and it is coming from Śrīla Vyāsadeva down to the present day. The process of receiving this knowledge which spans thousands of years is very practical. It is just like picking mangoes from a tree. The mango trees are very high, and the mango, king of fruit, is very delicate. One cannot carelessly throw the fruits down to the ground; that would be disastrous. Rather, from the highest branch a laborer picks the fruit and hands it down to another laborer sitting on the branch below, who in his turn hands the mango to a third laborer sitting on the branch below that. In this way, hand to hand, carefully, the fruit reaches the ground unblemished. That too is the way that the transcendental knowledge compiled by Śrīla Vyāsadeva reaches us; not suddenly or haphazardly, but very carefully handed down, without change, from Vyāsadeva to his disciple Madhvācārya, from Madhvācārya to his disciple Padmanābha Tīrtha, from Padmanābha to his disciple Narahari Tīrtha, and so on, from *guru* to disciple, in an unbroken chain of disciplic succession called *guru-paramparā.*

*Evaṁ paramparā-prāptam imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ:* "This supreme science was thus received through the chain of disciplic succession, and the saintly kings understood it in that way." (Bg. 4.2)

Today is the appearance day of our spiritual master, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aśtottaraśata Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. On this day we are given the opportunity to worship him as the perfect representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the thirty-third link of the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya Sampradāya, an eternal associate of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and the direct manifestation of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy; and his disciples are it's living proof, because who but the most unqualified, upon receiving loving service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, the *summum bonum* of achievements, could demonstrate Śrīla Prabhupāda's uncommon compassion? *Yogyata vicāre kichu nai pai tomāra karuṇā sār:* "If you examine us, we have no qualities; your mercy is all we're made of."

We, the residents of ISKCON Hawaii, pray to his one and only Divine Grace to continue to nurture the life he awakened in our hearts during his manifest presence, and to continue directing us, so that we may be instrumental in awakening to spiritual consciousness the millions of visitors to these islands, which were so dear to him. We are his surrendered servants and pray to him and to the Vaiṣṇavas for this opportunity, free from mundane considerations, politics, material desires, material poverty, illusion, lamentation, anxiety, fear, anger, attachment, and unnecessary hard work.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya*!

(Written by Ānanda-svarūpa Swami)

## Heidelberg

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. We are humbly submitting this offering for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace in the hope that we may be purified through association with you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day. We thus may be blessed with a fuller appreciation of your transcendental qualities. Our only hope for deliverance from the jaws of birth and death, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that we may somehow or other earn your kind and merciful glance upon our wretched selves. So we pray that we may not commit any offense in the performance of this solemn task of relating something of our very limited understanding of your unfathomable glories.

Your position as founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness was defined historically by the wondrous accomplishment of your establishing over one hundred Hare Kṛṣṇa temples around the world during the last twelve years of your activities on this planet. It was also defined legally in 1974 with the declaration that ". . . His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the *founder-ācārya* of ISKCON \[and\] the supreme authority in all matters of the Society. His position cannot be occupied by anyone else . . . ." Our prayer today, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that your position as *founder-ācārya* of ISKCON be finally established within our, the members', hearts. Only then will this movement be safe from the ravages of schismatic deviations that have rent asunder the mundane religions of the Kali-yuga, for only you can drive the Kali-yuga out of the hearts of all mankind and instill real peace there instead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you certainly desire that ISKCON be expanded unlimitedly by your followers. That, however, will only be possible if we build upon the foundation you have laid. The foundation of this movement is completely spiritual. *Golokera prema-dhana hari-nāma saṅkīrtana.* You stated, "We shall have our ISKCON in the spiritual world." It is not, as *māyāvādīs* might imagine, that this great movement you have begun is a material means to a spiritual end. Rather, you have established an embassy of Vaikuṇṭha within the mundane sphere. This transcendental fact may only be realized by those who are pure in heart. Only they may expand what you have established, for only they know what you have established. Others perceive but a shadow ISKCON, project their desires and frustrations upon it, and conclude that it is corrupt. But actually it is *their* hearts that are corrupt.

So to become pure in heart, we must establish you as the founder-*ācārya* of ISK.CON within our hearts, and that means we must follow your example and instructions strictly: "You should never act independently, because guidance in my words is always there. The main guidance is all of us should remain spiritually strong by chanting the minimum number of rounds and following the rules and regulations. The G.B.C. should personally observe strictly all the rules and regulations, and they should become the example to others. Then everything will be all right. Then there will be no fear of being victimized by *māyā."*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may eternally remain engaged in service to your lotus feet by taking shelter of the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇavas who have taken shelter there. We hope that in this way we may become qualified to relish completely the transcendental society you have established, and thus become blessed with pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Your servants in ISKCON Heidelberg,*
