## New Tālavana, Mississippi

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I have been thinking of what I have to offer you on your sacred appearance day. I have my body—wasted and useless from many years of sense gratification. My intelligence: almost none—same reason. My mind: certainly an unsuitable present to a common person, what to speak of an exalted soul like yourself. My wealth: hardly any, because of the intelligence problem. My words: same as above.

So all I really have to offer is my desire, and that desire is that you take all the above-mentioned items, purify them by your Midas touch, and somehow use them according to your desire.

Give us your mercy—the goal of our lives,  
The source of our determination, which constantly thrives  
At the lotus feet of the servants of you,  
Pointing the way—guiding us through  
The miseries of Kali and his frightening pit,  
Cleaning our hearts, making us fit  
To associate with the lord of all souls,  
The purpose of life, the goal of all goals.  
You have awakened us now from our powerful sleep—  
wallowers in ignorance, creatures of the deep.  
Though the urge is still there to stay in this mess,  
The suffering is minimized if we follow your process.  
It is only when we stray from your plan  
That māyā can make us her servants again,  
So give us your mercy—we are only your tools  
Trying to be something other than fools.  
To please you is what our lives are about.  
All sinful desires—we are kicking them out.  
So we beg for your mercy again and again;  
Without it we know there is no way we can win.

(Written by Puṇḍarīkākṣa dāsa)

Your servants at New Tālavana, Mississippi.

## New Vrindaban

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

YOU ARE an ocean of mercy, and we are so weak and fallen due to many lifetimes dominated by *kāma-krodha-lobha—*lust, anger, and greed—which prevent us from seeing things as they truly are. It is our desire to enjoy separate from Kṛṣṇa that keeps us in ignorance, earthbound by the three *guṇas* to the cycle of birth and death, and unaware that our dearest friend and master is always waiting for us to turn to Him.

O ever well-wisher, twenty years have passed since you first graced this Western world, descending to the depths of New York City, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, clashing cymbals, and pounding a drum to remind us that the Lord is present in the sound of His holy names, in His instructions of *Bhagavad-gltd,* in the hearts of all as Paramatma, in the all-pervasive Brahman effulgence, in the person of Your Divine Grace, and in Kṛṣṇa-loka. His eternal abode beyond this material manifestation. Still, we are so hard-hearted that we have no taste for your service, nor the sounds of the holy names, nor the transcendental truths revealed in your books.

Obstinately we return to our cruel taskmaster, Māyādevī, and reap the bitter fruits of material suffering. Although Lord Kṛṣṇa tells us that a man who delights in the senses lives in vain, we return again and again to the lures of Māyādevī and thus remain exiled from our eternal home.

We humbly plead, O great father: Save your fallen children. Kindly continue to encourage us to turn from darkness to light, from ignorance to truth, from Māyādevī's service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa's. How vain are all mundane pursuits! Out of habit, we chew the chewed on the path of death, and though we experience only bitterness, we learn nothing, and so set the wheel of *karma* in motion again and again.

O ever-loving father, now, in these difficult times, please, out of your infinite mercy, help us purify our senses by lifting us to the platform of your service and to that of your devotees following in the disciplic succession. Teach us humility that we may avoid the dictates of false ego: "I am the doer, and this is mine." Teach us, from your books and from within our hearts, to utilize everything in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa and to see Him as the only doer and proprietor.

Please forgive our countless transgressions at your lotus feet, offenses committed in the blindness of lust, anger, and greed, the three gates to hell. O spiritual father, seeing us helpless in the grips of Māyā, kindly have compassion on us and release those bonds. O ever well-wisher, turn our night into the bright day of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by granting us the strength to surrender to you during this very lifetime, to control the senses by keeping the rules, to chant the holy names, to attend *maṅgala-ārati,* to render some positive service, and to work together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world.

You have given us a matchless gift, a pearl of great price: pure love of Kṛṣṇa in devotional service. Grant us the spiritual determination to sell all that we have and purchase it. "What you give to Kṛṣṇa, you receive back a thousand times over." Please let us not remain misers, or cling to old ways, or hanker or procrastinate. Even now we can hear the hoof-beats of Death's horses riding inexorably toward us, moment by moment, second by second. Dare we turn from Lord Kṛṣṇa in the face of Death? Before the y*amadūtas,* dare we turn to sense gratification, like the man on his deathbed turning to admire a young nurse? What fools we mortals are!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, have mercy on us weak, conditioned souls. Dispel our ignorance with the torchlight of real knowledge, and enable us to understand your essential purports, to reject mundane knowledge as a waste of time, and to keep the truth before us constantly: "But what need is there, Arjuna, for all this detailed knowledge? With a single fragment of Myself, I pervade and support this entire universe."

O answerer of all! With all our hearts we thank you for sparing us your dear son Kīrtanānanda Mahārāja a little longer, so that he may continue to lead us back to you by following in your footsteps. Grant us the strength also to follow, to burn the rubbish of our past *karma* while there is still time, and increase our desire to get to Him, the eternal Lord of our heart, and thus perfect our human lives and live with you and all pure Vaiṣṇavas eternally in Śrī Kṛṣṇa's abode, Goloka Vṛndāvana. That is our heart's true desire.

*The inmates of New Vrindaban,*

## New York

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble offering at your lotus feet. By your causeless mercy we have been lifted out of the ocean of Kali-yuga and placed on the lifeboat of devotional service. We were all drowning in material illusion, but on the order of Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you came to America and saved us from *māyā’s* clutches.

Traveling from India at the advanced age of seventy, you placed your lotus feet on Western soil, and all auspiciousness followed. Starting with very little material facility, but with an abundance of faith in the order of your spiritual master, you founded ISKCON in New York City in 1966 and organized the first *saṅkīrtana* party in the Western world. The chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* in Tompkins Square Park marked the beginning of an inundation of love of God that was destined to spread all over the world. Although the Western countries were full of impersonalism and voidism, you were able to transplant the spiritual atmosphere of Vṛndāvana and Māyāpur and introduce real spiritual life in a land steeped in passion and ignorance.

As the foundling ISKCON gradually expanded and increased under your direction, you unsheathed the weapon of transcendental knowledge—*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, The Nectar of Devotion, Kṛṣṇa* book, *The Teachings of Lord Caitanya, Easy Journey to Other Planets—*to share with us your personal ecstasy Then you blessed us with your translation of the ripened fruit of Vedic wisdom, the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* explaining the highest transcendental subject matter of pure love of God. These books will guide mankind for the next ten thousand years. No one can adequately describe the glories of your preaching work. Motivated only by pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa, you opened more than one hundred temples, published more than eighty volumes of literature, initiated and trained thousands of disciples, and installed the worshipable form of the Lord on six continents.

As your disciples and granddisciples, we pray to you for inspiration to carry on the great preaching mission that you worked so hard to establish. As we celebrate the five-hundredth anniversary of the appearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we throw ourselves at your lotus feet, praying for the intelligence and determination that we will require to push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

By Kṛṣṇa's arrangement we have the opportunity to serve you in the most important city in the world, New York, which is populated by fifteen million conditioned souls—an unlimited opportunity for service to Your Divine Grace. We thank you for this chance to solidify our relationship with you by making New York Kṛṣṇa conscious.

We can never repay our debt to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you have given us freedom from birth and death, forcing our eyes open with the torchlight of knowledge. Since you have physically departed this planet, our only hope for success is to cling to your instructions as our very life and soul. Our fervent prayer is that you kindly pour down your rain of mercy to extinguish the forest fire of material existence that is blazing within our contaminated hearts. Please allow us to serve you birth after birth, otherwise our lives will become meaningless.

(Written by Locanānanda dāsa)

*Your ever-grateful servants at the Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*

## North Carolina

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

During this year we have been realizing more and more the necessity of associating constantly with you. Of course, because you have left the planet, that association must be with your *vāṇī,* or instructions. Mercifully, you have left us with an ocean of instructions in your books, letters, and tapes. We can now understand why you were working so intently to publish as much as possible in as little time as possible. There is no gap or missing section in your *vāṇī* that leads us to search elsewhere.

Your *vāṇī* is wonderful. Simply by associating with you in this way we remain enthusiastic, blissful, and steady in devotional service. Any spiritual problem can be cured by the cure-all of your association, and any spiritual problem can be diagnosed as coming from a lack of your association. Our success in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is dependent upon how much of your association we give the conditioned souls.

We should not be like the rich man's son described in your *Teachings of Lord Caitanya.* This son was suffering in his poverty due to his ignorance of the treasure bequeathed to him by his father. His astrologer, Sarvajña, had to advise him where to dig to find his treasure. If he dug in the wrong place he would be attacked by either a poisonous wasp, an evil spirit, or other dangers. We have no excuse, though, if we are in a poverty-stricken condition and are digging for our wealth in the wrong place, because we don't need an astrologer to tell us where the wealth is. It is not even hidden. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, tell us where the wealth is. You have many times stated that this wealth is in your books (and other *vāṇī* expansions: tapes, letters, etc.).

We are rich. Let us take the treasure which you have bequeathed to us and become situated properly in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness (which you have also bequeathed to us). And let us flood the whole world with this treasure, which increases in depth and quantity the more we distribute it!

What will we do now?  
*Where to go and how?*

Now that our lord has left,  
Clearly we are bereft.

But surely he must have left us something,  
With his clear knowledge of everything

Connected with the Supreme Lord, who  
Knows past, present, and future, too.

But where is that hidden treasure?  
Is it in position, or is it in pleasure?

"No, "says the sādhu who knows Prabhupāda well.  
"These ephemeral things bring you to hell."

That treasure is Prabhupāda, who will never leave  
You, his faithful disciple, to grieve.

His books, letters, tapes, and writing—  
Dive into the ocean of nectarine.

Your servants at New Gokula,

## Nova Gokula, Brazil

*My dear Gurudeva,*

PLEASE accept my prostrated obeisances at your eternally merciful lotus feet. All glories to your spiritual society and all your spiritual children and future generations yet to come. All glories to whoever surrenders to your divine mission.

When I joined here in Brazil, my temple life consisted of daily *hari-nāmas* and book distribution—a very simple formula for peaceful existence, I thought. *Prasādam* was also very prominent—all those transcendental recipes would drive me crazy in the ecstasy of serving the Lord, even while eating His remnants. As you can see, my Gurudeva, I was so blissful that I could never imagine that these things would ever stop. And of course they have not stopped. There are still chanting parties going out every day, a lot of book distribution, nice *prasādam,* and wonderful association.

However, my Gurudeva, in your absence I have been acting like the mouse away from the cat. My fire is almost nil, and whenever I hear any inauspicious news, my mind collapses. After all, I do not think I will be able to make it without a lot of other nice *prabhus* around me, and I wish to see you come and give the "sauce" to whoever needs it. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am such a contaminated, useless fool that I can't even absorb my mind in your *līlāmṛta,* in your Bhaktivedanta purports, in good *japa* recitation, and in the glorification of your surrendered disciples! I am praying now at your lotus feet that your *vāṇī* association may surcharge my consciousness and that I may resume my service to you anywhere, without any personal motives. Only then will I see the splendor of your society shining through my heart and guiding me back to Godhead. Only then will I be by your side at every moment to serve you as you order me to do.

(Written by Mahākāla dāsa)  
*Your servants of Nova Gokula.*

## Oaxaca

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which are the only way to obtain prema-bhakti.

Uddhava asked Lord Kṛṣṇa why an enlightened person, just like a conditioned soul, engages in external bodily functions, and Kṛṣṇa answered, "An enlightened person fixed in detachment engages his body in lying down, sitting, walking, bathing, seeing, touching, smelling, eating, hearing, and so on, but is never entangled by such activities. Indeed, remaining as a witness to all bodily functions, he merely engages his bodily senses with their objects and does not become entangled like an unintelligent person." Here Kṛṣṇa explains how a pure devotee like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the most intelligent person, for he is never entangled by anything within this material world. You had a nice residence in every city of the world, a comfortable car, a lot of disciples; however, you had only a desire to preach, preach, preach, and preach.

Now we need your mercy, because you are an empowered devotee able to deliver all the three worlds.

Jagad-guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the unlimited experience of material objects only increases one's anxiety, whereas perception of the Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, immediately brings one to the platform of peace. Thus one who is intelligent takes shelter under your lotus feet and obtains the transcendental knowledge by which one can stop all his anxieties and miseries.

Your eternal servants of Oaxaca, Mexico,

## Penang

**Purity and Prabhupada**

**Two Synonymous Words**

You surpassed all your Godbrothers par excellence  
In serving the order of your own Guru Mahārāja,  
While they simply looked on and criticized at your expense.  
Such a vaiṣṇava-aparādha offense.  
O Prabhupāda, you assured us that we could all become as pure as you.  
Above suspicion, without sin,  
Pray we don't again become victim,  
For if we ourselves cannot follow,  
Then how will we have the power to instruct or pass judgment?  
We will simply meditate on how to save ourselves from falling down  
Instead of meditating on how to save the world.  
O Prabhupāda, only purity will sustain us all.  
Neither let dissension enter into the heart of your ISKCON camp,  
For you warned of that before your leaving.  
"Cooperate amongst one another—that will manifest your love for me."  
We cannot afford to have any lack of purity, now nor at any time.  
O Prabhupāda, to be purely Prabhupāda conscious must be our singular aim.  
So let us banish all else from our minds,  
Lest we are again consumed in the mad fire of material existence.  
On this day, hear our fervent prayer,  
O lord and master,  
For without satisfying you,  
We cannot even gain access to spiritual perfection.  
O Prabhupāda, never let us lose sight of your lotus feet.

Your eternal servitors in Penang State, Northern Malaysia.

## Perth

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances from all the devotees in Perth, Australia.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western world and allowing us to take part in Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement. You have taught us everything—how to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* how to dance the "swami step," how to worship the Deity of Kṛṣṇa, how to cook and offer *prasādam* to Kṛṣṇa and take the remnants, how to play *mṛdaṅga* and *karatālas,* even how to wash ourselves. We are eternally indebted to you for teaching us how to be devotees.

It is said that a devotee is one who reminds you of Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, in every way you are always reminding us of Kṛṣṇa, and therefore you are the greatest devotee. You have left us so many inspired books, we have recordings of beautiful songs and scholarly lectures, so many temples around the world, and so many advanced followers to associate with.

We therefore beg to remain eternally engaged in your preaching mission to deliver all the fallen souls back to Kṛṣṇa. You are Lord Caitanya's right-hand man, the greatest preacher and savior of the whole world.

Jagad-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

(Written by Puruṣottama dāsa)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine self.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Although very few of the devotees in Peru had direct personal association with you, still, as we surrender to your divine instructions and become servants of your servants, we experience your transcendental association. Our country lacks material wealth, and its people have many misconceptions about spiritual life, but somehow your simple and sublime process appeals to them, and therefore we have great hope of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness here nicely.

You have given us the example of a perfect loving servant of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees; indeed, you love everyone because you see only Lord Kṛṣṇa and His energies. Please help us to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness to serve your mission sincerely and thus awaken the fallen sleeping souls, so they can realize their ecstatic and loving Kṛṣṇa consciousness and thus be eternally happy. Please grant us the intelligence to increase our book distribution and other ways of distributing the culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and let us also become pure devotees by following your instructions carefully, thus becoming examples for others.

*Your servants in Peru,*

## Philadelphia

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

YOU SAID that the disciple has to pass the test of the spiritual master, and certainly you are testing us. Yet all our arduous trials turn out to be but different versions of the same problem: how to take shelter of you.

And that, really, has always been the issue, just as much in your physical presence as now in your apparent absence. It *seemed* so much easier when you were here, and now people blame all difficulties on your absence. "Our whole problem," they say, "is that Śrīla Prabhupāda's no longer here."

But when you were here, weren't there difficulties in our movement? How many initiated disciples left your service when you were here? Were there no falldowns, no fights? How can these be blamed on your absence?

The absence, rather, was our own: we did not go to you for shelter, even though shelter was there. Is that shelter now gone? Who says that merely because you are removed from mortal sight the shelter of your lotus feet is withdrawn?

So we cannot argue that there is some vast difference between then and now: Then and now, your refuge was fully available; then and now, not everyone took advantage of it.

You built for the ages; you prepared for us a kingdom of ten thousand years. You built everything firm and solid, made it to last. For a materialist, all is finished after a few years of "spot-life." Mere death was not going to remove you. And so you gave us clear instructions how to take shelter of you so that time and change and death will have no sway. You forged an eternal, unbreakable link between us.

You said, *vāṇī:* my words, my teachings, my instructions, my order. Associate with me through my *vāṇī.* Association through *vapuḥ—*physical presence—is nice. But the real thing is *vāṇī.*

"Hear my instruction," is your first instruction, but what can be done for those who do not hear *that"!*

You instruct us in two ways of association through *vāṇī.*

*Vāṇī* is your order, and we gain your intimate association by cherishing your order through complete obedience.Lord Caitanya taught this very method of association to the *brāhmaṇa* Kurma (Cc. *Madhya* 7.128-129):

**yāre deka, tāre kaha 'kṛṣṇa'-upadesa**  
**amāra ājñāya guru hana tara' ei deśa**  
**kabhu na vadhibe tomāra viśaya-taraṅga**  
**punar api ei thani pabe mora saṅga**

The Lord told the *brāhmaṇa* to instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* "In this way," the Lord said, "become *guru* under My order and deliver your country. If you obey this instruction," Lord Caitanya promised, "the waves of material life will never touch you. Indeed, by following My order, you will always have My association."

By cherishing your order, you taught us, we will always have your association and will never be bewildered because of not having your guidance. In this way, your movement can flourish without check.

You clearly explained the meaning of "under my order" to us in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.18.5, purport): A conditioned soul is hampered by four defects. He is sure to commit mistakes, he is sure to become illusioned, he has a tendency to cheat others, and his senses are imperfect. Consequently we have to take direction from liberated persons. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement directly receives instructions from the Supreme Personality of Godhead via persons who are strictly following His instructions. Although a follower may not be a liberated person, if he follows the supreme, liberated Personality of Godhead, his actions are naturally liberated from the contamination of the material nature. Lord Caitanya therefore says, "By My order you may become a spiritual master." One can immediately become a spiritual master by having full faith in the transcendental words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and by following His instructions.

Liberated or not liberated, if we take complete shelter of your instruction (which is no more, no less, than Kṛṣṇa's instruction), we are competent to deliver our country, and material nature will not touch us.

Taking shelter of your books is the second way you have given us to associate with you through *vāṇī.* This is special mercy, extra favor. To associate with you through your books, you said, is actually *better than* personal association. You wanted us to cherish your books by studying them with an alert and inquisitive mind. You indicated the importance of intensive study of your books by asking us to establish a system of progressive examinations on your literature, and graduates on a certain level would, you said, inherit the "family name of Bhaktivedanta" and thus become *gurus.*

History shows that, unfortunately, we did not much cherish this order in the past. Yet we can begin now to do so, understanding your purpose: that this order is meant to induce us to delve deeply and continuously into your books.

Those who have taken to heart this order to read report that vast revelations await us. They say that to read your books—to read them properly, actively, attending to your words with rapt attention and with a mind eager for instruction—is to receive, in reciprocation, direct, personal instruction from you. Serious readers report that the deeper one delves into your books, the more he discovers; that there are layers and layers of instructions, each more profound than the last. Your books are spiritual. They are alive. They are you.

We can still gain intimate association. We lamented, after your passing, that we did not take advantage of your presence, that we missed such a great opportunity. Yet, knowing how lame we are, you remained by your mercy in your books, so that even now we can learn more from you than we learned even while you were here. The only unforgivable thing is if we still do not take advantage of your presence. You still have much to teach us; your days are far from past. And to those who say that our problem is that you have gone, we can point out that there is a sense in which you have not yet fully come.

In July 1975 (as reported in *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* Vol. 6, page 97) this interchange took place in Berkeley, California:

Reporter: "What will happen to the movement in the United States when you die?"

Prabhupāda: "I will never die."

Devotees: "Jaya! Haribol!"

Prabhupāda: "I shall live from my books, and you will utilize."

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to you to bestow upon us the intelligence to utilize what you have so mercifully given us. Lead us to cherish your order and your books, not sentimentally, but really, rigorously following your order and daily reading your books, so that we may once again have your association in this place; or rather, so that we will never lose your company.

Your servants in Philadelphia beg for just one drop of your mercy so that we may gain the intelligence to remain eternally fixed in the shelter of your lotus feet.

(Written by Ravīndra-svarūpa dāsa)

## Potomac

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Before offering your Vyāsa-pūjā homage, we must take note of the fact that you picked us up from a completely fallen state. We were completely outside the purview of Vedic culture. We knew nothing *about jñāna* and *vairāgya* before meeting you. You once gave an example of how Lord Rāma employed monkey soldiers to help defeat Rāvaṇa. Similarly, you came to America and enlisted the help of monkeys to help spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Just as one worships mother Ganges by offering Ganges water back to her, so similarly we must worship you by repeating your words, because we know nothing outside of Your Divine Grace.

As the years pass by, we are getting a clearer perception of your greatness. You fully embody the qualities of an *ācārya:*

*tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta*  
*jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam*  
*śābde pare ca niṣṇātaṁ*  
*brahmany upasāmāśrayam*

**"Therefore any person who seriously desires real happiness must seek a bona fide spiritual master and take shelter of him by initiation. The qualification of the bona fide** ***guru*** **is that he has realized the conclusions of the scriptures by deliberation and arguments and is thus able to convince others of those conclusions. Such great personalities who have taken shelter of the Supreme Godhead, leaving aside all material considerations, are to be understood as bona fide spiritual masters."** ***(Bhāg.*** **11.3.21)**

In your Bhaktivedanta purports you have very lucidly presented the conclusions of the scriptures based on the purports of our predecessor *ācāryas* and your direct Kṛṣṇa conscious realizations. Through them you have converted thousands of conditioned souls to the conclusions of the *śāstras.* In this age, where almost everyone is absorbed in bestial sense gratification, you have taken full shelter of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, leaving aside all material considerations. And by taking shelter of your service, we also are able to resist the currents of this age and remain in a renounced position.

Your books are like *cintāmaṇi* stones. Reading and distributing them immediately makes one ecstatic. You have ordered us to make our lifetime plan centered around distributing your books. We know that this will do good for us and those who have a chance to hear from you. As Lord Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā:*

*anye tv evam ajānantaḥ*  
*śrutvānyebhya upāsate*  
*te 'pi cātitaranty eva*  
*mṛtyuṁ śruti-parāyaṇāḥ*

**"Again there are those who, although not conversant in spiritual knowledge, begin to worship the Supreme Personality upon hearing about Him from others. Because of their tendency to hear from authorities, they also transcend the path of birth and death." (Bg. 13.26)**

You truly want to see the conditioned souls transcend the path of birth and death. Your mercy and compassion remind us of Vasudeva Datta, because your heart pains on seeing the conditioned souls suffer the pangs of material life, devoid of Mahāprabhu's mercy. Anyone who has received *hari-nāma* from you wants to give it to others. This is your transcendental potency

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nāhe tāra pravartana*

**"The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement." (C.c. Antya 7.11)**

You have spread *hari-nāma* like no one else. And you have enlivened your disciples and granddisciples to also go out and spread Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement. When we reflect on how much mercy you have given us in the form of the holy name and in the different activities of broadcasting the glories of the holy name, we simply become embarrassed by our feeble attempts to repay you. Please bless us on this most holy day of your divine appearance that we may truly dedicate our lives to your mission of preaching Lord Caitanya's message. Also, please help us to execute one of your most important orders: "If you actually love me, do not fight. Stay united. Do not let this become another Gaudiya Math. Maintain the society in my absence."

*Begging to remain your eternal servants,*

## Puerto Rico

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my fallen obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, to your books, and to your ISKCON movement.

We, your disciples and granddisciples, as well as those of us who are yet aspiring for initiation, are very thankful to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for His having sent you to this earth. Yes, today is indeed the most auspicious occasion for all living entities in this dark age of Kali. Ninety years ago Your Divine Grace appeared in the Calcutta suburb of Tollygunge, the same year that the transcendental pioneer Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura introduced Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the Western world by means of his book *The Life and Precepts of Lord Caitanya.* As you reflected one year on the occasion of the disappearance anniversary of your own Guru Mahārāja, "Who would have known that I would come to America, who would have known that I would have met all of you, who would have known that I would establish so many temples? It is all simply the inconceivable will of the Lord, working through the transcendental system."

The devotees here report that you have never personally visited Puerto Rico, but if only you had, you would have enjoyed the Vṛndāvana-like setting. Śrīmatī Tulasī-devī abounds here and there. Jubilant peacocks are always strutting around, and their unique repertoire of sonorous calls is heard daily. Mango and banana trees grace the property, as well as stately coconut palms. (Your disciple Viprahita prabhu can at times be seen atop such tall trees, cutting "cocos" for the pleasure of the Vaiṣṇavas.) Although we do not experience "the onomatopoetic sounds of the cuckoo," we are graced by the Puerto Rican equivalent, namely the melodious *coqui.* Also, as the temple (with Their Lordships Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma) sits on the summit of a very large hill, the surrounding countryside is always in plain view, all the way to the ocean. The terrain greatly resembles the jutting hills around Vṛndāvana that were once straddled by the gigantic forms of Pūtanā and Aghāsura, after the Lord had finished them.

Of course, the greatest pleasure for you would have been the transcendental sound vibration, the *hari-nāma,* the preaching effort of the devotees. We are very neophyte, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet we all share a burning desire to please you by spreading knowledge of Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa's holy name throughout this island. People are a little pious, but they are still suffering and are often confused by various strains of animal-slaughtering so-called Christianity. They do come forward, however, to take *prasādam* cakes from the table at the *hari-nāma* festival. They like the music, are curious about the books, and spontaneously give some donation.

So there is all hope that, by your mercy, by your divine grace, our lives can become blessed and transcendentally successful in the establishing of your ISKCON movement very solidly here in Puerto Rico. Let the true Vṛndāvana spirit captivate the hearts of all the residents. Let them become true lovers of Kṛṣṇa and see how the Lord is in everyone's heart—no living entity should be killed unnecessarily! Let the day come when we witness the practical institution of *varṇāśrama-dharma.* These are all your desires, and so they are ours as well.

We are fortunate to be living in the midst of an earthly paradise. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, may we also become beautified. May we develop pure minds that always see Kṛṣṇa in everyone, in everything, and also see all beings as part of Him. And may we never forget you, O Jagad-guru, the one who has come to herald the golden age of Lord Caitanya, the one who is the only hope for this age.

Although you may not have come here during your manifest presence, may you kindly accept our invitation that you come and stay with us from now on. We promise to follow your instructions so that we will never lose our appreciation that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are with us always.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for saving us.

(Written by Prabhupāda dāsa)

*Your eternal servants of the Puerto Rico Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Pune

Without ācārya there is no hope  
for conditioned souls to learn to cope  
with all the myriad of wild illusion,  
attachment to which brings sure delusion.

Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, you have come  
to help us, to teach us, to chastise some.  
Your efforts will never go to waste.  
You forced upon us the higher taste.

To give out Kṛṣṇa, no easy task,  
but still you give and give, not ask.  
Unselfish, pure, and fully knowing,  
Kṛṣṇa pleased. He keeps you going.

Your message never to be forgot,  
stating boldly what has become our lot,  
explaining how to be freed from doubt,  
the material world and how to get out.

O Prabhupāda, you always worked so much,  
tirelessly preaching, applying your touch,  
a touch of class, a touch of love,  
a touch that comes from Kṛṣṇa above.

Please help us, strengthen us, make us pure,  
protect us from the magic of māyā’s lure.  
Keep us happy, stay by our side;  
we desperately need you as our guide.

Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, you gave us your books,  
Kṛṣṇa's sweet name, fame, and even His looks.  
You gave us new life, without asking a cent.  
We'll serve you forever, cent percent.

*Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

(Written by Maṇihāra dāsa)

## Rio de Janeiro

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Restless and floating in this terrible ocean of material ' existence, the only ones who can be saved are those who are intelligent enough to hold onto the lifeboat of your instructions, which always indicate clearly the temporality and misery which are of this world.

Those whose eyes are covered by the darkness of ignorance can only be relieved from their blindness by looking upon the dazzling glitter of your golden body and by hearing your divine words, which alone can dissipate the darkness and deliver the light of instruction about devotional service unto the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The path of devotional service, under the shelter of your lotus feet, is the only source of eternal spiritual bliss.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are Kṛṣṇa's answer to our pleading prayers. You are a golden giant whose eyes deliver the purifying light of the sun, whose words have the power of a king, who delivers the love of a mother and the confidence of a father, whose smile is like the happiness of a child, and whose deeds prove the determination and the mercy of the spiritual master of the three worlds.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the tangible proof of the existence of the reality, of the knowledge, and of the love of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

*Your servants of ISKCON Rio de Janeiro,*

## St. Louis

I first met your devotees preaching on the street, performing kīrtana,  
giving your books to everyone they'd meet.  
One boy said, "Come join us, and give us a hand.  
You'll be blissful if you chant and become a Prabhupāda man."

I heard you speak in the park at the house of flowers,  
Condemning gross materialists, impersonalists, and those allured by mystic powers.  
Absorbed by your style, the spark was being fanned.  
It seemed like a very attractive proposition to be a Prabhupāda man.

We all went out on saṅkīrtana; only one man stayed at the temple.  
Your books went out profusely and gave the masses the spiritual example.  
The funds built international projects and purchased preaching vans  
To expand the numbers of fallen souls transformed into Prabhupāda men.

It was obvious the world began feeling the weight of this movement.  
They cried, "Brainwash. " You said, "Yes, the cleansing of their brain needs improvement."  
Farms, restaurants, and other cultural projects were added to your camp.  
The temples were overflowing—everyone wanted to be a Prabhupāda man.

We thought you'd personally be here forever, your association was so sweet.  
Every year in Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana we'd all walk with you on the street.  
But Kṛṣṇa needed you elsewhere; it was obviously His plan  
To give another planet of people the chance to become a Prabhupāda man.

Before you left, you instructed your disciples to carry on the legacy.  
For ten thousand years to come, the law books your books will be.  
You said, "Just cooperate together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your land.  
All programs will increase if you remain a Prabhupāda man. "

Now many years later the preaching is still strong throughout most of this planet—  
*Especially the countries where devotees are still book distribution fanatics.*  
*Of course, there is difference of opinion; that's how the material world stands,*  
*But nothing will be successful unless one's empowered to be a Prabhupāda man.*

They say we can judge a tree by its fruit tasting sweet.  
And all the devotees that I see "actually" attached to your lotus feet  
Are carrying on the sampradāya like a mango given hand to hand,  
'Cause we all have the fortune of some day becoming a genuine Prabhupāda man!

(Written by Sura dāsa)

## Salzburg

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Prayer**

O lord, O dearmost spiritual master,  
Let the swan of my mind enter within the network  
Of your transcendental book distribution.  
Let my heart beat to the rhythm of love and devotion.  
Let my intelligence become fully dovetailed  
In determined, steady, and consistent devotional service.  
I call on you, O lord and master!  
Please save us from the disaster  
Of giving up your instructions  
And abandoning your book distribution.  
I depend solely on you for my future.  
Kindly don't kick me away.

O dearmost worshipable master,  
I am your sold-out animal, yours forever, yours in service.  
Let my heart enflame with a burning desire  
To go out and meet the conditioned souls—  
To preach to them, to give them your books.  
My dear lord, my dear spiritual master,  
O savior of the fallen, O friend and well-wisher of the devotees,  
Kindly bless us poor conditioned souls.  
Give us your service, and let us become your beasts of burden.

**Realization**

When my heart blossoms  
With pure realization  
Of my eternal spiritual nature,  
My eternal link with Śrī Kṛṣṇa  
In unalloyed devotion,  
Then perhaps I'll be able  
To evaluate your causeless mercy, O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Which causes and sustains such realization.

Only then, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Can I start to deeply and sincerely,  
Consistently and eternally  
Offer my obeisances and service to you,  
With no motive save and except to please you.

Until then I'll serve you and worship you humbly  
And patiently wait for the realization.

Your servants at the Institute for Bhakti-Yoga—Salzburg,

Jaya Gurudeva dāsa / Jimuta dāsa / Vṛndāvanī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Susi / Śeṣa / Nanda / Nimāi / Rāmadāsa / Govinda

## San Diego

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The highest ecstasy in devotional service is to be merged in the feelings of separation. Through the feelings of separation, the devotee becomes more absorbed in thoughts of his lord and thus merges increasingly in waves of ecstatic love.

If I were actually a devotee, my love, devotion, and ecstasy would have all increased after your departure. My mind would have become fixed on your lotus feet as a result of your separation. Unfortunately, because I'm the lowest of devotees, I cannot understand the intensified devotional ecstasy of separation. I can only perceive the misery of separation.

I tell myself that you are still with us. A neophyte is only attached to the body of the spiritual master; the real disciple associates with the *guru* through his instructions. Those instructions still exist today with the same relevance and purifying potency they had when they were first uttered. However, if one is a neophyte, he is more attached to the body, even though he understands that the instructions are the essence. What can I say? What can I do? I am a neophyte. I do not understand. I feel unhappiness.

Daily we study the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which teaches us of the ephemeral nature of the world and of life itself. However, we were living as though you would be with us forever. We could not imagine a Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. To us, there was no meaning to ISKCON separate from you. You were ISKCON. You were knowledge personified. You were our guiding light, our inspiration, and our only happiness. Therefore if you were not with us, we would become confused, unenthusiastic, and unhappy. Therefore how could we ever imagine a movement without you?

In your absence, who would enliven your disciples? Who would inspire us (and even whip us) to print and distribute books? Who would continue to develop major preaching projects and temples in India? Who would be able to start new projects? Who would correct the philosophical misunderstandings that entered the movement? Who would settle disputes between leaders? Who would chastise us for being foolish, negligent, wasting your money, concocting preaching plans, and deviating from your desires in multifarious ways? Therefore you can understand, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how we, your disciples, could not imagine an ISKCON without you. Such a conception never even entered our minds, and if it did, we kicked it out.

Then you told us you were planning to leave this world. Who could understand this desire, what to speak of believe it? ISKCON means Śrīla Prabhupāda. How could you leave? We were totally helpless and dependent on you. We couldn't even imagine such a calamity falling upon our heads. Just see what fools we were! Just see what foolish disciples you had.

Then you left us. What to do? Who's *guru?* How to be *guru*? What's *guru*? What's ISKCON? What's Gaudiya Math? Who is Śrīla Prabhupāda? How to establish *varṇāśrama-dharma*? Apparently these are simple points that we thought we clearly understood in your presence. However, in your absence your foolish children stood somewhat helpless and overwhelmed by the shock of separation.

What a shock it was! For the first time (twelve years) we had an ISKCON without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. How awkward it felt. ISKCON meant you. Now we had an ISKCON without your physical presence. How strange it was. No more were we being regularly whipped to distribute your books, encouraged to expand the movement, chastised, corrected, inspired. Now we had to look more seriously at your instructions, your mission, and your life, otherwise we would drown in the ocean of material consciousness.

Now we were forced to realize that you are actually present through your instructions. This understanding was forced upon us. Now we had no other way to associate with you—no other way to get inspiration from you, no other way to get guidance from you. Yet how difficult it is to realize your presence in this way. Śrīla Prabhupāda, those days in your association were filled with sweetness, warmth, and ecstasy. Those days are gone. I simply think of them and lament.

The *gopīs* were so agitated in separation from Kṛṣṇa that they wanted to forget Kṛṣṇa, thus ending their misery. Sometimes I would stupidly think that if I never met you or became your disciple, then I would not be experiencing the pain of your separation. Then at least my life would not be filled with this agony. Just see what a sinful rascal I am! Actually I am selfish. I long for the days when you were here, the days when life was blissful, simple, and satisfying. Simply I am thinking of my sense gratification.

You personally witnessed the dissolution of the worldwide preaching of your spiritual master's mission, but you were not discouraged. You steadfastly continued to spread Lord Caitanya's message. Sometimes I become discouraged and critical because in your absence the blissful unity of our movement is constantly being threatened. This can become a stumbling block. Sometimes I feel that perhaps the expectations you had for this movement will never be met. But when I reflect on your position and service in reference to your spiritual master's mission, I truly realize my foolishness. Your order to spread this movement still stands, whether or not everything is to my liking. If I truly want your association now, I must work a lot harder to get it. I have to adopt the philosophy of "do as I am doing," if I want your association. You fully followed your Guru Mahārāja, and thus you stated that you always felt he was with you, by your side. Thus I can see that to mitigate my own suffering of separation, to realize that you are by my side, I must dedicate everything to spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, just as you did. The real disciple must never let anything discourage him, no matter how apparently real that discouragement seems. If anyone had reason to be discouraged, it was you, not me. You had no support from any quarter. We still have so much facility to preach.

Ultimately, what else can I do? The entire world is waiting to meet you. There are people everywhere ready to become devotees. Can I tell them to go to hell because in my unhappiness of your separation I am no longer enthused to preach? I must give them to you. They will never meet anyone else who will help them. And there are so many young and strong men who are now *gurus, so* the newcomers will most likely enjoy the blessing of physically associating with their *guru* for many more years than we had, perhaps even for their entire lives.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness is dynamic. Unless we spread it, we will die. What is the value of a dead man? I am trying to be alive, even though I am sometimes feeling dead due to your separation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive me for being such a neophyte. Please forgive me for being so depressed and unhappy in your absence. Please forgive me for not realizing that you are by my side, that you are present as fully as ever before in your words and service. I'm trying to understand this, but it's difficult, because I'm self-centered. I still look back to those "good ol' days," the sweet days in your association, and thus lament. But what good is that lamentation? My earnest prayer is that someday I may realize that you live forever, and that as your follower, I also live with you.

(Written by Mahātmā dāsa)

*Your worthless servants of ISKCON San Diego,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## San Fernando

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

It was predicted by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu that this saṅ*kīrtana* movement would be preached all over the world in every town and village. After five hundred years this is now a reality, blessing all suffering conditioned souls of our time with this causeless mercy.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have accepted the burden of writing books, training disciples, and opening temples, to establish ISKCON, giving us the opportunity to be engaged in your divine mission.

Recently a new branch of ISKCON has been opened in South Trinidad, enabling more people to partake in chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*, the matchless gift of this age.

Through your merciful instructions, we, your granddisciples, are now following in your footsteps, serving our Guru Mahārāja and fulfilling your desire to please your spiritual master.

*Remaining always your servants,*

## San Francisco

This Vyāsa-pūjā day is not just a festive occasion. It requires deep concern of the truly initiated disciple, a contemplative day of not just flowers, to remind the devotee of the eternal link to Kṛṣṇa and one's guru.

*guru kṛpā binā nāhi, koi sādhana bal*  
*bhajamāna bhaja anukṣaṇa, śrī guru-caraṇa-kamala-bhajamāna*

**"O mind, worship the lotus feet of the spiritual master. Without the mercy of** ***sad-guru*****, no spiritual practices have any potency. Therefore, worship him at every moment."**

The door back to home has become ajar,  
By His Divine Grace opened. He came from afar.

Not an unimportant task to reckon.  
To reawaken our rasa he has come to beckon,

Delivering a rather motley crew.  
Śrī Guru extends his mercy to not just a few.

A place of pilgrimage his morning walks,  
Crushing scientific māyāvāda views with his talks.

Kṛṣṇa sent his best and said, "Only you can do!"  
Such a humble paramahaṁsa, Prabhupāda said, "What I can do?"

The books he wrote he ascribed "Kṛṣṇa has done."  
He read them himself—they leave a sahajiyā undone.

He gave us Kṛṣṇa books, oh, what knowledge!  
He envisioned even a varṇāśrama college.

A cowherd boy in the cowboy West,  
His example and the words in his books always the best.

By his purity and love many a soul won  
Teaching "Right is might, "a qualified paramahaṁsa he's one.

Only a "kali-chela" will think he's equal with his guru.  
Beloved Prabhupāda, with straw in teeth this dāsa begs to serve you.

On this most auspicious day of your transcendental appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we fall down at your feet with straw in our mouths and beg you to give us the purity we so greatly lack to help spread your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Just before your apparent departure from this world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said that "Your love for me in my absence will be tested by how you cooperate with each other." Please give us tolerance and freedom from false prestige to be able to cooperate to maintain our own Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to expand it according to your instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also said that "It does not matter whether I am personally present or not. Everything I have to say is already in my books." Please give us the simple determination and faith to follow your instructions and to not concoct our own philosophy. Prabhupāda, you have given us everything, but we are so fallen we have very little attraction for *hari kīrtana.* Please let us remain only the insignificant servants of your servants.

(Written by Bahuśirā dāsa)

*Your eternal servants in San Francisco,*

## San Salvador

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We offer our most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of our spiritual master. His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-ṣata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

On this auspicious day on which we lovingly celebrate the appearance of our Guru Mahārāja, we pray to Providence to give us the capacity to describe at least a little spark of his effulgent glories.

*naivopayānty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudāḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ’ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakti*

**"O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahma, for You appear in two features—externally as the** ***ācārya*** **and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You."**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, only the greatest souls like you come down to this mortal world empowered specifically to preach the way back home, back to Godhead. Such pure devotees are able to execute extraordinary transcendental tasks: to wake up from their lethargy so many foolish conditioned souls and distribute profusely the philosophy of love of God throughout the whole world. For us it isn't an easy task to find proper words and a harmonious composition in praise to describe and understand Your Divine Grace; nevertheless, by your mercy, all of us are receiving the opportunity to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our prayer to you is that you bless us forever to be able to think of you in all our activities, because by thinking of you the most difficult things become easier, and in forgetting you, all things become more difficult.

Let us remain linked to your divine association by the uninterrupted and unmotivated devotional service to your lotus feet.

*Your worthless servants of New Kurukṣetra Mandira and Hogar Escuela Hare Krishna,*

## Sao Paulo

Lights and colors,  
friends and lovers,  
all are useless  
if I can't get your glance.  
Music and flowers,  
even thousands of suns,  
all are useless  
if I can't get your glance.  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!

Possession and honor,  
or even thousands of gods,  
all are useless  
if I can't get your glance.  
In presence or in separation,  
it doesn't matter the situation,  
even in heaven, even on hell,  
I just want your glance.  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!

## Seattle

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our most humble obeisances at your most merciful lotus feet, which are able to deliver all the fallen souls of this terrible age, and even a most wretched soul as myself. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of your appearance, without which there would have been no hope for any of us. Unfortunately, so many years after you have physically left us, we have yet to mature to the point of appreciating that association you gave, what to speak of appreciating and understanding your instructions. Despite so many years of experience, so many instructions, we are finding it so difficult to be humble. You are Jagad-guru, commanding all respect, and yet in all your dealings you exhibited the greatest humility, tolerating the offenses and ignorance of your followers. How unfortunate it is that we have not been able to adopt that supreme quality as a humble preacher of Lord Caitanya. Instead we arrogantly laud our own achievements, or those of others if there is some benefit, forgetting that just a short time ago our activities were indescribable. Yet with our position, our money, our territory, our followers, we are thinking, "Certainly I am empowered." Yes, but by your order Śrīla Prabhupāda, and only as the caretakers of your opulence, not the enjoyers. Because of this misconception in my own heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is very difficult for me to maintain, what to speak of trying to expand what you have given us. Always my mind and senses are raging for sense gratification, only to be cheated and kicked by *māyā.* Sometimes I think I've accomplished something, but you and Kṛṣṇa reveal how ludicrous it all is. In light of your person, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are the most insignificant beggars. We are just like small children playing with fire and unaware of it's danger. In the midst of the fire of *māyā,* we foolishly underestimate the danger and the consequences of a little inattentiveness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you warned us that we were not afraid of *māyā.* After so many lifetimes of games, now we have to grow up, because again a lifetime is coming to a close. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, force me to become serious, because on my own I cannot do anything and have no motivation for anything except for sinful life. Always I'm thinking enviously of others, caring for only my own welfare. Teach me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to develop just a fraction of the compassion which you exhibited.

Who but Lord Kṛṣṇa's dearmost servant could deliver such a fallen society? Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to stay and assist you.

(Written by Naikātmā dāsa)

*Your most insignificant beggars,*

## Singapore

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your transcendental self on this most glorious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

In the *śāstras* it is very clearly mentioned that only by the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa can one obtain a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa as a *guru*, and only by his mercy can one obtain pure love of Godhead.

As in this statement, we are finding it more and more true that only through your causeless mercy is there any hope at all for us. It goes without saying that we are certainly the most fallen of all living entities. We have no good qualifications whatsoever. Although time and again we get carried away by the waves of mdya, each time by your transcendental association we are somehow or other kept alive.

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not cast us away, as it would be impossible for us to make any advancement otherwise. Please accept whatever little we are trying to do as a humble offering at your lotus feet. We feel that only by your desire will any endeavor of ours be successful. Please bless us, as we are only trying to be an instrument in your transcendental preaching force. Kindly deliver this place and make us dance in any way you please.

Your most worthless servants,

## South Brazil

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most respectful obeisances. All glories unto you.

After traveling for so long in this circle of *saṁsāra*, by such good fortune we came in contact with a pure devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is full of compassion for the fallen souls.

For us this mercy came in the shape of your beloved son Hṛdayānanda Goswami, who is following exactly in your footsteps and is acting as your transparent via medium.

By the relationship we have with our spiritual master, we can love you and always be with you in good consciousness. We will be able to know who you are. You are in your books, in your instructions. We are learning to love you by the kindness of your so-beloved son. What we hear from him always reminds us of your desires, Śrīla Prabhupāda. He always tells us, "Distribute books! Distribute books!" That is because you always said to do so. By this book distribution, from master to disciple and so on, our love for you will be increasing throughout time. If we always remember your instructions, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to never put this *bṛhat mṛdaṅga* down, our love for you will certainly increase. Other things will follow as a consequence of this book distribution.

There will be many masters in your disciplic succession, and these masters will be the best object of love from their disciples, as for ourselves there is no better object of love than our spiritual master. And this should be the main qualification for a disciple: love for his spiritual master. You showed us this way. But even though the disciple may love his own spiritual master, if this spiritual master is your eternal servant, he is going to inject in his disciple pure love for you through him. Because even though there may be many masters—thousands of them, preaching about Lord Caitanya—you are always going to be the big Prabhupāda, the greatest of the great. This is your inconceivable mercy upon us fallen Westerners. There is no one as dear to Kṛṣṇa as yourself, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the special jewel of our disciplic succession.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, no one can compare to you! O swanlike personality among all personalities! O Jagad-guru! If we begin to try to count your good qualities, we will never stop. You created a perfect society because you are perfect. Those who take shelter of your beloved children can understand the greatness of your mission.

We were never able to have the mercy of your personal association, but we developed a relationship with our spiritual master, and he told us how to love you and how to depend on your book distribution. He showed us the way to follow you. We want to always be able to participate in this *saṅkīrtana* movement of yours. Even fallen as we are, we pray to have your mercy. We know we will have it because you are full of it. Please let us be engaged under the shade of your lotus feet.

(Written by Rāsānanda Swami)

*From your servants from South Brazil,*

## Soviet Union

Śrīla Prabhupāda went to the Soviet Union  
To free them from their Marxist confusion,  
Boldly walking around Red Square,  
Doing what no other guru would dare.

Professor Kotovsky spoke with Prabhupāda awhile,  
But he could not give up his Communist style.  
Not able to accept the existence of the soul,  
He said surrender to Lenin was life's goal.

Prabhupāda knew he was speaking to a fool,  
Despite his high position in the Moscow school.  
Impossible to speak to anyone else instead,  
The authorities were afraid of what might be said.

However, Prabhupāda met one Muscovite boy,  
And in Kṛṣṇa's service he began to employ.  
This Russian named Anatoli learned very fast.  
Prabhupāda answered every question he asked.

The seed of devotion planted in Anatoli's heart  
Made the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement in Russia start.  
Simply repeating the pure message he had heard,  
He exposed materialism as being absurd.

Bhakta Anatoli became initiated  
As Ananta-śānti dāsa; he was obigated  
To serve Prabhupāda's transcendental desire  
For freeing the Soviets from material life's fire.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness was spreading far and wide,  
Soviet citizens accepting Prabhupāda as their guide.  
It was a miracle happening behind the Iron Curtain  
By the grace of guru and Gaurāṅga, that is for certain.

In 79 was the Moscow International Book Fair,  
Prabhupāda's books were there, and the KGB didn't care.  
The BBT undoubtedly had the most popular booth,  
Because nothing is more attractive than the Absolute Truth.

Then Brezhnev was out and Andropov came in,  
Determined to make Communism pure again.  
The KGB then started disrupting meetings,  
The devotees were harassed, even with beatings.

Many were arrested and sent to different prisons,  
Tried and convicted for ridiculous reasons.  
There they are forced to work very hard.  
With very little food, they practically starve.

Ananta-śānti was arrested and declared "irresponsible,"  
So the court sent him to a psychiatric hospital.  
There he is drugged while locked up with the insane.  
The doctors are sadists who enjoy giving him pain.

Four and a half years to be spent in a labor camp  
That has isolation cells that are dark, cold, and damp,  
Viśvamitra kept preaching, his service unconditional,  
Retried and sentenced to four and a half additional.

Premavatī dāsī arrested while with child in her womb,  
Forced to endure trial in a Soviet courtroom.  
Sentenced to four years in a labor camp for mothers,  
She daily saw her baby for an hour with many others.

It is a mystery, and everyone wonders why,  
That after eleven months, what caused her baby to die?  
How much longer will these miscreants be so cruel?  
We pray they meet Nṛsiṁha-deva face to face in a dual.

The dangers, the risks, the chance of prosecution  
Don't stop the Soviet devotees from book distribution.  
It's amazing they print Prabhupāda's books on their own,  
Agitating the authorities and making them groan.

So many devotees are already incarcerated,  
So many more are being investigated.  
Inconceivably they continue, by Prabhupāda's grace,  
Spreading Lord Caitanya's mission to the Soviet race.

(Written by Dhūmrakeśa dāsa)

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

(inside the Soviet Union in a psychiatric hospital) Ananta-śānti dāsa

## Spain

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to this ISKCON movement, which is your transcendental body.

"He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die,  
While thou art living still in sound . . ."

You are with us in the form of your *vāṇī,* or divine instructions. You have left your books, which will be the guiding light of human society for the next ten thousand years. And you have left us a house in which we may all live peacefully.

When you were present in your *vapuḥ* form, you frequently spoke out to protect our movement from various pitfalls. It was very easy then to understand what you desired and what you did not. Now there appears to be confusion when we do not hear your voice, which still speaks clearly to those who care to listen.

You made your desires for ISKCON very clear. From the very beginning you wanted ISKCON to be guided by a G.B.C. body, just as your Guru Mahārāja wanted. The same direction, the same goals, the same programs are there. Our relationship with you has not been changed by mere physical separation. Neither do we need to take another *śikṣā-guru* in your absence. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the supreme *śikṣā-guru* for the entire world, and one who takes up your instructions is also *guru.* The difference is that now we must become more conscious of what will please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: "What would Śrīla Prabhupāda say about this, or what would he do about that?"

We may say, "I love Śrīla Prabhupāda," but these words are hollow without adherence to your desires. Remain with this ISKCON movement and preach—this is the position of one who loves Śrīla Prabhupāda. Sometimes superficially there are faults in our society, just as one sometimes sees imperfections in the body of a pure devotee. Just as one overlooks the bubbles, foam, and mud in the Ganges, we may tolerate these difficulties for the sake of pleasing you.

You have sprouted a living, dynamic branch of the Caitanya tree. One who minimizes his connection with you cuts himself off from the mercy of the Pañca-tattva. By pleasing you, we become nourished and revitalized.

But you are not to be pleased easily. We must give up our envy and ambition and preach in a spirit of cooperation. ISKCON is nondifferent from the spiritual world. We cannot live in this transcendental house as long as we invite Kali to sit within our hearts. There is no room in the ever-expanding spiritual sky for jealousy, quarrel, and hypocrisy. And it is certain that as Kali-yuga progresses, as the threat of world war becomes imminent, the only safe position is to remain within the movement you have created, a refuge in the midst of the storm.

What else can we offer you but to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness together, without fighting amongst ourselves? When we can say that there is a set of books in every home, when the *mahā-mantra* is heard on every tongue, we know this will please you. When your goals of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness become our goals, then we have actually attained perfection. We do not know any other process.

(Written by Agrāhya dāsa)

*Your fallen servants in Spain,*

## State College, Pennsylvania

All glories to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda on the most auspicious day of his appearance.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Our humble offering to you is that we will continue trying to serve, to become purified, and to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please accept this, because I have nothing else of any value to offer you.

May all your ISKCON dreams come true, one after another, and may all of your followers unite together to serve you.

(Written by Trilokeśvara dāsa)

*Your insignificant disciples,*

Trilokeśvara dāsa / Vrajavaneśvarī-devī dāsī / Narottama

## Stockholm

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
Falling flat by your seat  
Begging for mercy  
Serve forever your divine lotus feet.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
This māyā is so strong  
Keeps shaking me hard  
Try not to do anything wrong.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
I'm struggling to get through  
It's sometimes so sad  
Being here without you.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
Despite all darkness around  
Effulgent like Gaurāṅga you appear  
Broadcasting the holy sound.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
Dear father and guide  
Your nectarean words  
Make all ignorance subside.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
I can still hear your voice  
Strong but sublime  
"Rascals, you have no other choice."

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
I remember your lotuslike face  
There on the Vyāsāsana  
Reflection of Gaurāṅga’s grace.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
What more can there be  
Chanting your name  
And serving you eternally?

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
Serving the Lord with pure heart and mind  
That's your divine gift  
To this fallen Kali-yuga mankind.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
The future will see  
The miracle you've done  
To set everyone free

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda  
Falling flat by your seat  
Begging for mercy  
Serving forever your divine lotus feet.

*All glories, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

(Written by Prapujaka dāsa)

## Sweden

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances. All glories unto you on this auspicious occasion of your ninety-first appearance day. Your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is all-auspicious, because simply by our remembering you, then Kṛṣṇa, who is situated in the heart as Paramātmā (Supersoul), becomes moved to purify us from the contaminations of *māyā*, which constantly disturb us with so much mundane nonsense that we gradually become mad after unreal things. However, simply by congregationally chanting JAYA PRABHUPADA, we become peaceful and fearless. Certainly you are the master of all mystics, in that we, your servants here on the Swedish farm, who are from one isolated corner of the North Pole of the earth planet, feel intimately in touch with your servants, admirers, and friends all around the world who are also gathered in ISKCON temples on this day to honor you. Especially when reading your Vyāsa-pūjā book, we feel embarrassed by the humility of our Godbrothers who have accepted your lotus feet as their all in all. We pray that we may never misuse our relationship with you for some material purpose, but rather that we may successfully reach the goal of constant remembrance of you as our eternal lord and master.

JAYA SRILA PRABHUPADA!

## Switzerland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

It is said that the transcendental activities and words of the pure devotees are eternal and that their glories are sung in all the three worlds. There have been many who spoke properly, but whose behavior was not perfect; and there have been many who acted properly, but who could not speak perfectly. But in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find all perfection. Both your activities and your speech were perfect. Therefore all living entities can become purified by the words emanating from your lotus mouth: *guru-mukha-padma-vākya cittete koriyā aikya.*

Since only a few very fortunate souls had the opportunity of your *vapuḥ* association, you have mercifully given your association to everyone in the wonderful form of your transcendental books. You said once that every single word in your books is transcendental. Indeed, they are so powerful that one can make spiritual progress simply by reading a few lines or even just one word, what to speak of an entire book or the whole set!

Your books are the real revolution for mankind, and they are gradually appearing in every household throughout the world. Of course, with our Dr. Frog mentality we can understand neither the real power of these books nor how they will effect a total change in the world picture; but we can understand that a pure devotee has desired that Kṛṣṇa consciousness be spread throughout the world and that it is the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that His name be chanted in every town and village. Who or what could possibly stop this tidal wave? No one and nothing—except perhaps our own speculations, our not following your instructions and not working together. Therefore we pray to your lotus feet that we can make your desire our desire:

*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati ei sei uttama gati*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

Attachment to your lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires. If We—each one of us—can do this, then we can push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement with the same determination and enthusiasm as you did.

Because these books are your life and soul, all problems come when we don't distribute them. Here in Northern Europe we have the great fortune to be working together with your dear servant His Holiness Harikeśa Mahārāja, so we always have plenty of books. The warehouse is always full of your books—full of love of God. They sit there and anxiously wait to be distributed, and we are simply praying to your lotus feet that we may be allowed to go on distributing these books more and more. Otherwise our lives are simply useless.

(Written by Rohiṇīsuta dāsa)

*Your servants at the Swiss Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda and present ISKCON** ***ācāryas***

## Toronto

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances. You who are the foremost world representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, please be merciful to us. Guide us to remain on the pure platform, and give us strength to follow the four regulative principles and to chant sixteen rounds of the *mahā-mantra* daily.

Please accept this modest offering in the shape of poetry, which expresses some of our realizations about the age in which we live and about what you taught us is the panacea for this troubled world.

**The Resolution**

Truth, purity, mercy, austerity diminished,  
All symptoms of personal integrity are all but finished  
And society stands on sin alone,  
Its fabric unraveled and remaining unsewn.

The presidential seat usurped by the lowest born,  
The citizens in a condition left forlorn.  
Justice is shown them on the strength of monetary power.  
"Tomorrow is nil, "they think, and they live for the hour.

Sex is the thrust for the populace inane,  
Their fashioned hair marks fame, fortune, and gain.  
Vows have no substance, though they swear, "I do."  
"I don't" is the reality; they seek a mate anew.

Wit is determined by one's jugglery of words.  
Message of the Absolute is practically unheard.  
High birth is detected by the gold in one's pocket,  
A simple man, penniless, a target to mock at.

Family has no place, only "individualism,"  
The independent spirit creating his own schism  
From his bond to the Lord, who in this age is aborted.  
"Our Father, who art in heaven" will all sound morbid.

Harassment by burglars, beggars, and government men,  
Excessive taxes burden again, again, and again.  
Attacks from the front, behind, and all around.  
In the myriad of mobbings there is no "safe and sound."

The flesh of the animal is the mainstay of man.  
The herb is near extinct as industry hoards the land.  
Toxins are the beverage and smoking the delight;  
Karma piles up expansively to explode into a fight.

Hypocrisy will reach its summit when sages renounce God.  
This denotes the age of Kali: it is all dark and odd.  
To find a genuine master is practically not at all.  
We beg to know an answer so that we may recall  
Our relationship with Godhead, so that we may reject  
The anomalies of Kali and turn back to reflect  
On the savior called Gaurāṅga, the illustrious monarch  
Who beckons all to halt the evil in light of this remark:

*harer nāma barer nāma barer nāmaiva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyatha*

"In this age of Kali, the age of darkness, one can  
reach life's goal by chanting the name of Hari,  
chanting the name of Hari, chanting the name of Hari.  
There is no other way, no other way, no other way. "

Your servants from New Remuṇā Dhāma, ISKCON Toronto.

## Trinidad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept our humble obeisances. Because you have so humbly followed the order of your spiritual master with great enthusiasm and determination to print books and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English-speaking countries, more and more people here in Trinidad, as well as all over the world, are receiving the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We are seeing again and again that because we followed your order to distribute your books profusely, as a result more and more people are becoming attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Because you are empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, you can spread the saṅ*kīrtana* movement. We simply have to present your books, your philosophy, what you have taught us. Please continue to engage us in this way.
