## Vienna

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Once again we are celebrating your most glorious appearance on the face of this planet, and again we are feeling that our words cannot express or describe your sublime activities. We have nothing of worth to offer you on this auspicious day but what you have given us. We hope you will accept our humble offering, just as the Ganges is worshiped by an offering of Ganges water. By your personal association and by your transcendental teachings recorded in your books like *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and *The Nectar of Devotion,* you have saved countless fallen conditioned souls such as ourselves from the dangerous clutches of material existence. No one within the three worlds can sufficiently praise your glorious activities in establishing ISKCON and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world—a work which is going on constantly with increasing strength, everywhere. You have revealed the perfect example of how one should make the order of one's spiritual master his life and soul. Just as you took the order of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, as your life's mission and achieved success, so we pray that by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we may become fully dedicated to carrying out your order to publish and distribute your books far and wide, in as many languages as there are, to convince people of every race and creed to take up the nonsectarian process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by becoming devotees, and to establish places of worship of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in every home and in every town throughout the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we are unqualified, out of your unlimited kindness you have accepted us as your disciples and grand-disciples, and you are guiding us toward the perfection of life—Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your appearance in this world is the most significant event, a brilliant flash of light in the dense darkness of Kali-yuga, an appearance as brilliant as the sun. Persons like us who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the age of Kali shall get light from your divine appearance.

We thank you from the core of our hearts and pray that we may always remember your lotus feet and remain fully surrendered to your divine instructions.

*Your servants in Vienna, Austria,*

## Villahermosa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance in this world.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (5.5.18) Rsabhadeva says to His sons:

*gurur na sa syāt sva-jano na sa syāt*  
*pita na sa syāj jananī na sā syāt*  
*daivaṁ na tat syān na patiś ca sa syān*  
*na mocayed yaḥ samupeta-mṛtyum*

**"One who cannot deliver his dependents from the path of repeated birth and death should never become a spiritual master, a father, a husband, a mother, or a worshipable demigod."**

Then, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you write in the purport: "There are many spiritual masters, but Ṛṣabhadeva advises that one should not become a spiritual master if he is unable to save his disciple from the path of birth and death. Unless One is a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, he cannot save himself from the path of repeated birth and death . . . Ordinarily, the spiritual master, husband, father, mother, or superior relative accepts worship from an inferior relative, but here Ṛṣabhadeva forbids this. First the father, spiritual master, or husband must be able to release the dependent from repeated birth and death. If he cannot do this, he plunges himself into the ocean of reproachment for his unlawful activities."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given so many quotes about the responsibility of the spiritual master, and your very life was a perfect example. Please, Jagad-guru, give us the proper intelligence to learn how to perfectly follow your divine instructions and to cooperate together in your very dear ISKCON.

*Falling at your lotus feet, your servants of Villahermosa Mandira,*

**Granddisciples**

Premānanda dāsa / Trilokeśvara dāsa / Akiñcana-gocara dāsa / Rādhikā-devī dāsī / Mādrī-devī dāsī / Bhakta Fernando / Bhakta Sergio / Nārada Muni dāsa / Śrīvāsa dāsa

## Vṛndāvana

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

IF ONE LOOKS UP from his immediate devotional task and perceives the direction of the golden armies' march, one with true vision will glance a fire-red sky of sweat, blood, and tears, fire-red horizon promised in tales of old: "Red sky in the morning, O sailors take warning." For us, the future is a loud thing feared by the generals and the citizens of ignorance as well. The air smells of sulphur and burning things, like a hell of broken glass and smothered dreams. Something seems amiss in even the baited calm of this early day, as though the world were waiting for either the final "incident" or another reprieve from certain dire fate.

The long rhetoric of peoples' revolutionary champions and smooth-shaven anti-Christs cleverly commands the attention of the collective un-Kṛṣṇa-conscious mind's enflaming lust for material freedom. From the guts everyone movingly shouts for the death of the "others," creating spontaneous scriptures for their martyr's last words. Is there doubt the world moves to the dynamic force of the madness of panic, a long, deep-throated cry away from the sage words of saints blessed in the garb of equipoised renunciation? Oh, how the cry of the damned will break the heart of mother Bhumi! Only the truly innocent and surrendered will pass to preach God's sacred names in this black age.

It is the consequence of causeless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we are looking for you everywhere, as moonstruck sunflowers exclusively beg the life rays of the first morning sun. You are the sweet sunshine of Kṛṣṇa's loving heart, Prabhupāda, and we are the drooping prairie sunflowers bewildered by doubts of weaknesses and self-hypocrisies. You are an inviolable ray of truth, unshakable guardian angel, wielder of the indescribable light of the holy name, and champion of those who seek enlightenment through humility in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. The weapon of love is in your lotus eyes, and you could not have forgotten your golden army of bright-faced boys and girls. Take us home, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all of us, young and old, big and small, black and white. We are lonely in your apparent absence. It is only by the consequence of your causeless mercy that we are looking for you everywhere.

Begging to be forever your humble servants beneath the grace of your lotus feet,

*The devotees of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira,*

## Wellington

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Today is a special day for increasing our remembrance of you. Not that we forget you; how could we?

The daily struggle to live in this world sometimes clouds our memory, fatigues our body, and dulls our enthusiasm. It is then that remembrance of you acts like a potent tonic to dispel fatigue and lethargy.

The zeal and enthusiasm you have for *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* remains unsurpassed. Your depth of faith in Kṛṣṇa brings us a great confidence—confidence which needs repeated strengthening. And that is why today is so special. We spend today in full absorption, hearing about your appearance and activities, your fame and paraphernalia.

Whenever we meditate on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, faith is restored, confidence is gained, and enthusiasm is increased.

Let us pray that we will always remember you, Prabhupāda. Let us honor you and serve you, for you are our only hope in these troubled times.

*Jaya, jaya Prabhupāda.*

*Your servants in Wellington,*

## West Germany

*sri-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-dayā karaha vicāra*  
*vicāra karile citte pābe camatkāra*

**"If you are indeed interested in logic and argument, kindly apply it to the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If you do so, you will find it to be strikingly wonderful." (C.c.** ***Ādi*** **8.15)**

This Vyasa-puja day is a special opportunity to consider the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. That mercy can be seen in two ways. One way is by studying the life of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and the other way is by studying the results of Śrīla Prabhupāda's life. And just as Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja expresses confidence that his readers will be amazed by studying the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we are confident that anyone coming in contact with the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda will find that mercy similarly "strikingly wonderful." We are confident that any person approaching the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda will experience a change in heart and begin the earnest quest for perfection.

In the beginning of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Sūta Gosvāmī offers his respects to Śukadeva Gosvāmī, "who can enter into the hearts of all." Similarly we offer our obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is equally able to enter the hearts of all, thus "bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world's misdirected civilization." This change of heart is the actual test of any religion, and Śrīla Prabhupāda would sometimes challenge on this basis. "It is practical," he would explain, and he would point to his own disciples as proof of the effect of genuine religious practice: "Each and every one of our students who has seriously taken to this process may be examined individually, and any impartial judge will find it easy to see that they have advanced in their transcendental realization further than any philosophers, religionists, *yogīs, karmīs,* etc."

Of course, we know that Śrīla Prabhupāda expects much more of us than our being merely more spiritually advanced than learned materialists. Like a proud father, sometimes he would express that expectation indirectly, by presenting his disciples as already perfected devotees:

"These boys and girls can go on chanting twenty-four hours and not get tired!"

In this way Śrīla Prabhupāda showed that the bona fide disciple glorifies his spiritual master and the entire disciplic succession by practical realization and demonstration of their mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda often said, "We have to test everything by its practical result." Thus Nityānanda Prabhu's purpose in approaching Jagāi and Mādhāi was to demonstrate the potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's mercy. If their hearts could be changed, then Lord Caitanya would be glorified. And Śrīla Prabhupāda, seeing the example of Jagāi and Mādhāi as a successful laboratory experiment, determined to apply the result in the field of the entire world, fulfilling Lord Caitanya's prediction that His name would be chanted in every town and village.

Now, by Śrīla Prabhupāda's mercy, we may become instruments in seeing that the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* goes on spreading, bringing about a factual change in the heart of society—bringing about the practical result that people turn away from suicidal life-styles and frustrated atheism. Therefore we simply pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda to give us the confidence and determination to perfect our lives by taking his sublime clear instructions into the core of our hearts—not for our own selfish ends of self-aggrandizement, but to enable us to purely demonstrate the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda to the world, bringing everyone to his divine lotus feet.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda we are engaged here in the service of Śrī Śrī Prahlāda-Nṛsiṁha-deva, who destroy all obstacles on the path of devotional service—both external and internal. We pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda that we may be imbued with his sense of urgency to spread the message of Lord Caitanya, reducing all selfish consideration to nil, as Nṛsiṁha-deva reduces all demoniac elements to nil.

*Your loving disciples and granddisciples in West Germany,*

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## American BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*  
*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.1.22*

**"We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the dangerous ocean of Kali, which destroys all the good qualities of a human being."**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this occasion of our celebration of your appearance amongst us, we find that our primary contemplations are those centered upon the unparalleled humility embodied in your transcendental moods of devotion to your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. While you are the most important person in our lives, and while you are also the manifest loving mercy of God appearing solely for our sakes, and while you are also *jagad-guru* in this Kali-riddled world, still we also know, by Your Grace's having revealed to us the logical / illogical wonders of divine truth *(acintya-bhedābheda-tattva),* that your opinion, as revealed and stated by you, regarding your own personal qualifications is a singularly necessary key which we must use if we are to be of any true service to your mission and, indeed, if we are to be spared from the turbulent ocean of Kali . . .

"... Personally, I have no qualifications whatsoever for accepting disciples and directing so many activities, but my Guru Mahārāja has asked me to come, and I have come, and, by his grace, so many things are now going on nicely."

"... My only qualification is that my Guru Mahārāja has sent me and that therefore I came. So now I am just arranging with the help of you boys and girls for the fulfillment of the desires of Guru Mahārāja."

In these statements and in your living example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find true renunciation shining with absolute and inexorable brilliance, without diminution from even the slightest mote of self-interest, of spiritual pride.

Therefore, for ourselves, we are simply praying that we can always be thoroughly absorbed in the mood and mission of you, our Guru Mahārāja . . . you have shown us how. Only by such transcendent absorption are we able to avoid the abode of the venomous serpent of spiritual pride, whose at-first-subtle bites cause one to contemplate himself as being possessed of so many good qualifications and of excellent intuitive abilities as regards scriptural interpretations of such questions as "What is spiritual advancement and what is not?" "Who (else) is spiritually qualified and who is not?" "What is preaching and what is not?" etc., etc. One who, due to not being absorbed in *guru-sevā,* fails to endeavor to slay this snake of false pride with the twin sword of transcendental knowledge and genuine renunciation soon finds himself distanced from both his peers and any worthwhile followers alike, and he is thereupon very likely to further endanger his spiritual future by manifesting envy and its ensuing anger.

We worship you as *jagad-guru* and give our lives to the service of your mission, confident that by your grace we are enabled to be ever mindful that, per your exemplary relationship with your Guru Mahārāja and his mission, whatever seemingly "personal" qualifications which we appear to have brought to or which manifest amidst our worship / service of you and your mission are simply ornaments which flourish and are exalted with ourselves by devotional usage but which are also tarnishable and can become leaden should we, amidst such exaltations, forget what we're about,. . . should we forget that which is the only qualification to which we can lay valid claim: personal determination to serve you *on your terms.* To ascribe to ourselves within our hearts any other "spiritual" qualifications is great folly and cannot, by constitution, lead to any positive spiritual result . . .

(Written by Mahendra dāsa)

*Your humble and faithful servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust of North America,*

Rāmeśvara Swami / Mahendra dāsa / Jāmbavān dāsa / Raṇadhīra dāsa / Nirantara dāsa / Nirākulā-devī dāsī / Śatadhārī-devī dāsī

## Australian BBT

Let us offer our most respectful obeisances unto the greatest of all spiritual preceptors, Śrīla Prabhupāda, whose appearance on this planet was strictly in line with the mood and teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have shown us by your appearance and activities the real purport of Lord Caitanya's advent. You taught us that the Lord appeared in His Gaurāṅga form to deliver the most fallen souls by teaching them the highest and most confidential knowledge of how to love the Supreme Lord. You instructed us to follow in the footsteps of Mahāprabhu and try to save every conditioned soul in every town and village by preaching Krsna consciousness and inducing everyone to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you established a multitude of temples, so that people from far and near can take advantage of this facility to associate with devotees and hear philosophical discourses about the Supreme Lord and find shelter from the anxiety-ridden atmosphere of the material world. You gave us *prasādam,* the mercy remnants of food offered to the Lord, which not only purifies us but captures and controls the rampant tongue and gives us a higher taste, so that we never take to abominable habits again. You gave us the Supreme Lord in His Deity form to worship and care for, so that we may become personalists after so many lives of conditioning in impersonalism. You gave us *maṅgala-ārati,* so that we may receive inspiration and blessings from Their Lordships first thing in the morning, filling our minds, vacant after hours of sleep, with the beauty, fame, wealth, strength, renunciation, and knowledge of the Lord, enabling us to push on through the day with restraint over our otherwise insatiable senses. You gave us the *mahā-mantra,* which alone can deliver one from the miseries of repeated birth and death. It is all-auspicious and allows one to perceive all happiness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us your example to follow. You left us a whole movement full of dedicated and pure-hearted devotees. You left us a vision and plans for a capitol city, Māyāpur, to center and unite your movement, which serves to manifest the cooperative spirit you wanted. You gave us a mission, a goal to strive for, which is to establish ISKCON as the world authority on religion and science.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given all this and so much more, but your ultimate mercy to all of us is without a doubt the unlimited amount of knowledge you put in your books. To describe your translations and writings as "monumental work" is an understatement, because you have given in your books all the knowledge one needs to make life perfect. This knowledge is pertinent not only for the present but is designed to enlighten suffering humanity ten thousand years from now. This reflects your greatness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we humble souls trying to assist you in your BBT would like to express our gratitude to you by renewing our vow to establish you as the world's foremost authority, by printing, distributing, and preserving your masterpiece: your books.

(Written by Bala dāsa)

Bhavānanda Goswami / Nareśvara dāsa / Jyotirmaya dāsa / Gopamātā dāsī

## Portuguese BBT

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories unto Your Divine Grace, who are the personification of Lord Caitanya's mercy on us, the fallen souls of this *pāścātya-deśa.*

Your appearance among us is inconceivable. Even if we try to understand it with our thousands of brains, we still cannot reach a glimpse of your unlimited good qualities. I can clearly see it now that so many of us, your disciples and followers, are sincerely trying to serve you and at the same time have so many different opinions as to which is the best way. But you are so wonderful that you can accommodate all of us under the shade of your lotus feet. That is your special greatness. That is your special *līlā.* You are the great commander in chief to put order in the chaos of this hellish civilization. Unfortunately, because of our fallen condition, we sometimes miss you and do not follow your exact instructions. We cannot estimate exactly the value of your appearance among us, but how is it possible for us to ever know it fully, when you are unlimited? We can only be humble servants and stay with you by preaching and distributing books. I always pray to become humble and simple, as you showed us by your personal example. Like a *paramahaṁsa* preacher, you came and made so many devotees, opened so many temples, translated and distributed so many books, but still you remained so simple, unaffected by this world of fame. Being the purest servant of Kṛṣṇa, you have opulences like fortune, beauty, and wisdom, but as well, you have detachment from all this. May your personal example be our guide for many years to come.

I was a young boy the first time I heard of you, but I could never forget you, and from that time on I have had a very strong feeling of the urgency to faithfully serve you and your movement. I pray to have this feeling forever. I may be fallen, dull, a rascal, and with no intelligence, but I want to faithfully serve you life after life. I do not know where you are now, but I want to be there. Actually, we do not even know who you really are. Of course, you are no less than a Vaikuṇṭha gentleman, but we can never speculate who a pure devotee is. Your appearance on this planet earth is still inconceivable, and not even Anantaśeṣa, with thousands of heads, will come to an end in describing your unlimited qualities.

From this branch of your BBT, the Portuguese division, we offer our most humble *daṇḍavats* unto you and beg to have a little of your potency to print and distribute books. We know we are not like our dear God-brothers who did the impossible in printing all of your books in such a short time, but we are the little spider carrying the grain of sand. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength to print and distribute your books as the main duty in our lives.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants from Portuguese BBT,*

Īśvara Swami / Mahākāla dāsa / Jaḍa Bharata dāsa / Rāma-avatāra dāsa / Aṅgirā Muni dāsa / Hadāi Paṇḍita dāsa / Indrasaraṇa dāsa / Śrī Kṛṣṇa-mitra dāsa / Trivikrama dāsa / Uttameśvara dāsa / Eka Parameśvara dāsa / Rāma Rāghava dāsa / Māyeśvara dāsa / Ravi dāsa / Mānada dāsa / Bhakti-śakti dāsa / Bhakta Valdir / Bhūtamātā-devī dāsī / Lalita devī dāsī

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Sannyāsīs

## Agrāṇī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Within the depths of my spirit there is a heart of gold. Therein, upon an effulgently golden lotus, I am preparing a place for your lotus feet.

For so long a time this golden heart, sealed within a steellike fortress of false ego, was hidden from anyone's view, its splendor and brilliance obscured by its gross, ugly coverings.

Though compelled by an instinctive desire to rejoice in and behold its transcendent beauty, this wayward soul had instead become witlessly infatuated by the gilded attractions of a fool's paradise. With each blind attempt to fulfill the quest for the supreme happiness, I had only fallen that much deeper into darkness and despair.

You then came to me, timely, when I was in my darkest and most desperate hour. Your sublime personality, radiant with love and compassion, disarmed and melted my steel-framed ego, exposing a glimmer of the golden heart.

Divine love is the characteristic of this gold, a love pure and selfless, the love you have shown me and which in turn is awakening within me for you.

Your divine presence has transformed a once dismal and lonely existence to one rich with love and happiness. I have thus become your dependent, dependent on your love, which is as dear to me as life itself. Indeed, the reciprocation of your love is the very substance of my life.

I sometimes wonder about your divine origin, and why I have so fortunately been made a recipient of your kindness. All I can understand, however, is that you are my *guru,* and that through your causeless mercy I have been delivered from the darkest ignorance. In reality, I care not to know you in any other way. Your role as my eternal guide and father elicits from me the tendermost sentiments of love and affection.

Yet I am painfully aware that my love is insignificant. I am a mere neophyte. I do love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, though not nearly enough. Since I am unable to fully take up your instructions, my heart remains plagued with unwanted desires.

Nevertheless, my soul yearns to burst out of its egoistic confines; it longs to express itself fully. Please bless me that my love for you may not be obstructed by lust for material enjoyment. I fall at your lotus feet and pray for the strength and determination to meticulously remove the material coverings, so that my heart may finally effuse brilliantly golden in pure, selfless love for Your Divine Grace. Thus I may fulfill this most cherished of my desires—to enshrine your lotus feet upon the golden lotus within my heart of gold.

*Your unworthy disciple,*  
Agrāṇī Swami

## Ānanda-svarūpa Swami

Grief-stricken I come to you today, my master.  
Who can tolerate your separation?  
Only as a symptom of your grace!

You brought renunciation to the kingdom of pretense,  
Honesty and straightforwardness to a political imbroglio.  
And you were the belle of the ball!  
The one who danced in tune,  
Your seraphic smile dazzling the jaundiced eye,  
Your soft words caressing my heart,  
And your transcendent ire an undeserved blessing.

Shall I list some of your achievements?  
Red-faced and ashamed, disappointed, angered, and frustrated by the necessity, I must—and this after only nine years!  
"Never was there a time that I didn't remember Kṛṣṇa."  
That's the first achievement, that you never fell!  
"If I have any credit in this connection, it does not belong to me personally, but it is due to my eternal spiritual master."  
That's the next achievement, that you never cheated.  
And the best achievement of all is that you spoke the truth.  
As It Is:

\[from Śrīla Prabhupāda's *Vaiśiṣṭyāṣṭakam—*see 1982 Vyāsa-pūjā book\]

21\. If everyone simply sat down together and considered these things, then what preaching there could be!

22\. This is your order also, that everyone coming together should merge in your message and preach it to the world.

23\. If everyone simply imitates, then there will only be a contradictory result. As long as it goes on, there will only be failure.

24\. Even now, my Godbrothers, you return here on the order of our master, and together we engage in his *pūjā.*

25\. But simply a festival of flowers and fruit does not constitute worship. The one who serves the message of the *guru* really worships him.

26\. The service of the message is the real meaning of the *Vedas.* Don't be proud, brothers. Come back to this.

29\. Oh, shame! My dear brothers, aren't you embarrassed? In the manner of businessmen you increase your disciples.

32\. But take a good look at the terrible situation that has arisen. Everyone has become a sense enjoyer and has given up preaching.

36\. From the seas, across the earth, penetrate the universal shell; come together and preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

37\. Then our master's service will be in proper order. Make your promise today. Give up all your politics and diplomacy.

38\. Everyone, come together on this very day and give your valued opinion. All of you get together and decide what should be done.

41\. If there is only one Supreme Lord, then a true *sādhu,* even if his wealth increases, gives up his desire for profit, adoration, and prestige in this material world.

42\. Your gold, brother, is the father of sense gratification. This meaningful statement was spoken by Prabhupāda himself.

43\. Give up your wealth for preaching. Sit down together and make a special effort to come to a decision.

44\. The Supreme Lord Himself says that everything is His. Don't protest. Just unite and preach.

45\. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave this final message himself. Take great care to do that completely and in all respects.

46\. Otherwise your *sannyāsa* will be wasted and useless. O saintly ones, be careful; otherwise afterwards you will regret.

47\. So what is the difficulty for all of us to come together in this way? And why do these things even have to be said to you?

48\. Give up your stubbornness; there is no time! Come, all my brothers, on this auspicious holy occasion.

52\. The demons simply plunder the Lord's wealth. The hapless populace cries *"Hay, hay"* in distress.

56\. The householders beg from the *sannyāsīs,* and why not? The *sannyāsīs* have tens of millions in the bank.

60\. Everyone has become a witch by the influence of the age. Everyone is unhappy due to the absence of the mercy of the *vaiṣṇavas.*

61\. The *vaiṣṇavas* are famous as *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī.* This fame will increase as the preaching increases.

63\. *Prāṇmām upakārāya:* Do work for the benefit of all living beings. This is Mahāprabhu's teachings. This is the storehouse of happiness in this life and the next.

64\. So much work has fallen into your hands, so gather together and do it.

65\. Vāsudeva Datta said to the Lord, paying his obeisances: "Save all the living beings in the material world.

66\. "Give all their sins and sufferings to me, who am the lowest of all. In this way all the miseries of the suffering conditioned souls will end."

67\. He was the best of Vaiṣṇavas, *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī.* He knew that there is no real happiness in gratifying the senses.

76\. The entire world is filled with the use of material objects without a trace of devotion to the Lord.

77\. Still, they are searching after peace. Preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness-fulfill their desires.

78\. Today, my brothers, get to this task. Save the battered souls by your preaching.

79\. Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please be merciful today. This time have compassion. You are not a conditioned soul of this material world.

I am always full of lust, a materialistic rascal,  
And except for your mercy, have no good qualities.  
Please inspire me—let me properly honor all living beings.  
Thus, by accepting your loving mood, make me your true servant.

śakti-buddhi-hin, ami ati dīn, koro more ātma śaṭha

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Ānanda-svarūpa Swami

## Bhakticāru Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

While offering my obeisances unto you, I visualize your lotus feet, so soft and so tenderly pink. I wish I could become a speck of dust and cling onto them forever. They are the most wonderful shelter, and who needs it more than I? I am your youngest son, and most probably that is why you showered so much affection upon me. You allowed me to stay with you all the time, and whenever I went anywhere, you asked me, "Where did you go?" I used to give some quick excuses. But now I realize how foolish I was to try to fool you. You urged me to be with you, but like a naughty child I went away to do some mischief, and when you asked me, I gave some silly excuses. Now you have gone away—I wonder whether in utter disgust.

You came to take us back with you. Those who are fortunate and pure went back, and others will go back after they have successfully completed the service you assigned to them. But I am so unfortunate and dirty that I could not develop the urge to go back to you. I am so foolish that in spite of hearing so much about that eternally blissful world where you are eternally present, I still try to derive some pleasure out of this miserable material nature—and that also at your expense.

You were always so concerned about me. One day, in Vṛndāvana, Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja sent me to Mādhava Mahārāja's *maṭha* to meet Śrīla Kṛṣṇa dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja. When I came back, you asked in your normal, tender voice, "Where did you go?"

I replied, "I went to Mādhava Mahārāja's *maṭha."*

You asked, "Why?" Although you had been bedridden for weeks, your voice roared like thunder. I began to tremble in fear, and I could not reply.

Again you asked, "Why did you go?" Your voice was so loud that the room seemed to shake with the sound.

Still trembling with fear, I replied, "Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja sent me there to ask Śrīla Kṛṣṇa dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja to come to see you."

You said, "Oh! But I want you to be with me all the time."

Another time, when you had a dream that a Rāmānuja Kavirāja prepared some medicine for you and you became cured by taking that medicine, we looked all around Vṛndāvana for a Rāmānuja Kavirāja but could not find one. Then it was decided that I would go to Śrī Raṅgam to look for a Rāmānuja Kavirāja. When you were told that someone should go to Śrī Rangam, you liked the idea. But when you were told that I was being sent, you said, "No, send someone else." Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja looked at me with a smile on his face, then he asked you why you were not allowing me to go.

You replied, "I want him to be with me now."

So mercifully you wanted me to be with you, but with time I am simply drifting away from you. Yesterday I was looking at your deity in the temple. It looked as if you were sitting there. Then I questioned myself, Did I really believe you were sitting there? If I really did, then could I stand there, so unconcerned? No! Then I would have to jump with joy, start to sing your glory—"Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda"—to announce your presence to everyone.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me the intelligence and determination to sing of your glories all the time. From now on, let me engage my body, mind, and words in singing your glories. So on this auspicious day I want to take the vow that from today, my body, mind, and words will simply be engaged in singing your glories, because I want to be with you all the time.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakticāru Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I was born in the darkness of ignorance, and you, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, have opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I therefore offer you my humble obeisances and fall at your lotus feet again and again in appreciation and recognition of the causeless mercy you have shown upon me.

Two very special qualities of a pure devotee are his meekness and mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are both humility and compassion personified, and you have so wonderfully exhibited these unique features of a Vaiṣṇava. Who once having come in contact with you can ever think of leaving your service? Coming in contact with you means directly associating with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, for you are His most dear representative. This is collaborated by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, the great illuminator of our *bhāgavata* school: "The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all the revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\]." (Śrī Śrī Gurvastaka, 2) This is your exalted position, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you exhibited fully and constantly the symptoms of a *paramahaṁsa*. You were and you remain always like the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, absorbed in the intimate pastimes of the eternal couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Simultaneously you are always meditating on serving Their Lordships by establishing the sublime teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu through this great movement of ISKCON. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya in his "Śrī Śrī Ṣaḍ-Gosvāmy-aṣṭaka" therefore equally refers to you when glorifying the activities of the six Gosvāmīs: "Thus they are honored all over the three worlds, and they are worth taking shelter of because they are absorbed in the mood of the *gopīs* and are engaged in the transcendental loving service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa."

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let me take full and unconditional shelter of your divine lotus feet. In the midst of any difficulties, please give me the strength to remain firmly attached to your sublime instructions and allow me to assist you in whatever menial manner I can. Let my desire become your desire, my meditation your meditation, my service your service. I am hopeless without your continued mercy.

You will be pleased to know that here in West Bengal the Nama Hatta movement is making daily more and more impact. This, of course, is already known to you, as you are directly present in these activities, as was revealed to me by some of our preachers who have recieved *darśana* of both you and Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. I am thus fully confident that you, my eternal spiritual father, along with all the great *ācāryas* of our Brahma-Gauḍiya-Vaiṣṇava-sampradāya, are very, very closely watching all our humble attempts in trying to help you spread the *harināma saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an ocean of mercy, your glories are sung by all the demigods, and your fame is spread all over the three worlds. I am but your insignificant servant. Please forgive me for all my offenses and shortcomings. I have but one humble request: Please never allow me to leave the shelter of your lotus feet to again fall into this great ocean of nescience. I am unable to express my gratitude for this most precious of all gifts, pure devotional service, which you are so wonderfully offering to me and to the rest of the world.

*I beg to remain your humble servant,*  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktivrajendranandana Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. On your Vyāsa-pūjā day, I make a small offering unto your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a *mahā-bhāgavata* and *paramahaṁsa* who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are the eternal confidential servitor of the Lord who has come to deliver the whole world through your causeless mercy, which is manifested in your mission—the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. I have been serving you since the early year of 1972 in this movement. I pray for your mercy that you will guide me every moment of my life, so I can remain attached at your lotus feet for the rest of my life. My faith and conviction increases more by reading your books and associating with your sincere and spiritually matured disciples and granddisciples. They give me spiritual strength and bliss for making advancement towards my spiritual life.

You have initiated me in the chanting of the holy names of the Lord, the *mahā*-mantra, which is the life and soul of my spiritual advancement. My realization for awakening love of God will be spiritually matured as long as I go on chanting inoffensively, without any personal motivation and material desires.

In the *Vedas* it is stated that as long as we have full faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa, all the imports of the Vedic knowledge are revealed. You instructed us in Vṛndāvana to push on the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu by assisting you in your movement. This ISKCON is nondifferent from you and Kṛṣṇa, because of your purity being of the same quality as Kṛṣṇa's. The only way to please you and Kṛṣṇa is to maintain our purity, which is our life and soul for pushing on your ISKCON movement. Actually, we can please you even more by becoming pure devotees. Kṛṣṇa is very kind to us in that He has guided the sincere souls to you.

You said in one of your lectures that your *guru*, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda, was a Vaikuṇṭha man. Prabhupāda, you are also a Vaikuṇṭha man, and it is very difficult to find more words to glorify you, because I am a more fallen soul than any other.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktivrajendranandana Swami

## Bhakty-abhaya-caraṇa Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which are ample and secure shelter for every one of your followers.

Despite my evident lack of capacity and qualities, you keep giving me the opportunity to serve you and the great honor of representing you. Day after day, the pride of being your disciple and acknowledgment for your undeserved grace keep on growing.

You are the creator architect of this new world in which we're living. ISKCON is, by your divine grace, a spiritual world within the material world: indestructible, secure, and with a vast space for everyone. And although in its interior, questionings regarding authority, quality, fidelity, etc., keep going on, that will help the major devotees to be really major and the minor devotees to understand the meaning of being major. We are all your sons, and in the course of time those who are sincere will be completely purified, and those who are not sincere will be bewildered. But you and your books will remain there always, permanently, as the guarantee and guiding light for everybody and for all generations to come.

Each of your words has the virtue of cleansing the heart, and each of your arguments destroys the materialistic concepts, cornerstones of this unfortunate civilization, and at the same time they build a new world with solid bases of knowledge.

You are my master. My debt toward you increases with every day, because every day I am receiving more and more of your mercy. By your mercy I breathe, by your mercy I preach, and it is by your mercy that many conditioned souls are being occupied in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa and that thousands of pieces of literature are being distributed here in Costa Rica. Like Lord Kṛṣṇa, you are pleased that your devotees get the credit for the work.

Now many of your disciples are taking the responsibility which you alone had taken before—to take the conditioned souls back to Kṛṣṇa. Personally, I feel unable to do something like that, but through your divine grace I may now be "one of those lame men who are able to cross mountains."

You are the guarantee for everyone who approaches us looking for shelter. They are confident because we are *your* disciples. Our mercy is *your* mercy, because we are made from you. We can presently help other people because we repeat your words, follow your instructions, and distribute your books. Your words are woven with the thread of truth. Everyone who contacts you through your books feels a great benefit and recognizes your grandeur. In this way your simplicity and humility are intensely penetrating my heart. "Guru is the head which inspires the institution and serves the association"—these words were made for you.

Your glorious movement is like a tree, always increasing its strength, and although it changes leaves every spring, it bears fruits every year. Even though we are working, doing our best, how can we be capable of deeply understanding the nature of your movement and the extent of your mission? You've created *tīrthas* all around the planet, and you've changed Kali-yuga into Satya-yuga for many of us. You have mined the whole world with the energy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is causing a great number of casualties to the enemy, *māyā.*

We must never forget that you ended our material bondage, teaching us how to love Kṛṣṇa, thus changing our lives. Please give me your blessings so that I can continue distributing your mercy in Latin America and around the world.

*Your disciple,*  
Bhakty-abhaya-caraṇa Swami

## Bhavānanda Rāya Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have done what no one else has been able to do. *Spṛṣaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo- 'bhiṣekam:*

You have saved us and given us Kṛṣṇa along with the potency to give Him to others. Let us not forget your unique position. Let us not try to imitate you but try to follow the high standard which you have set. It is there for everyone to see. You have truly fulfilled the meaning of *ācārya:* one who teaches by example.

Myself, I am but an insignificant *jīva* struggling to become free of *māyā’s* influence. Any advancement that I may have made is all due to your mercy, without which, every last one of us would be condemned. I am simply praying that we may continue to try to serve you.

*yathā kṛṣṇārpita-prāṇas*  
*tat-puruṣa-niṣevayā*

**"My dear king, if a sinful person engages in the service of a bona fide devotee of the Lord and thus learns how to dedicate his life unto the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, he can be completely purified."** ***(Bhāg.*** **6.1.16)**

You have so nicely stated the proper attitude for the devotee in your 1936 speech to the Gaudiya Math: "Personally I have no hope for any direct service for the coming crores of births of the sojourn of my life, but I am confident that some day or other I shall be delivered from this mire of delusion in which I am at present so deeply sunk. Therefore let me with all earnestness pray at the lotus feet of my divine master to allow me to suffer the lot for which I am destined due to my past misdoings, but to let me have this power of recollection: that I am nothing but a tiny servant of the Almighty Absolute Godhead, realized through the unflinching mercy of my divine master. Let me therefore bow down at his lotus feet with all the humility at my command."

You will always be with us in your Bhaktivedanta purports, your letters, and personal instructions as long as we are following these instructions.

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!

*Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Bhavānanda Rāya Swami

## Bir Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

The sincere disciple's relationship with the spiritual master can be compared with the chaste wife's relationship to her husband. You make this comparison in your purports to the Fourth Canto, Chapter 28, of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Just as there is no place in Vedic society for a wife who is unfaithful to her husband, there is no place in spiritual life for a disciple who is unchaste to the spiritual master.

What is chastity, and what is unchastity in relationship to the *guru*? Chastity means to have faith that all of one's spiritual needs are being provided by the *guru,* and to act with this understanding. If one, out of illusion, feels that there is something lacking in the *guru's* guidance and that therefore he needs to leave the *guru's* association in order to obtain more guidance, his position is that of a prostitute, and he will be bereft of spiritual knowledge.

Specifically, to be chaste to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, means that we remain in your movement, have full faith that everything that we need is there in your books (as you often stated), that we cooperate with each other in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we devote our lives *to your* mission. As you state in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (purport to 4.4.50), "A devoted disciple of the spiritual master would rather die with the spiritual master than fail to execute the spiritual master's mission ... In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations." Our reason for even breathing should be to satisfy you.

With every decision we make, large or small, we should question ourselves, "What would Śrīla Prabhupāda want? What would he say about this? Would Śrīla Prabhupāda be happy with this? How can we increase the pleasure of Śrīla Prabhupāda?" Bearing these thoughts in mind, we can remain fixed at your lotus feet. If we do not think in this way, we will lose your association, and without your association we are lost.

Later in the purport to text 51 you state, "In conclusion, if a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* or *vapuḥ.* This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush of Vṛndāvana, while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to the principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty."

What is to be done, though, if the spiritual master is not personally present? How can one get the direct personal instruction that he needs? Śrīla Prabhupāda, you answer this question fully in your purport to text 52 of the same chapter of the *Bhāgavatam.* You state, "At such a time \[the absence of the spiritual master\] the Supersoul immediately appears, provided the devotee is purified in heart by following the directions of the spiritual master. A sincere devotee who follows the instructions of the spiritual master certainly gets direct instructions from his heart from the Supersoul. Thus a sincere devotee is always helped directly or indirectly by the spiritual master and the Supersoul." So there is no cause for concern, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You use the word *immediately* in your purport because that is a fact. If we are chaste in our relationship to you, there is no gap in receiving instructions from you, despite your apparent absence.

So I am praying, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to never be deviated from my determination to remain at your lotus feet, considering your lotus feet to be everything I need.

*Your servant,*  
Bir Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Daśaratha Paṇḍita Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. This most auspicious occasion of your appearance day is a wonderful opportunity for a disciple to put forward some words of contemplative praise and sincere appreciation for what you have done for all of us and also for the whole world.

There is a nice verse in the Sixth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* which is deliyered by Yamarāja to the Yamadūtas, describing the qualities of great pure devotees of the Lord, such as yourself, and the destination of those foolish human beings who do not take advantage of such association. In that verse, Yamarāja says, "*Paramahaṁsas* are exalted persons who have no taste for material enjoyment and who drink the honey of the Lord's lotus feet. My dear servants, bring to me for punishment only persons who are averse to the taste of that honey, who do not associate with *paramahaṁsa*, and who are attached to family life and worldly enjoyment, which form the path to hell." (*Bhāg*. 6.3.28)

Modern civilization has become godless, due to the populace being puffed up by the false prestige of possessing material opulence and so-called scientific improvement. Practically no one is interested in the Supreme Personality of Godhead, a relationship with Him, or how one should act. In one purport you write, "Trying to defy the authority of the Supreme Lord, so-called scientists, philosophers, and politicians manufacture plans and schemes for the destruction of the world. They cannot do anything good for the world, and unfortunately, because of Kali-yuga, they have plunged the affairs of the world into mismanagement. Thus there is great need for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement for the benefit of innocent people who are being carried away by propaganda of such demons. If this kind of civilization continues, the time will soon come when the Supreme Personality of Godhead will take away all the material opulences. Then people will come to their senses."

In such a time of darkness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have appeared as our saving grace. Armed with transcendental knowledge and a "master plan" for the respiritualization of the entire world, you have been sent by the Supreme Lord with the message that will bring real peace and happiness that the conditioned souls are looking for. That message is that God is the owner and controller of the entire creation. He is the supreme enjoyer, we are His eternal servants, and He is the greatest friend of everyone. And by chanting the holy names of the Lord, all misconceptions and illusions brought about by our own lusty and greedy desires can be removed by the potency of the holy name, just as by the power of the sun a filthy place contaminated by urine and stool can be purified.

As Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has said, "Without serving a bona fide spiritual master, an ideal Vaiṣṇava, who can be delivered from the clutches of *māyā* \[illusion\]?" So we are very grateful and eternally indebted that you have appeared to enable the world to get *darśana* of that "ideal Vaiṣṇava," and we pray that you continue to give us your mercy and the blessings of Lord Caitanya, and that we will be fitting tools in your hands to assist you in the great attempt to save the world by preaching vigorously and distributing millions of your books.

I am very lowly and fallen, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and like a dog I am begging at your lotus feet that you not kick me away from the association of all the wonderful Vaiṣṇavas you have made and are continuing to make through your movement, ISKCON. Help me to not see the faults of others; rather, show me that the faults are all mine. And kindly, by your lotus hands, pick me up more and more to be with you at all times, and prune me to be the kind of disciple that can assist you in your mission as much as possible. All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda! And all glories to your true devotees and followers.

*Your attempting son and servant,*  
Daśaratha Paṇḍita Swami

## Devāmṛta Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. When presenting Vyāsa-pūjā offerings to you, it is natural to meditate on what you want. Love means selfless striving to fulfill the desires of the beloved. You have placed on your shoulders the burden of many magnificent goals. You have also delineated the basic ways and means that will achieve the goals, so transcendentally brilliant are you. The forceful clarity of your mission effectively blocks any route for an attentive disciple to speculate and spoil things. Yet we can become so much empowered by foolishness that we cannot see the forest for the trees. Either we turn the house topsy-turvy, or else we sleep soundly, mistaking the complacency of middle age for spiritual peace.

I like to remember one particular way you explained the oft-told story of the illiterate *brāhmaṇa* at Śrī Raṅga-kṣetra. The spiritual master of the illiterate *brāhmaṇa knew* his disciple could not read, you said. Still he ordered the *brāhmaṇa* to read the *Bhagavad-gītā* every day. The illiterate *brāhmaṇa* struggled daily to comply. Eventually Lord Caitanya came.

In a *Madhya-līlā* purport to this pastime you write, "This is a good example of a person who became so successful that he was able to capture the attention of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu even while reading *Bhagavad-gītā* incorrectly. His spiritual activities did not depend on material things such as correct pronunciation. Rather, his success depended on strictly following the instructions of his spiritual master. . . . actually the meanings of the words of *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are revealed to one strictly following the words of the spiritual master. They are also revealed to one who has equal faith in the Supreme Personality of Godhead."

*Trying to be your servant,*  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhanurdhāra Swami

**The Greatest Realization**

(An Homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda)

Each spiritual master in the *paramparā* must carry the message of Śrī Vyāsa to his disciple. The analogy of the postman delivering the letter is often used to indicate that the message is delivered without alteration. Becoming a spiritual master, though, is a lot more difficult than becoming a postman, because to carry the letter of Śrī Vyāsa takes great realization—a realization so deep that the insights of the *Vedas* may be presented not only chastely, but in such an appealing manner that even those with few material or cultural assets can relish and understand the imports.

Although all representatives of Śrī Vyāsa are glorious, sometimes in history, particular *Ācāryas* become noteworthy for profound demonstrations of their realization by communicating Vedic truths to those who even appear to be bereft of piety. For example, Śrī Nārada enlightened even the most aboriginal and sinful of hunters, and the *nāmācārya* Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura quickly elevated even a prostitute to the highest stage of perfection. A self-realized soul can therefore inspire devotional sentiments despite even gross impiety. And that is a mark of his realization.

Although such great spiritual teachers are rare, at times the Supreme Lord may call upon His servants to exhibit an even more profound demonstration of realization, by rewriting or expanding the Vedic literatures to accommodate the general lack of goodness and spiritual receptivity of humanity at large. The history of Śrī Vyāsa’s method of presenting the *Vedas* is an exhibition of such an extraordinary realization. Seeing that the residents of Kali-yuga would not be able to understand the original *Vedas,* which are meant for those with exceptional qualities of goodness, he began to edit them. By dividing the original *Vedas* into four, and enhancing key philosophical points with examples from Vedic history (the *Mahābhārata),* he tried to make Vedic concepts available to those who ordinarily wouldn't have the capacity to do so. The greatest manifestation of his realization, though, remained his expanded commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra,* the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* where he was able to present, in an easily understandable manner, a progression of Vedic truths culminating in the summum bonum of all knowledge, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

But among all great *ācāryas,* the realization or ability to communicate Vedic truths was most exceptionally demonstrated in the preaching of Śrīla Prabhupāda. If the Vedic culture is the receptive environment where the seed *of bhakti* can best fructify, then perhaps no *ācārya* before had met such opposition to devotion, for Śrīla Prabhupāda would plant the seed of *bhakti* in a society where even the basic concepts of human decency were out of norm. And not only was he constantly successful in making a culture of purity and restraint relishable to the most indulgent materialist, but like Vyāsa he would expand the Vedic literature for common understanding, and, even more astounding, at a time when the mass of humanity had sunk to new depths of cultural degeneration and spiritual ineptitude.

Ironically, one of the best expressions of Śrīla Prabhupāda's unique standard of realization was by a critical Godbrother who sought to minimize his work. He stated that he also went to the West and could have achieved what Śrīla Prabhupāda had done, but "didn't have the mentality to preach to the hippies." Trying to belittle. Śrīla Prabhupāda, he was in fact indicating Prabhupāda's greatness, for the "mentality to preach to the hippies" was certainly not indicative of any lack of sophistication entailed in preaching to those of lower status, but of an understanding of the Absolute Truth that was so fully realized that he could take the most sublime and advanced realms of thought and, without an inch of compromise, make them irresistible to all.

Dhanurdhāra Swami

## Dyutidhāra Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī kṛṣṇa caitanya prabhu nityānanda*  
*śrī advaita gadādhara śrīvasādi-gaura-bhakta vṛnda*

*hare kṛṣṇa, hare kṛṣṇa, kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa, hare hare*  
*hare rāma, hare rāma, rāma rāma, hare hare*

*Dear Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

OUR LOTUS FEET are always in motion, traversing the *brahmāṇḍas*. In kindness you appeared to us in a place of torment and despair to end our suffering. Thinking of you is like regaining life—seeing you with your *karatālas* in hand, chanting "Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava," as the whole world nods and sways in response. You are delivering the shimmering forms of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa to the soul, awakening the real senses within this dull body. I know you have the answer to every question, and the vision of eternity. You have laboriously recorded every detail of spiritual reality, and you have revealed the topmost love at Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

We who are aspiring to serve you are always feeling completely dependent on your mercy, which is factually all we are made of. Before coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness we were living only in the reflection of reality. It took your love to cause us to want to be free of that fearful state, a love of courage and fearlessness to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Being Kṛṣṇa's pure devotee, you perceive only His presence everywhere. Kṛṣṇa is always guiding your every thought and action; therefore you live in Vaikuṇṭha. You are the topmost confident of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and only you could have declared such a bold attack on *māyā*.

Please never stop preaching the sublime doctrine of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* to my disabled mind and heart, and allow us to some day fully recover our love of Kṛṣṇa. By your great kindness, allow us to always serve you, thereby giving some small value to this faulty lifetime. Begging to always have the shelter of your lotus feet.

*Your negligent disciple,*  
Dyutidhāra Swami

## Gaṇapatī Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my fallen obeisances in the sacred dust of your lotus feet.

Once again you have bestowed your benediction upon me by compelling me to cast all else aside and, meditating on your transcendental form and *līlā,* attempt to elicit from my callous heart some expression of appreciation and gratitude to Your Divine Grace. And once again I find myself torn between a myriad of *anarthas: jvara* (reluctance and lethargy), *prathiṣṭhāśā* (desire for name and fame amongst the devotees), and *kuṭi-nati* (fault-finding and diplomatic behavior). Apparently my only impetus to write this offering is to impress your disciples and granddisciples by some display of scholarship and eloquence or to opportunistically level some criticism against existing ISKCON attitudes or policies. Obviously, my concern should rather be to impress Your Divine Grace with a Kṛṣṇa conscious attitude befitting a true disciple of yours and to introspectively examine and reveal my own deficiencies with the expectation that you may mercifully rid me of them. While I was contemplating this task and feeling helpless to execute it, you sympathetically guided my attention to your own explanation of what mentality an advanced disciple of yours should naturally manifest. As you have inspired and inaugurated my devotional life and taught me everything I know—how to eat, how to sleep, how to bathe, how to dress, how to speak, how to worship, how to preach, how to surrender, and how to love—you have similarly revealed to me how to aspire to become and maintain myself as your dedicated servant. I would here like to refer to some nectarean instructions you imparted in Bombay, in 1976, regarding the disciple's eternal relationship with the spiritual master, and I pray that you kindly help me to come to this standard.

Śrīla Prabhupāda: "Everyone is independent—*yathecchasi tathā kuru.* But one who wants to be liberated, he has to surrender. That is voluntary. Otherwise everyone is free to do whatever he likes. Those who are surrendered souls, they'll wait for the instruction of *guru* and do accordingly—that is the process. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho.* That is the process. Otherwise everyone can remain independent. All living beings are independent. Even if I say that you do not do it, you can do it. Even Kṛṣṇa gives independence to Arjuna—*yathecchasi tathā kuru.* 'I have told you everything. Now do whatever you like.' So that depends on the candidate. Everyone is free to do anything, but if he is actually serious, then he has to do—*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho."*

**Devotee:**"What prompts the soul to misuse his independence?"

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** "Because he has got little independence—*yathecchasi tathā kuru.* He has got."

**Devotee:** "Why do some misuse it and some never misuse it?"

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:**"That depends on him. If one is determined that 'I shall only act according to the advice of my Guru Mahārāja,' then he is perfect. One has to submit, like Arjuna says, *śiṣyas te 'haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam.* Otherwise he will argue."

**Devotee:**"Even *nitya-siddha* has *guru.* Even the liberated souls—*nitya-siddha."*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:**"A liberated soul never says that 'I am liberated.' As soon as he says liberated, he's a rascal. A liberated soul will never say that 'I am liberated.' That is liberated. Caitanya Mahāprabhu—He's God—*gum more mūrkha dekhi' karila śāsana—*'My Guru Mahārāja saw Me fool number one, and he has chastised Me.' That is the example. If one remains always a servant, everlasting, *of guru,* then he is liberated, and as soon as he thinks that he is liberated, he's a rascal. That is the teaching of Caitanya Mahāprabhu—*guru more mūrkha dekhi'.* Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is *mūrkha*? Why He is passing Himself as *mūrkha*?—'I am fool number one.' That means that he's liberated. He must be ready always to be chastised by *guru. Then* he is liberated. And as soon as he thinks that 'I am beyond this chastisement, I am liberated,' he's a rascal. Why Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *guru more mūrkha dekhi' karila śāsana*? This is *sahajiyā.* He is enjoyer—'I have become liberated. I don't require direction from my *guru—*I am liberated.' Then you fall down. Why this Gauḍīya Math fell? Because they tried to become more than *guru.* Before passing away, he gave all direction and never said that this man should be next *ācārya.* But these people, just after passing away, they began to fight, 'Who shall be *ācārya*? That is the failure. They never thought, 'Why Guru Mahārāja gave us instruction so many things, why he did not say that this man should be *ācārya*? They wanted to create artificially somebody *ācārya* and everything failed. They did not consider even with common sense that if Guru Mahārāja wanted to appoint somebody as *ācārya,* why did he not say? He said so many things—and this point he missed—the real point? Let them declare some unfit person to become *ācārya,* then another man comes, and another and another. So better remain a foolish person perpetually to be directed by Guru Mahārāja. That is perfection. And as soon as they learned that Guru Mahārāja is dead—'Now I am so advanced that I can kill my *guru* and then become . . .' "

All glories to Your Divine Grace and your divine instructions!

*Your aspiring everlasting servant,*  
Gaṇapatī dāsa Swami

## Giridhāri Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**"We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the dangerous ocean of Kali, which destroys all the good qualities of a human being." (*****Bhāg.*** **1.1.22)**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance.

When I reflect on all that you are, and all that you have mercifully given us, it becomes so crystal clear that you alone are the "captain of the ship" providing passage for the conditioned souls of this age to cross over the ocean of material existence.

To the amazement of the whole world, you single-handedly implanted the Vedic culture in lands which were thought to be barren. Against all odds, you distributed Kṛṣṇa's holy name, prepared a library of transcendental knowledge to guide mankind for the next ten thousand years, erected beautiful temples for the glorification of the Lord, and provided unlimited devotional service for anyone and everyone who desires to partake. These and many other unparalleled feats were accomplished by you in Kṛṣṇa's service, always for the benefit of others, never for your personal enjoyment.

There is no meaning to the word "ISKCON" separate from you. By definition you have said that "ISKCON is my body, and the BBT is my heart." When will we take your words seriously? You said that "Your love for me will be shown by how well you cooperate together." When, as a society, will we actually put aside all of our personal considerations, ambitions, and desires, and simply do as you have requested? Complete success will only come if we work together to fulfill your innermost desire of DISTRIBUTING BOOKS—and thereby Kṛṣṇa consciousness—to each and every conditioned soul within this material world. We beg you to please help us develop the purity, compassion, and maturity required to follow your instructions and push forward this movement.

So on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, I fervently pray to have the benediction of executing my each and every action with the clear realization that you are personally present before me. In this way only will I truly understand what it means to follow you and be of some small assistance to your glorious ISKCON mission.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Giridhāri Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

*Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept this humble offering on this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance.

My prayer is to be faithful,  
To start this life anew,  
By fixing up my mind  
On only pleasing you.  
Embracing all surrender,  
Painful as it seems,  
Living for your service,  
To help fulfill your dreams.  
The order of your guru  
Always on your mind,  
Life and soul you gave us,  
So we could also find  
Your Lord, whose name could lift us  
Beyond the false ideal  
That pleasure of the senses  
Is tangible and real.

And we must give to others  
What you have given us:  
The śāstra in succession,  
Your purports as the thrust.  
You changed the hearts of millions,  
Which no one else could do.  
So with your books we'll follow,  
Until this age is through.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Today, on this most auspicious occasion of the celebration of your appearance within this world, I am intensely feeling separation from you, remembering your pastimes amongst us.

For a brief moment only you appeared at the threshold of my life, an uninvited but most welcome guest, who upon entering became my dear-most friend, as you changed the course of my destiny from an endless repetition of births and deaths to the shelter of eternal service at your lotus feet.

My actual life began the day I met you; before that there was only the pretense of life in my great struggle for existence within this human race.Those first moments close to you passed quickly in the infancy of my spiritual life, but in your absence you have given me the strength to bear all trials and tribulations as I strive to become the proper disciple I know you want me to be. As time goes on, memories fade of all but the many years of service unto you: exactly half my life to date. . . and, may I pray, forever more.

With many pleasant summers turned to chilly autumn falls, I understand more clearly now, it's as you've always said—this world is not my real home, it only leads to death. O Prabhupāda! I see it's true, except for those who follow you beyond this shadow of eternal truth, to the transcendental realm where you now sport.

As each year unfolds and the seeds of your instructions manifest within my heart, I feel the strong grip of *māyā* letting go, and myself coming a little closer to the goal of unalloyed devotion unto your lotus feet. But, Prabhupāda, the road is long, and there are many dangers that lie between where I am now and where you are standing safely on the other shore.

So my prayer on this auspicious day is that you will continue watching over me, and chastise me if you see fit, to keep me free from lust and greed and all that is still in between my seeing you, as once before ... in the beginning, when you appeared for but a brief moment at the threshold of my life, my dearmost friend, eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jagatguru Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. On this auspicious day of your appearance it is very nice to be with your many disciples and remember your transcendental glories. As you often said, "I am always with my spiritual master." Similarly, although I am only a neophyte in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, by your grace I know that you are always there to guide me across this ocean of darkness and material suffering.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala -līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a rain cloud pours water on the forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master is extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master."**

It always gives me great pleasure to glorify your transcendental activities, and thus there is nothing else I would rather do. Everything is already done by you. Therefore I am simply like a newspaper boy distributing the news. Because your glories are enlivening to all living beings, both conditioned and liberated, I am sometimes given a little praise.

This past winter, while distributing your books in Hawaii, many people thanked me for the work I was doing. One little old lady even said that God would bless me. But I was most encouraged when a man said, "I met Prabhupāda once, and I am sure that he is very pleased that all these books are being distributed."

I would certainly be very happy if God would bless me, but what is more important is that you are pleased. Your presence is already God's blessing, and I know that to please you is the highest perfection of life.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"By the mercy of the spiritual master one is benedicted by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master no one can make any advancement. Therefore I should always remember my spiritual master, and at least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master."**

It gives me pain to see that some of your disciples have so easily forgotten your mercy and exalted position. If someone says that they are equal to you or greater than you, my mind and intelligence become disturbed, and I find it very difficult to tolerate. I can simply beg such fallen souls to give up this foolishness arid return to the shelter of your lotus feet.

I remember in 1976 you wrote me, in reply to my letter, that you liked my plans for traveling and distributing books. My methods have changed somewhat, from a simple van in India to an elaborate cultural exhibition touring the United States. Here I am, ten years later, doing the same thing. You approved of this traveling and preaching, especially for a *sannyāsī.* At that time some of my Godbrothers were criticizing me for my traveling. Now again even after so many years the same criticism is still there. But they do not know that it is your order for me to travel and preach, and therefore I can never give it up.

I do not expect to have many followers in this life, nor will I ever have a big position or very much money. You have already given me much more than all of that. You have given me the opportunity to serve you eternally. I am simply a beggar, and I stand before you. On bended knees, with folded hands, I simply beg you to give me the dust of your lotus feet. If one is so fortunate to achieve even a minute particle of the dust of your lotus feet, it is understood that he has achieved the greatest wealth, which can never be surpassed in this world or the next.

It may happen that someone will read or hear this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, but I do not expect that they will understand. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only you will understand my heart, because you are my eternal master and friend. I wish always to remain your servant because you are Jagad-guru and I am Jagad-guru dāsa. I am always desiring your mercy.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Jagatguru Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the most holy dust of your divine lotus feet. Once again we try to express our helplessness and inability to properly glorify Your Divine Grace. You performed so many wonderful pastimes during your manifested presence with us, and many books have been written and are being written in order to give your association to as many human beings as possible. Even one moment of your association is the most valuable thing, because you carry Kṛṣṇa in your heart. You awaken everyone to their original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The more we try to follow your footsteps and to carry on your mission, the more we can begin to understand your greatness. If you had simply translated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and given your purports, that alone would have been amazing. But you organized printing and distribution in many languages, and you established a society dedicated to the study and following of the principles of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* You created a shelter from the deteriorating influence of the Kali-yuga. Most wonderfully, you trained and inspired others to continue your mission.

You are the perfect ambassador of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Please give us your mercy to be your assistants. We are slowly advancing your mission in Japan, the land of the *śūnyavādīs.* Let us never forget your desire that every home have a full set of your books. Your books are the light, like the sun, to save the world from the dense darkness of Kali-yuga.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"Essential truth spoken concisely is true eloquence." (*****Cc. Ādi-līlā*****)**

"Books are the basis  
Preaching is the essence  
Purity is the force  
Utility is the principle"

Unblemished eloquence  
Complete understanding

You did display

Ours is to follow  
Not emphasize one line

O Founder-Ācārya

For building support

Prabhupāda’s pure mood
