Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

## Krishna Balarama Swami

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthā prakāsante mahātmanaḥ*

**"Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed."**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I am very glad to glorify you on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, for by your glorification one has an opportunity to associate with you. By your association even a fully contaminated person can be transformed into a pure devotee. Association with the spiritual master can be obtained through his instructions. It is said that the instructions of a pure devotee act like a volcano to a conditioned soul. When a volcano erupts, at that time, so many kinds of good (beneficial) and unwanted things come out from it. Similarly, when someone takes your instructions seriously, then your instructions work like the eruption of a volcano. This eruption brings out all the good and bad qualities of a person, whereupon he loses all the bad qualities, and the good qualities give benefit to others. This is your wonderful quality, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Unless one is a bona fide spiritual master, he cannot situate his followers on the transcendental platform. The disciple of a bona fide *guru* acts like the sunflower, which always likes to face the sun. When the sun turns, he also turns his face towards the sun. Similarly, the disciple who is serious to go to the spiritual world always follows his spiritual master in all circumstances, without problematic interpretation. Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *guror ājñāṁ avicāraṇīyām:* "The disciple who is serious to go back to Godhead should not add or deduct anything from the instructions of his *guru."* Only in this way one can advance in spiritual life. Otherwise his spiritual life is for a short time like a waning moon. *Punar mūṣika-bhāvaḥ.* Flourishing for a short time is not called flourishing, but rather failure, due to his not attaining the *sanātana-dhāma.* It is stated in the scriptures, *kṣurasya dhārā nihitā duratyayā / durgaṁ pathas tat kavayoḥ vadanti:* "The path of spiritual realization is very difficult. It is sharp, like a razor's edge. That is the opinion of the learned transcendentalists." So it is understood that if someone wants his spiritual life to be easy and sure, then he must follow his spiritual master's instructions as he received them.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have mercifully taught us how to realize the goal of human life. You are the personality who causes us to render devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You said that chanting can purify the heart and soul and take us back to Godhead. You also said that taking *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* frees the soul from *karma* and purifies one's own existence. As it is stated in the *śāstras, prasādam* is Kṛṣṇa's remnants and contains some of His saliva. If anyone happens to take such *prasādam,* then he surely achieves liberation from this material world; of this there is no doubt.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have brought us to the spiritual path. For this reason we are indebted to you. It is also stated in the *śāstras* that unless a disciple goes to the spiritual world, he is unable to fulfill the obligation to repay the debt to his spiritual master. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I really doubt my ability to reach the spiritual world. Unless you help me from within and without, I can only cry. As it is sung by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura—*gurudeva! kṛpā-bindu diyā koro ei dāse tṛṇā-pekhā ati hīna / karuṇā na hoile kāndiyā kāndiyā prāṇa nā rākhibo āra:*

"Gurudeva, please give to this servant just one drop of mercy. I am lower than a blade of grass. Give me all help. If you are not merciful unto me, I can only weep, and I will not be able to maintain my life." What else can I do? Śrīla Rupa Gosvāmī also states—*nitya-siddha kṛṣṇa-prema sādhya kabu naya:* "The spiritual path is eternally pure and perfect." When a devotee follows it nicely, he becomes purified. If he fails, however, then he becomes the loser. But the spiritual path remains the same. For an example, the tracks of a railroad allow so many trains to pass on them without being affected. If a train jumps the tracks, the train becomes destroyed, not the tracks. Similarly, if a devotee deviates from the raillike spiritual path, he becomes bereft of the spiritual destination, the ultimate goal.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could understand that you were very careful about establishing the ancient *paramparā* system. Your anxiety was to keep the purity of the Society unadulterated. Unless you yourself, now as a *caittya-guru,* take care of us, then it is practically impossible for us to go further. Then, *janme janme prabhu sei* has some sensible meaning. The spiritual master is said to be the external manifestation of the Supersoul. The merciful father like your kind self was sent by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa to help us continuously until we reach home, back to Godhead. A genuine disciple knows that after the physical absence of the spiritual master, the *guru* instructs as the Supersoul from within. Therefore this Vyāsa-pūjā day is an opportunity for us to beg for practical help from our *guru.* So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I helplessly beg for your kind mercy. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Krishna Balarama Swami

## Lokanātha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I was not looking for you. However, by the Lord's causeless mercy I found you. Due to my conditioning, sometimes I was considering you to be an ordinary person. Please forgive me. This mentality is being rectified as I realize how greatly I am being benefited as a result of your having entered into my life. Every day people are approaching us with such statements as, "Swamiji, there is no peace. I'm fed up . . ." and so on. Only because I met you am I spared from passing through the trials and tribulations which the whole world is passing through.

There are billions of contemporary fellow human beings around me on this planet, which include my dear father, mother, brothers, sisters, and friends. No one had offered any substantial help to me—but what can be expected from such illusioned and bewildered creatures? Therefore on this occasion of your ninety-first appearance day, I thank God, Lord Kṛṣṇa, for sending you to this world and showing me and many others the path by which to cross over this obnoxious material world.

Somehow or other, from my childhood I had within my heart a predominating desire to serve people. In spite of my good intentions to help others, I didn't know what to do or where to start this service to humanity. Your first lesson to us was that we are not these bodies but pure spirit souls, eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa. So I understood that serving the people meant serving the souls and engaging the souls in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Otherwise all endeavors are a useless waste of time and energy. All the materialists, communists, imperialists, so-called religionists—practically everyone on the list is trying to care only for the body, ignoring altogether the very basis of the body, the soul.

When you asked me to start bullock-cart *saṅkīrtana,* your guideline was that I should go to the villages and give people the taste of the holy name. Following your instructions now for almost two years, and assisted and inspired by many of your disciples and granddisciples, I am traveling from village to village, begging thousands and thousands of souls to embrace the holy name and be happy

Today, on this auspicious occasion of your divine appearance, I am humbly, with a straw in my mouth, begging for strength, determination, and all other essential ingredients required to continue this "every town and village" Padayātrā program for at least ten more years. That is, may I continue up until 1996, which coincides with your centennial birthday celebration. This Padayātrā will go to nooks and corners of India, and as much as possible to various countries outside of India. Wherever we go, we strongly desire to introduce you as the savior of the whole world. Lord Kṛṣṇa is in full command, and you are His very dear devotee. We pray that our little show of Padayātrā will be allowed to go on year after year after year.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am eternally indebted to you.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Lokanātha Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your greatness grows day by day as we continually realize how weak and worthless we are in comparison to you. In a time of rampant irreligion and gross sensualism, you incarnated to mend the links and reestablish the *vaiṣṇava-sampradāya* throughout the entire world. From New York to San Francisco, Montreal, Mexico City, London, Paris, Nairobi, Bombay, Fiji, Hong Kong, Sydney, Honolulu—everywhere—you introduced KṚṢṆA, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, to be known and loved through disciplic succession. Worldwide harmony, a spiritual culture to unify all people in all lands around the chanting of God's holy names (Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare) under one transcendental banner, ISKCON, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

The boldest, bravest preacher and lovable father to five thousand disciples, a moving touchstone amongst the masses, you transformed the dead into living, the misers into giving, and the loveless into loving.

You alone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, made Kṛṣṇa come alive and seem so believable. When you would conclude a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class by saying, "And if you just give this one life for serving Kṛṣṇa, you will go back to Godhead. I guarantee. You will go back to Godhead," we would burst out in loving unison "JAYA ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA!!!!" as we all felt our hearts crack in happiness and tears well up in our eyes.

You allowed us to love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you taught us how to give everything to Kṛṣṇa by surrendering unto you. We want always to feel surrendered to you and try to fulfill your desires. Although Kali-yuga challenges our determination to follow your program, and doubts and confusion compete within the mind to create speculations about what you really said or meant, with Vaiṣṇava humility we can see clearly that you wanted us to strictly follow all the rules and regulations of *sādhana-bhakti* without deviation or concoction.

We practically witness the immense strength contained within the regulated spiritual program you have given us for self-realization. However, under the influence of Māyā’s passionate kicks, we feel moved to change and devise a "new, improved" way to love Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so kindly given us *everything* we need to go back home, back to Godhead, in this very lifetime.

Please give us your blessings so we can become good leaders by becoming exemplary followers.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

*harer nāma harer nāma*  
*harer nāmaiva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva*  
*nāsty eva gatir anyathā*

**"In this age of Kali there is no alternative, there is no alternative, there is no alternative for spiritual progress than the holy name, the holy name, the holy name of the Lord." (Cc.** ***Ādi-līlā*** **7.76 \[*****Bṛhan-Nāradīya Purāṇa*****\])**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances. You alone brought the holy name and sat, chanted, and danced amongst us and so revived us from the condition of the living dead. You, representative of Vyasa, have written in your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā*, Chapter 7, texts 76-96) the story of how Lord Caitanya received the authority to spread the holy names. You have also written about your own attempt, which became so successful: "Believing in the words of His spiritual master. He \[Lord Caitanya\] introduced the *saṅkīrtana* movement, just as the present Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was started with belief in the words of our spiritual master. . . We believed in the words of our spiritual master and started in a humble way—in a helpless way—but due to the force of the order of the supreme authority, this movement has become successful."

Now some say, rather abstractly, that it was your "love" which unified even differing characters in your movement. But your love was not blind or sentimental. You lovingly gave us the only authorized process. You lovingly taught us to perform the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*. You built a house for the whole world to live in, and at its one hearth the fire of *saṅkīrtana*. You taught us to offer all "feasible goods," and even all *"varṇāśrama-dharma"* in the flames. Your house was nondifferent from the holy names, and by dancing and chanting we could easily enter in.

Every Māyāpur festival you would chant amidst *kīrtana* scenes of wild, ecstatic joy, going round and round with your family of junior and senior disciples, all united in the irresistible force of the holy names. By the force of that *kīrtana* our whole year was pervaded, preaching empires were won, obstacles overcome, and the future was always bright with the safe shelter of the holy name.

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your presence unmanifested, our positions secure, unmotivated, do we find no tangible profit to dance, chant, and jostle each other, united in the holy name?

Now, even after having traveled extensively thousands of miles, after having taken *prasādam* and rested, do we still find it too impractical for all of us leaders to all obey you and all enjoy cooperating, to all dance and sing all together, just to please you in the holy *dhāma*\]

Now are only those with nothing better to do confined to the *kīrtana* hall?

Now do we think that you will not be present when we all join in chanting and dancing together?

Now do we think that our problems have an origin in other than neglecting your most essential order to dance and chant together?

Now do we think that cooperating together to show our love for you means something other than all coming together twice a day, at least, to all become simply absorbed in dancing, chanting, and feasting?

Now do we, bureaucrats, look for unity in special boards and judiciary commissions rather than in the pure process you have taught us?

Now are we so faithless and cynical that, for musical reasons, economic reasons, health reasons, for fear of ego-loss, or for fear of touching sweating bodies, we cannot even annually dance, carefree, with all our "enemies" and "friends" for more than just the minimal respectably discreet few minutes?

Now shall we, even at the Māyāpur festival, forgetting your simple order, as sophisticated elders maintain our gorgeous profiles and stay aloof, above, in sedate apartments, and leave the actual serious business of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* to our juniors, disciples, and the uninitiated public below? Now do we, like retired pensioners, cowards, send others to lead the *kīrtana*?

Now shall we, as *gurus* ourselves, prefer only to dance and chant at home with our doting families, finding it too demeaning to dance and sing with awkward Godbrothers in the holy *dhāma!*

Now has the only authorized process of the *Bṛhan-Nāradīya Purāṇa* which you brought, Prabhupāda, been proved wrong, inefficient, outmoded, old-fashioned, uncouth, and minimized by the latest computer programs, debates, meetings, arguments, administrative structures, committees, papers, word juggleries, political considerations, and other formulas?

Now do we think that we can make some short-cut process to spiritual advancement and preaching success, without the need for us to all intensely chant congregationally together to remove the encrusted layers of accumulated conditioning around our immortal souls? Now, avoiding your instructions, do we want to just rubber-stamp our purity?

Now, without you physically there in the *kīrtana,* do we feel incapably weak to bridge psychological barriers between ourselves? Now have we become so dull that we cannot even see the hand of *māyā* at work?

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, do only naive simpletons and envious fanatics really believe that all our difficulties will be overcome when the prophesied flood of love of God flows out from the total *mahā-saṅkīrtana* of all of us dancing and chanting together in ecstasy in the holy *dhāma*? O Prabhupāda, I am certainly less intelligent, and certainly I am still an envious neophyte struggling to chant offenselessly, but I am still longing for that holy day when we all dance and chant together, brother to brother, father to son, uncle to nephew, cousin to cousin, face to face, eye to eye, limb to limb, heart to heart! Surely seeing all the obstacles to the flood of love of God being cleansed away in the consuming fire of such a *kīrtana* you will be pleased, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! I am a complainer who could achieve nothing significant for you. Still, somehow I am hanging on like a louse sucking your blood! Please bless me that I can become purified, so that I can set a good example myself and help to bring us all together in the ecstatic chanting and dancing process which you gave us!

Mahāviṣṇu Swami

## Nava Yogendra Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

HARE KRSNA!

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have very kindly spread the most ancient religion, *sanātana-dharma,* all over the world. You have given new light to millions of people around the world, and you are especially kind upon the most fallen souls like myself. Your kindness is the only hope for myself. Except for your mercy, I have no hope.

Today is your divine appearance day, the most blessed event for fallen disciples like myself. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you appeared on this earth to save fallen humanity, and you have actually and miraculously done this by giving the real way of life, the Kṛṣṇa conscious way. Simply by hearing and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, all perfection is realized by even the lowest of men. No one could be lower than me, because while you were personally here, I did not fully take advantage of your short stay in this world and serve you as I should have. I never took seriously the obvious fact that you could not stay with us in this temporary, material world. Now I can only lament that I did not serve you nicely.

At any rate, this is not the day for lamentation but for joyous remembrance that you did appear directly from the spiritual world and performed so many gloriously transcendental activities in such a short span of time. There is no one in the world who will ever be able to challenge that you are not a specifically empowered incarnation, the mercy incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Because of your strong desire to preach the great message of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, all the credit belongs to you alone for the amazing success of establishing this great moment in such a big way all over the world. And because of your absolutely spotless character, all sincere seekers of true spiritual life will no longer be bewildered by so many cheating *gurus* and so-called spiritualists. You alone have set the standard, Śrīla Prabhupāda, never to be imitated but always to be followed strictly.

Numerous times you have instructed all of us how important it is for us to follow your perfect example of executing the rules and regulations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The following letter from Your Divine Grace to Śrīmān Gopījanavallabha dāsa on September 19, 1974, gives a nice indication of your desire in this regard:

"Upon your recommendation I am initiating the following disciples. You must see that they strictly follow the regulative principles, the four prohibitions as well as the devotional practices of rising early, taking morning bath, putting on tilaka, attending mangal artik, chanting japa sixteen rounds, and attending *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class. This is the duty of all my initiated disciples, whether they are big or small. Everyone is a devotee. So everyone is expected to follow the devotional practices. Here in Vṛndāvana I am enjoying my disciples how they are speaking. So two times daily everyone must attend the classes. During the morning and evening times there should be no business, simply *kīrtana.* chant, dance, and be purified. Do not be *paramahaṁsa* that now I haven't got to hear *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā.* Don't be a better *paramahaṁsa* than your Guru Mahārāja. I go and sit and I enjoy the lectures and how they are performing *kīrtana.* So as I am doing you must also do."

As your eternal disciple I pray for the strength to always be enthusiastic about these basic practices, which are the solid foundation on which you built this great society of devotees. Without these practices I know that *māyā* can easily drag even the most advanced man of discrimination to a very abominable condition. Unfortunately, as you well know, we have too much experience of this since your departure from our physical perception.

I further pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you will continue to be kind to me and bless me with the strength to preach more and more vigorously. I already have so much personal experience of success in preaching the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu in various parts of the world, and I know that it is only by your mercy, my beloved Guru-deva. Please continue to use me as an instrument for carrying your pure message and divine teachings to sincere souls everywhere.

When I met Your Divine Grace, I was inspired by your purity and dedication to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and I knew that you were my eternal spiritual master. I sincerely desired to serve your most beautiful lotus feet. As a *sannyāsī* I am trying do this. I am daily praying that I may continue to strictly follow all the instructions that you have given both personally to me and through your most beautiful and sublime books.

I also pray that you will use me as an instrument for inspiring others to seriously take up your divine and most sublime instructions. My vow to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that if you will use me in this way, I will continue to preach at all cost, even at the cost of my own life. If it is your desire that I be disappointed by not having any similar-minded association, or that I be abused, cheated, lied to, threatened, beaten physically, stolen from, or whatever, then that will be my happiness. My only desire is that you please use me somehow or other to preach in this life and in all the future lives that I may have.

I cannot properly thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me your divine mercy and for giving me a purpose for living.

Helplessly I am simply begging again and again to remain,

*Your eternal servant,*  
Nava Yogendra Swami

## Nirañjana Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

When I think of you it brings me hope,  
For within those thoughts you are  
Personally present to encourage me  
And keep me from drifting far.  
But due to māyā’s influence  
I oftentimes will drift.  
It weakens me to forget your words,  
Your plans, your form, your gifts.

My only hope is to chant your glories  
So everyone I meet can hear  
Of the greatest personality,  
Whose teachings are so clear.

Just as clouds during autumn season  
Cover the evening sky,  
The stars, the moon cannot be seen.  
You'd think that they'd know why.

But foolish men will look up and see  
The glowworms in the trees,  
And they'll think these little creatures  
Are real luminaries.

So in this age of Kali  
They trust politicians more than you;  
Even atheistic scientists  
Have won their favor too.

Our duty is to awaken them  
By introducing them to you,  
For it's by your pure example  
That Kṛṣṇa comes to view.

Just one contact with Kṛṣṇa  
Will give their lives a chance  
For going back to Godhead,  
Where all walk is found as dance.  
So now I must become pure too  
In bringing your words to them,  
And now that chance to cleanse my heart  
Is available again.

Each moment brings a chance for me  
To remember what you desire.  
To take this homage one step more  
I must jump into the fire.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
Please keep pushing me to preach  
The teachings of Lord Caitanya  
To everyone I meet.

*Begging to remain your eternal servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Although we are Lord Kṛṣṇa's eternal servants, we, the conditioned souls in this material world, are imagining ourselves to have many illusory identities. Thus we fight amongst ourselves to gain prominence in this material world, trying to enjoy temporary pleasures in a world of birth and death. But fortunately we have the opportunity to relish your nectarean association in the form of your transcendental literature, tapes, films about you, and the good example of your devotees. Indeed, you have declared that ISKCON is your transcendental body.

An important part of your glories is your followers, those who live by your transcendental instructions. Therefore, I pray for the intelligence to enable me to become the servant of your servants. Please grant me the humility to serve them, and the transcendental discrimination to do it properly. As you are always seeing Kṛṣṇa everywhere and in everything and are serving Him with transcendental love, let me bow down in service to your lotus feet and the lotus feet of your followers and lovingly serve them with enthusiasm, confidence, and patience.

Please make me an instrument to glorify Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and Their followers by giving me the ability to expand the Kṛṣṇa conscious culture around the world, especially by printing and distributing your literature and by making my life nondifferent from your orders.

*Your servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rādhānātha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I am not a poet, a prolific writer or an eloquent speaker, but I have a sincere desire to glorify you on this auspicious day of Vyāsa-pūjā.

All devotional qualities are fully embodied in every aspect of your transcendental personality. Your pure devotion to *guru* permeates the entire world. It especially pervades the hearts of those followers who always remain loyal to your words. You have given us simple guidelines which can never be given up: rise early in the morning, bathe, attend *maṅgala-ārati,* chant sixteen rounds, follow the four principles, read your books, and humbly serve your servants. These simple instructions contain your all-powerful mercy, which alone can protect us from the uncountable allurements of *māyā.* Please give me the strength to never fall away from your lotus feet by neglecting these principles. By remaining true to your basic teachings and preaching the same, we can truly glorify you, who are all-glorious. You have divinely empowered many devotees to glorify your mercy in wonderful ways. Kīrtanānanda Swami has constructed your beautiful Palace and New Vrindaban, making your glories known throughout the world. Bhagavān dāsa Goswami is distributing millions of your books. Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami and Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami are writing transcendental literature describing your glories. Harikeśa Swami is printing your books and preaching your glories in the most dangerous lands. Bhaktitīrtha Swami is converting heads of state to be your followers. These great souls, and many others, are munificently expanding your unlimited glories around the world. I am very small, incapable, and unfortunate, but I have one prayer that gives me solace: I pray that you pick up this useless life with your lotus hands and do with it as you wish.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāma Govinda Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
On this auspicious day of your appearance, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, let me fall flat at your divine lotuslike feet, offering to you respectful *dandavatas.*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In your Bhaktivedanta purport to the *Antya-līlā* of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have given me the goal of my *sannyāsa* life: "In fact, a Vaiṣṇava accepts the *sannyasa* order to remain an eternal servant of his spiritual master. He accepts the *sannyāsa* order knowing that he is unequal to his spiritual master, who is a *paramahaṁsa.* Therefore a Vaiṣṇava accepts *sannyāsa* out of humility, not out of pride." (Cc. *Antya-līlā* 13.61)

Also, in your description of how Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu reclaimed the leper Vāsudeva, you have reminded us of our fallen condition and instructed us to remain humble, and that our duty is to preach.

*kintu āchilāṅ bhāla adhama hañā*  
*ebe ahaṅkāra mora janmibe āsiyā*

**"Being meek and humble, the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **Vāsudeva worried that he would become proud after being cured by the grace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu."**

prabhu kahe,—"kabhu tomāra nā habe abhimāna  
nirantara kaha tumi 'kṛṣṇa' 'kṛṣṇa' nāma

"To protect the *brahmana,* Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu advised him to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* incessantly. By doing so, he would never become unnecessarily proud."

*kṛṣṇa upadeśī kāra jīvera nistāra*  
*acirāte kṛṣṇa tomā karibena aṅgīkāra"*

**"Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu also advised Vāsudeva to preach about Kṛṣṇa and thus liberate living entities. As a result, Kṛṣṇa would very soon accept him as His devotee."**

"Although Vāsudeva Vipra was a leper and had suffered greatly, still Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu cured him. The only return the Lord wanted was that Vāsudeva preach the instructions of Kṛṣṇa and liberate all human beings. That is the process of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Each and every member of this Society was rescued from a very abominable condition, but now they are engaged in preaching the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They are not only cured of the disease called materialism but are also living a very happy life. Everyone accepts them as great devotees of Kṛṣṇa, and their qualities are manifest in their very faces. If one wants to be recognized as a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, he should take to preaching work, following the advice of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Then one will undoubtedly attain the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya, Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, without delay." (Cc. *Madhya-līlā* 7.146-148)

Therefore, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as a dog or servant is very satisfied to get a competent perfect master, or as a child is completely satisfied to get a competent father, we are satisfied simply by engaging in your transcendental service. We thereby know that we have a competent master, competent to save us from all kinds of danger in this material existence, and therefore we have come to the guaranteed protection of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we are being freed from material anxiety. This life of anxiety is called material existence. To be completely satisfied and devoid of anxiety, we must come to the position of eternally rendering service at your lotus feet in your mission.

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraṁ*  
*praśānta-nihśeṣa-mano rathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

**"By serving You constantly, one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall I engage as Your permanent eternal servant and always feel joyful to have such a fitting master?" (Cc.** ***Madhya-līlā*** **1.206)**

Rāma Govinda Swami

## Romapada Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Dependent. We cannot wait  
Life's goal to show its face.  
Impatient, just to catch a glimpse!

Your glory in its place.  
Truth echoing throughout space  
Illumines like blazing mace—  
Your words! Follow them! Follow them!

Again and again the signal comes,  
Time caring not to wait  
While we prepare, elusive still.

Fools only hesitate.  
Forewarned: Avoid ill fate!  
Beyond duality's love and hate—  
Your words! Such overwhelming strength!

Our guide, most merciful Prabhupāda,  
Father, shelter, master.  
All souls to be delivered home!

How did we come to fall? No!  
Deviate not from the call!  
Cast aside mind's wicked brawl!  
Your mission—our life and soul.

Like sparks in darkness by your will transformed,  
Life s meaning by eagerness attain,  
Unblemished ācārya, your word and deed.

Incapable to feign  
Nor cheap words explain  
Who else can claim?  
Spiritual monarch, you reign!  
Your light brings us embers ablaze.

This is your grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your will, your order is for all to reach full devotional quality of life, also. May we not waver from your will. Only by the strength of your will. This day we pray to cling ever more firmly, with love, to your desire for us to attain your full mercy.

To serve you faithfully according to your standard is to be with you. I want to serve you ever more favorably, Śrīla Prabhupāda, more than anything. Please keep me with you always.

Romapada Swami

## Smita Krishna Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. On this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance day I thank you for all that you have given me, a most insignificant servant of your lotus feet.

In confirmation of my relationship with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was awarded *sannyāsa* by your disciple and my Godbrother Harikeśa Swami on the sacred occasion of Lord Caitanya's five-hundredth anniversary this year. This is a vow to serve you with all my mind, body, and words, at all times and in all circumstances. That means to be fully dependent on you always. Sometimes I hear the expression of being an "independent-thinking Prabhupāda disciple." That might sound lofty and good in these times of independence, but for my own self I prefer to try to qualify as a disciple fully dependent on you for my thinking and acting. That way I will naturally be fully independent of any other ideas or influences and thus safe from all of *māyā’s* illusions.

That is the glory of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you have freed me from this material nature. Therefore I wish to declare my full dependence on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this sacred occasion. Let me stay faithful to your instructions at every moment.

At no stage of our progress on this path of devotion can we ever consider ourselves independent of our spiritual master. That will ruin our spiritual life. And you are so wonderful that in spite of my only having just a few moments of association with you, still you have the potency to save me and keep me alive on the path of devotion. That proves how wonderful just a moment's association with a pure devotee is. That moment of association can open the doors of liberation for the conditioned soul. You gave your association not only to me but to the whole world. Your short time of bold preaching while you were present here was enough to save this whole planet earth. Provided we stay faithful to your instructions, we will be able to carry on the work you started to save the whole world.

Therefore I again bow down at your divine lotus feet and beg to forever stay your humble servant.

Smita Krishna Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the spreading of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

The last time I had your *darśana,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, was during the summer of 1977 at Bhaktivedanta Manor, in England. During that time you came to the West for the last time, and although your health was failing, you spent your last Janmāṣṭamī and Vyāsa-pūjā appearance day celebration with us there. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

No one thought of being *guru.* The thought was unthinkable. You were the center of our meditation, and without you we could not even maintain our life. One morning we learned that you had been very sick the night before, and around 10 a.m. an ambulance came to rush you to the hospital. I remember them carrying you out on a stretcher, and how we all prayed to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva and Rādhā-Gokulānanda for hours together: *tava kara-kamala-vare nakham adbhuta-śṛṅgam . . .* the prayer you taught us to protect the spiritual master. We had no other thoughts, no other anxieties, other than for your welfare. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

And yet, though minor surgery was performed, you would not stay overnight in the hospital. You returned to Bhaktivedanta Manor, and within a few days you again allowed us to carry you down on your palanquin to have *darśana* of Their Lordships Rādhā-Gokulānanda and Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā. You had no faith in the useless *karmī* doctors, only faith in Kṛṣṇa and *guru,* and by your personal example you showed us how to take shelter of the Lord. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

The question arose: When was Janmāṣṭamī? Our calendar, put out by Pradyumna, said it was one day in the West and a different day in India. By astrological calculation he had worked out the world in time zones, and those who lived West of a particular time zone would observe it on the following day. Seeing the confusion, you said the calculation was "over-intelligent," and whatever day it was celebrated on in India, it should be celebrated the same day all over the world. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today, when you would personally dispel all of our doubts and confusion and bring light to the darkened world in this troubled age of Kali.

I remember how thin you looked, wearing your dark glasses even while having *darśana* of the Lord. Never before had I seen you look so weak in physical strength! You had to be carried from the car to the palanquin when you first arrived. The thought that you might not always be physically here haunted us in the back of our minds. O Prabhupāda! Don't leave us! We are drowning in an ocean of material desires. If you leave us here to rot in this material world, how will we ever be delivered, and what to speak of all of the fallen conditioned souls? "His Divine Grace has instructed me to inform you that he is very pleased with your sentiments," Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja wrote me in the last months. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

On your appearance day, which we are celebrating today, you graciously agreed to sit through the ceremonies in the temple room. Your Vyāsāsana had to be pushed back to make room for the palanquin, and you listened intently as Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja gave an address glorifying how you came to the West on the order of your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda. On one or two occasions you stopped him to correct a date or minor detail here and there, but you seemed pleased with his speech of appreciation. We followed with *guru-pūjā* and a wonderful *kīrtana,* which you appeared to enjoy very much. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

During that visit, you called for all of the devotees to come to your room, and we packed into your *darśana* room to see you. You sat propped up with some pillows behind your low-lying desk, and someone brought you a huge tray filled with cut fruits. You took one small piece and asked that the tray be passed around to all of us, and as the room fell silent, one might have been able to hear a pin drop. "So," you said, your weak but deep voice speaking with confidence, assurance, conviction, "is everyone happy?" "JAYA, ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA!" we exuberantly shouted, tears coming from our eyes. "JAYA!" Yes, Prabhupāda, we are happy! You have made us happy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and without you we would be rotting in this miserable material world. You are our only solace, our only reason for living, our life and soul! Are we happy? O hear me, conditioned souls! Give up your petty pursuits and just surrender to Śrīla Prabhupāda! Take our hand and let us lead you all to his lotus feet! Just try to understand the nature of the divine love, the eternal relationship between the *guru* and the disciple. Give up your envy, your reluctance, your miserable temporary plans, and just surrender to him! My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

You kindly stayed with us for three weeks or so. The weather had never been so warm, so sunny for England. You went to see Rādhā-Londonīśvara, your favorite Deities, and then you returned to your eternal home, Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma. You bathed us all in the nectar of your association, and you kindly looked upon all of us, especially in the last few months. You taught us that we should always preach, even if we are dying, and you taught us that the next stage of your mission, the establishment of *varṇāśrama-dharma,* was still to come. You taught us never to give up the fight against *māyā,* and you taught us how to increase our transcendental affection and dedication to you. My mind goes to that time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nine years ago today.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! May you always continue to remain within our hearts. Just as you many times told us, "I never felt separation from my Guru Mahārāja," so let us always remain eternally in your association by following in your transcendental footsteps. Thus we may never feel too much separation-from Your Divine Grace, A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*Your fallen, useless, miserly beggar disciple,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, to your auspicious appearance day, and to your International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

You are expert, Śrīla Prabhupāda, at everything. You are the perfect scholar, the perfect businessman, the perfect poet and writer, the perfect diplomat, the perfect cook and *pūjārī,* the perfect teacher—in sum, the perfect pure devotee of the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa. In any position, at any time, we are but your imperfect assistants.

Today we are especially contemplating your expertise at playing the two kinds of *mṛdaṅga.* You explained many times that the *mṛdaṅga* drum devotees traditionally use to accompany their chanting of the holy names on the streets and in temples, although a transcendental instrument, can be heard for only a few blocks at best. You said, on the other hand, that the printing press is *bṛhat mṛdaṅga,* "the big *mṛdaṅga,"* because printed glorification of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees can resound worldwide and for years and centuries into the future.

As for the first kind *of mṛdaṅga,* there are recordings of your *bhajanas* and *kīrtanas,* and just the sound of your hands tapping and slapping the clay-headed drum—simple, familiar beats, but like no one else has ever played—is enough to transport a listener to Vaikuṇṭha, the spiritual world.

Your books, too, are beautiful recordings of your playing of the *mṛdaṅga—*the *bṛhat mṛdaṅga.* Your book *kīrtanas* will resound for ten thousand years, lighting up this dark Kali-yuga, energizing a brilliant worldwide Kṛṣṇa conscious culture.

You have left to ISKCON these big and small *mṛdaṅgas,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, and as you used them to glorify your spiritual master and to distribute Lord Caitanya's mercy, so we must use them to glorify Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace. You have even left to ISKCON your personal *bṛhat mṛdaṅga,* this *Back to Godhead* magazine, and we can see nothing more fitting than our ensuring that this particular *mṛdaṅga* resound monthly (for the next 12 x 10,000 months) with glorification of your transcendental personality and of your ever-enlightening Bhaktivedanta purports.

We see the *Back to Godhead mṛdaṅga* simply as an advertisement for you and your books. In addition to the pages of actual advertisements, the articles themselves are attempts by your disciples and granddisciples to express their realization and appreciation of the written treasure of knowledge you have bequeathed the world. Devotees who put themselves forward to give your books to conditioned souls on the street, in classrooms, and at our temples may also put themselves forward in the pages of *Back to Godhead,* with all the advantages that this *bṛhat mṛdaṅga* affords.

As members of the *Back to Godhead* staff, we humbly pray that we may assist your ISKCON to vigorously beat upon the *Back to Godhead mṛdaṅga.*

*Your lowly servants at BTG,*

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our unlimited obeisances at your lotus feet. It is described in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī was capable of entering into the hearts of all living beings to inspire them toward pure devotional service. Similarly, anyone who is trying to follow the instructions found in your books is able to factually experience your unlimited mercy and guidance from within the heart. Thus you are truly the spiritual master of the entire universe.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has instructed that "he reasons ill" who says that a Vaiṣṇava has died, for the Vaiṣṇavas live eternally in their instructions. We at the Bhaktivedanta Institute particularly realize and relish the truth of your presence in your instructions. Preaching to the scientific and intellectual class of men is one of your dearmost projects. Although we are unqualified, as we try to humbly execute your desire and vision, we see that wonderful results are being manifested. This is due only to the potency of your pure instructions. You have instructed us to simply go on preaching to the scholars: "I will send all the men and money you want." Śrīla Prabhupāda, during the World Congress, we realized how at every step you are personally behind your instructions to ensure success. Over twelve hundred delegates from India and around the world attended the congress and went back home with your books. These scholars so thoroughly experienced the spirit of devotional service that one delegate from Britain even remarked, "These four days we have felt that we were in a different world!" Many other equally favorable comments were expressed by a group of foreign delegates who visited Vṛndāvana after the congress. The congress has been a major step towards introducing the scholars to the sophisticated culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is only by your blessings that we have been able to make this loving offering to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu on the occasion of the five-hundredth anniversary of His appearance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have said, "The Bhaktivedanta Institute has the greatest responsibility to enhance the prestige of ISKCON." We realize that this can be done only by our undertaking daring projects of international reach. On this auspicious day of your transcendental appearance, we reaffirm our one-hundred-percent commitment to serve your desire and vision. Please give us, O master, the courage and determination to build the greatest institute of all times, which will be a veritable tree of transcendental knowledge, providing shelter for all the scientists and scholars. We wish to develop an international headquarters for the Bhaktivedanta Institute so that it will become the foremost institute of our times for acquiring true scientific knowledge. Kindly guide us in this mission to deliver the divine message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the fallen intellectuals of Kali-yuga.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have already delivered the treasure of love of Godhead to everyone freely. Our duty is only to inspire them to relish this treasure. You have verily poured a great rain of mercy upon this planet. May the sun of *bhāgavata* science in the form of the Bhaktivedanta Institute shine through in the wake of this rain, making the rainbow of love of God manifest in the sky of all scientists' hearts, so that they may, coming out into the field of devotional service, dance and sing your glories eternally.

O lord and master, please kindly give us only this benediction—that our endeavors be worthy of your vision and our surrender to your desires complete.

*Your servants from the Bhaktivedanta Institute*

*(Bombay, Tirupati, San Francisco),*

## Bhaktivedanta Institute of Religion and Culture

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to you on your appearance day!

Your appearance in this world was at a certain point in time, but the appearance of your mercy is never-ending and is available at all times to your sincere followers. In *The Nectar of Devotion* you relate to us Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī's key instruction that is the basis of, and the essential purpose behind, all laws governing devotional service to Kṛṣṇa: Always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. It is due to your appearance that we know Kṛṣṇa at all. Therefore, only by always remembering you and never forgetting you can we follow this ultimate instruction.

There are many ways in which you have not allowed us to forget your lotus feet. I reflect upon some of the countless times you made me remember you in the midst of my educational preaching. Once, as a teaching assistant for a required course on India at Harvard, I was watching a slide show in a large lecture hall which was filled with 370 students. Suddenly, the effulgent forms of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa Balarāma at your Vṛndāvana temple illuminated the screen, transporting me for a moment to Vṛndāvana. Another time, when I was teaching a course on the religious traditions of the world, one of my students approached me after class. Asking me if I had "ever seen this book before," he brought forth his own copy of *The Science of Self-Realization.* As I gazed at your glorious picture, I said that I had indeed seen the book before and recommended it highly. And then there was the time, many years ago, when I nervously arrived very early to the room where a crucial Sanskrit exam was to be given. A proctor, who administers exams, was the only other person there. She asked me, "Do you know Sanskrit?" "We'll soon find out," I answered anxiously. She then remarked, "The only Sanskrit I know is 'Hare Krishna'!" In these ways you have repeatedly reminded me of Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the greatness of your teaching becomes more and more apparent as I study in depth so many theories and philosophies of religion. The more I learn of these other systems of thought, the more I become appreciative and take shelter of the sweetness of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* and its incomparably sublime principles and practices. You explain that *bhakti* is not unique to Vaiṣṇavism, and that it can be found in other religious traditions. But you demonstrate that there is no other tradition that develops true theism and devotion to God as completely, fully, and purely as Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava philosophy does. You have presented the ripened fruit of theism as Lord Caitanya's teachings, wherein you describe how it is possible for the devotee to attain the most intimate relationship with the Supreme. As I conduct my studies, I am constantly reminded of your superexcellent teachings.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, manifest the power and the sweetness of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava movement. The lineage from which your ISKCON emerged is called Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism because it originates in Gauḍa-deśa, the specific Bengal region in which Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared. But now, due to your powerful preaching, Gauḍa-deśa is no longer just in Bengal but has expanded throughout the world. Wherever you have established the glorification of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, surely Gauḍa-deśa is present, enabling us to be ever absorbed in the *saṅkīrtana* movement in constant remembrance of you.

The region of Bengal known as Gauḍa-deśa is a place where sugarcane grows wild. It is this abundance of sugarcane that earned the region the name Gauḍa-deśa, because *gauḍa* means, literally, "sweetness." How appropriate it is that this is the birthplace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose teachings are distinguished by their sublime sweetness of Deity, *mādhurya-bhāva.* When you brought Gauda-desa to us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought this irresistible sweetness, both in your person and teachings, and you continue to bestow this sweetness on us as witnessed many ways, especially in the sincere loving relationships between devotees. It is this sweetness which we cannot forget.

The devotee always remembers your mercy, because it is powerful, and the devotee never forgets your mercy, because it is sweet. Thus, your divine appearance continues in each moment of our service to you.

We at the Institute humbly submit ourselves to you and beg you to accept our service: researching the history of Vaiṣṇavism, developing higher-educational materials and methods for ISKCON, building the "Matsya" library of Vaiṣṇava literature and source materials, and writing, translating, and publishing books and journals of high scholarly quality.

(Written by Garuḍa dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Swami International Gurukula

**Prabhupada**

PRABHUPĀDA—this wretch falls down before thee!  
Raises up a flag to wave thee glory;  
Adamantine heart melts / or the one who  
Brought thee happiness and life eternal!  
Hear my prayer, dear lord, as I prostrate me  
Underneath the beams of thy cool foot-shade,  
Praying / or thy mercy, ever hopeful,  
Anxious for thy glance to light upon me.  
Death's quite overcome by thy sweet mercy;  
All I have is thine; please stay close by me!

**Gurudeva**

GURUDEVA—what was my life without thee?  
Useless plans and hopeless dreams devoured me;  
Righteousness, I fear, did not dwell in me.  
Urgently I sought to end my torment:  
Did you hear me, lord, when I cried for thee,  
'Ere my wicked nature did consume me?  
Verily thou didst, and gave me shelter.  
All I have is thine; please stay close by me!

## ISKCON Cinema

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

While reading Śubhānanda dāsa's book *Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna,* we were delighted by this passage by Thomas J. Hopkins, professor and chairman of the Department of Religious Studies at Franklin and Marshall College:

"Bhaktivedanta Swami's success story is an unlikely one to say the least. He was seventy years old when he arrived in America. It was his first trip outside India, he had no money and no local means of support. But he did have a revitalized and spiritually rich Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava tradition that had been imbued, by both Bhaktivinoda and Bhaktisiddhānta, with a spirit of universality and of relevance to the modern world. But it must be admitted, certainly, that not anybody could inherit and transmit that legacy. Other disciples of Bhaktisiddhānta had tried and failed to bring the devotional message of Caitanya to the Western world. And after Bhaktisiddhānta passed away, his organization, the Gauḍīya Math, was torn asunder by internal power struggles and other kinds of upheaval. Bhaktivedanta Swami, obviously, was a very special person, a spiritual leader of rare power. His success in bringing the message to the West and planting it in a place where it took root and flourished without compromising his tradition has to be seen as largely an effect of his tremendous personal spirituality and holiness, and his incredible determination.

"It's an astonishing story. If someone told you a story like this, you wouldn't believe it. Here's this person, he's seventy years old, he's going to a country where he's never been, he doesn't know anybody there, he has no money, he has no contacts. He has none of the things, you would say, that make for success. He's going to recruit people not on any systematic basis, but just picking up whomever he comes across, and he's going to give them responsibility for organizing a worldwide movement. You'd say, 'What kind of a program is that?' There are precedents, perhaps. Jesus of Nazareth went around saying, 'Come follow me. Drop your nets, or leave your tax collecting, and come with me and be my disciple.' But in his case, he wasn't an old man in a strange society dealing with people whose backgrounds were totally different from his own. He was dealing with his own community. Bhaktivedanta Swami's achievement, then, must be seen as unique." (pages 127-128)

From Professor Hopkins's objective and scholarly analysis, your achievement as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON is greater than Jesus Christ's achievement as the forefather of Christianity.

May our appreciation of you and our realization of your achievement grow, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And just as in the years following your spiritual master's disappearance your spiritual potency increasingly manifested, so now, in the years following your disappearance, may our remembrance and love for you increasingly manifest. May that remembrance and love illumine our hearts, as the full moon illumines the night.

*Your servants,*  
Yadubara dāsa / Viśākhā-devī dāsī

## ITV

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

When King Rahūgana expressed surprise upon seeing King Bharata's spiritual achievements, Bharata replied:

*rahūgaṇaitāt tapasā na yāti*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na cchandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda-rajo- 'bhiṣekam*

**"My dear Rahugana, no one can attain the perfected stage of devotional service without being favored by a great soul or a pure devotee. No one can attain the perfectional stages simply by following the regulative principles of scriptures, or by accepting the renounced order of life, or by prosecuting the prescribed duties of householder life, or by becoming a great student of spiritual science, or by accepting severe austerity and penances for realization."**

Thus Lord Caitanya told Sanātana Gosvāmī that all scriptures stress association with pure devotees of the Personality of Godhead!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that pure devotee for us. No one in all the universes has delivered anything that can compare to your unlimited mercy which constantly showers upon us through your *vapuḥ* and *vāṇī.* In describing the devotee in your *Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* you write:

"A person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness who is fully devoted to the transcendental loving service of the Lord develops all the godly qualities of the demigods... A devotee of the Lord is always kind to everyone, and he does not pick quarrels. His interest is in the essence of life, which is spiritual. He is equal to everyone, and no one can find fault in him. His magnanimous mind is always fresh and clean and devoid of material obsessions. He is a benefactor to all living entities and is peaceful and always surrendered to Kṛṣṇa. He has no material desires. He is very humble and is fixed in his purpose. He is victorious over the six material qualities such as lust and anger, and he does not eat more than he needs. He is always sane and respectful to others, but he does not require respect for himself. He is grave, merciful, friendly, poetic, expert, and silent."

The more we read your words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the more we want to replace our own hackneyed and oft-used grotesquely fabricated phrases and sentences with paragraph upon paragraph of yours. The more we read your books and letters, listen to your audio tapes, and repeatedly view your videotapes, the more we want only to speak your thoughts and observations. The more we try to unsuccessfully glorify our own meager feats of endeavor, the more we want to glorify your great character and accomplishments. When will that day come when we can immerse ourselves one hundred percent in your association through broadcasting your message everywhere—when the entire world shall rejoice in your mercy, and when you holy name shall be sung in every town and village? Like repentant sons and daughters, we pray for the day to arrive when you will be proud of us, your disciples and granddisciples. We beg to remain in your service.

*Your fallen servants,*

Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa / Siddhānta dāsa / Raṇajit dāsa / Raṇadhīra dāsa / Śacīdulal dāsa / Caitanyacandra dāsa / Ādi-puruṣa dāsa / Bhakta Phillip / Bhakta Ghee / Bhakta Tom / Bhakta Toma / Bhakta John / Mohana-devī dāsī / Pāvaka-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Cindy / Jīvānanda

## Sankirtan Movement, Inc.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

This book is filled with obeisances at your lotus feet, and we are honored to add ours. Everyone is offering obeisances to you. Everyone honors and respects you. Everyone wants to love you. All of us claim to love you. *Love*, you define as cooperation to spread this movement in your physical absence.

We may not, then, be able to claim to be great lovers. Perhaps some can. The confusion between personal motivation and cooperation abounds, however. Cooperation or love is difficult in a place where everyone thinks he's right. The standard, everyone will agree, is in your transcendental books. Your books should be read carefully by us all. We should let this knowledge go to our heads, and nothing else. The external trappings will crumble around us, but if we can cause one person to sincerely chant the holy name and become a pure devotee, we are then successful. This is the big service you want.

The proof of right is might, and the might is Kṛṣṇa and pure devotion to Him. Thus purity is the might. Purity is the right. Purity is found in your books, and thus anyone who is pure is the embodiment of the knowledge you have given. Anyone who carries the pure mace of knowledge you have presented will rise above the horizon of men to influence the world.

Cooperation; love. Those lacking personal ambition know it best. They are the true leaders, the real workers. Cooperation—a one-way street to the dictatorial, to the ambitious, to the *māyāvādī*. Love—a two-way street for you, for the humble, for the Vaiṣṇava. Only the *māyāvādī* wants to merge his personality into that of another. The humble Vaiṣṇava just wants to be with you, to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is perfect in every way. Teach what he taught, not something new. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

## Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Saṅkīrtana Pādayātrā

*Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara.

We made it! After seven thousand kilometers and nineteen months on the road, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's Saṅkīrtana Padayātrā has arrived in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur to celebrate the five-hundredth birth anniversary of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ lāṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāriṇam*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who is the deliverer of the fallen souls and by whose mercy a dumb man can speak eloquently and a lame man can cross mountains."**

Only by your causeless mercy were we able to complete the long journey on the plains, through the forests, on the mountain tops, crossing many rivers and oceans. And the results have been so enormous that it would be unwise to stop this preaching program. There is unlimited potential. You wanted to go on pilgrimage yourself during those last days, but you left before that desire was fulfilled. So for the pleasure of Their Lordships and Your Divine Grace, we are continuing.

Lord Caitanya traveled throughout South India, but also in northern India, to Vṛndāvana. So under the banner of "Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Saṅkīrtana Padayātrā," it is only appropriate we continue following His footsteps through northern India. By the time we complete our second phase, *padayātrā* will be completing ten thousand kilometers.

We also desire a ten-year *padayātrā* program, to commemorate your first centenary celebration. This might sound like "shooting for the rhinoceros," but this is what you expected out of your disciples—that we would not only continue spreading the holy name throughout India, but also through the international borders. Lord Caitanya's prediction is becoming more and more a reality. There is no limit in sight to this *padayātrā* program, and we all pray at your lotus feet for blessings so that the *padayātrā* may keep marching on.

The *padayātrā* daily introduces ten to twenty thousand conditioned souls to the glories of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, your divine *mūrti* form, your transcendental literature, and your disciples and granddisciples from around the world. This is making the Lord and Your Divine Grace (who are the empowered representative of the Lord) more familiar throughout India and the world.

The *padayātrā’s* expenditure is considerably low, yet it produces so much. Our humble endeavor has accomplished some substantial results: We often distribute your books in very remote, out-of-the-way places, and they become rare, prized possessions, to be passed down in a family for generations. *Padayātrā* has made more than a dozen full-time ISKCON devotees. We have been able to stage a big public festival every night for the past twenty-four months,.which is over seven hundred full-scale festivals. ISKCON received tons of publicity, which was all favorable: television, radio, magazines, newspapers—all broadcasting the news of ISKCON's historic *saṅkīrtana-padayātrā* on a grass-roots level. We personally come in contact with hundreds and thousands of people face to face, which is bringing Indians together for national integration. In several states the *padayātrā* itself set up *nāma-haṭṭa-saṅghas* during its visits—we preached successfully in schools and colleges; left behind monuments of permanent reminders; made thousands of friends.

You once said that all your devotees should spend some time in India to "become cultured." *Padayātrā* offers us all a way to become deep and rich in cultural understanding. It is an opportunity which inspires new faith in us and makes our lives more successful.

On this most blessed event, your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we would like to extend our special thanks to different BBT's and individuals for the generous financial support. And special thanks to the presidents and devotees of the ISKCON (India) temples who served as hosts and co-workers in each stop of our journey.

In conclusion, Śrīla Prabhupāda, may we say that it is only by your causeless mercy that you have made us instrumental in organizing and continuing this *padayātrā* program.

(Written by Lokanātha Swami)
